# HTTP API Reference

Social Home exposes HTTP APIs on two distinct surfaces:

- **HFS** (per-household server) — serves the web UI, mobile apps,
  and any third-party integrations the household admin enables.
  Everything under `/api/*` plus `/federation/inbox/{id}` (federation inbound).
- **GFS** (global federation server) — serves the public
  space-directory, RTC signalling relay, and operator admin portal.
  Everything under `/gfs/*`, `/cluster/*`, and `/admin*`.

This file lists every live endpoint. For the *why* behind the
protocol events these routes trigger, see
[protocol/](./protocol/README.md). For the high-level shape — HFS ↔
GFS topology, identity model, sync tiers — see
[architecture.md](./architecture.md).

## Authentication

| Model | Where | How |
|---|---|---|
| **Bearer token** | HFS `/api/*` | `Authorization: Bearer <token>` or, for WebSocket only, `?token=<token>`. Tokens are minted via `/api/auth/token` (standalone) or via the HA adapter. |
| **Signed envelope** | HFS `/federation/inbox/{id}`, GFS `/gfs/*` | Ed25519 signature inside the posted envelope. No separate auth header — the signature *is* the auth. |
| **Cookie session** | GFS `/admin*` | `admin_auth` middleware. Logged in via `POST /admin/login` with a bcrypt-verified password. |
| **None** | Health, VAPID key, public SSR pages, directory listings | Explicitly public. |

API tokens appear in access logs (because of the WebSocket `?token=`
fallback) and browser history. **Operators must redact tokens from
log aggregation.** Code must never log the full query string of
`/api/ws`. Both the household server and the GFS run aiohttp with
`RedactingAccessLogger` (`socialhome/access_log.py`), which writes
`token` / `api_key` / `access_token` / `password` query values, the
household dashboard's `/enter?t=` setup token and the `sig` of a signed
media / map-tile / app-bundle URL (a bearer credential for its TTL; `exp`
stays readable) — and the bearer token segment of the GFS's
`/join/{token}`, `/highlight/{instance_id}/{highlight_id}/{token}`,
`/gfs/highlight_tokens/{token}/revoke` and
`/gfs/spaces/{space_id}/invite/{gfs_token}` paths and of the household's
`/api/invite-links/{token}/code`, `/api/remote_invites/{token}/{decision}`,
`/api/spaces/{id}/invite-tokens/{token}` and
`/api/highlights/{id}/publish/{token}` paths — as `***`, in the request
line and the `Referer` alike. A reverse proxy in front keeps its own log
and needs the same treatment.

## Conventions

- Content type is `application/json` unless otherwise stated.
  Multipart is used for avatar / cover / media uploads.
- Errors use one shape, `{"error": {"code": "...", "detail": "...",
  "params": {…}}}` — see [Errors](#errors). HTTP status codes are
  standard: 200 / 201 / 204 for success, 400 for validation, 401 for
  missing auth, 403 for authorisation failures, 404 for missing
  resources, 409 for conflicts, 422 for refused input, 429 for rate
  limits.
- Pagination uses `?limit=N&cursor=…`. Cursors are opaque; don't
  parse them.
- Timestamps are ISO-8601 UTC, serialised via orjson.
- **Space feature access levels (§4.3).** Every write to a space's posts,
  pages, tasks / task lists, stickies or calendar events (including a
  poll / schedule-poll attach, a Bazaar listing edit and a bot's post)
  passes the space's `*_access` level for the acting user. Under
  `admin_only` only the owner and admins write; anyone else gets
  `403 {"error": {"code": "ACCESS_ADMIN_ONLY", "feature": "posts" | "pages"
  | "tasks" | "stickies" | "calendar", …}}` and nothing changes. Comments,
  reactions, votes, schedule answers, bids and RSVPs are never gated. See
  [`protocol/spaces.md`](./protocol/spaces.md#feature-access-levels-v_42).

## Errors

Every error answer is `{"error": {"code", "detail", …}}`, built centrally by
`BaseView._iter` (`socialhome/routes/base.py`):

- `code` — a stable, machine-readable code. Branch on this, never on `detail`.
- `detail` — a short English sentence for API clients and logs. It never
  carries raw ids, library error text or other internals.
- `params` — optional, flat JSON values the message needs (a minimum age, a
  length limit, a price floor). Present only when the code has some.
- A few older codes carry their hints as top-level fields instead
  (`feature`, `reason`, `section`, `count`, `current_version`…).

The SPA shows its own translated text for a known `code`
(`client/src/apiErrors.ts`, keys `error.*`); an unknown code shows `detail`,
and a generic answer (`NOT_FOUND`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`, the
blanket `422 UNPROCESSABLE`, any 5xx, or no body) shows a per-status line.

Coded refusals (`socialhome/domain/errors.py` `CodedError` subclasses):

| Code | Status | `params` | Raised by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ALREADY_MEMBER` | 422 | — | `POST /api/spaces/{id}/join-requests` by a member |
| `USER_ALREADY_MEMBER` | 403 | — | `POST /api/spaces/{id}/members` for someone already in |
| `BANNED` | 403 | — | Joining, following or accepting an invite to a space you're banned from |
| `USER_BANNED` | 403 | — | An admin adding / inviting a banned person (no user id in the answer) |
| `INVITE_ONLY` | 403 | — | A join request to an invite-only space |
| `INVITE_EXPIRED` | 404 | — | `POST /api/remote_invites/{token}/accept` / `decline`, `POST /api/spaces/join` with an unknown, expired or used-up token |
| `SUBSCRIBE_NOT_ALLOWED` | 403 | — | `POST /api/spaces/{id}/subscribe` on a space that takes no followers |
| `HOME_NOT_SET` | 403 | `whose` (`own`) | A join to a [local community](./protocol/local-communities.md) when this household has no home location set — the host's own people joining one of its communities, an admin's direct add / local invite, or a person who agreed to send the location (`share_location: true`) on a household with none |
| `LOCATION_REQUIRED` | 403 | `whose` (`host`) | A join to another household's local community that carried no approximate location: `POST /api/spaces/join` (invite link) and `POST /api/remote_invites/{token}/accept`. Nothing was decided or counted — the SPA asks the person ("your approximate location (about 1 km) is sent to *host*") and sends the same call again with `share_location: true` |
| `OUTSIDE_AREA` | 403 | `whose` (`own` / `host`) | A join whose ~1 km cell lies wholly outside the local community's area (`own` — this household's home; `host` — ours, as the space's host answered a redeem) |
| `JOIN_REQUEST_SENT` | 403 | `request_id` (and `space_id` over federation) | A follow, a local invite accept or an invite link into a local community when the home's ~1 km cell straddles the rim: no seat — a pending join request for the space's admins was filed instead (worded like any request: "Your request was sent to the admins"). Asking again while it waits answers the same request |
| `LOCAL_AREA_RATE_LIMITED` | 429 | `whose` (`own` / `host`) | Spam control on the paths a person starts — open join, join request, follow, invite accept, invite-link redeem — past 10 joins of that person into that community today (UTC), successful ones and edge reviews included, or (a remote person) 20 of their household's: no verdict until 00:00 UTC. Per person; an admin's invite, direct add or approval is never counted |
| `LOCAL_AREA_PUBLIC_ONLY` | 422 | — | An area (`lat` / `lon` / `radius_km`) on a space that isn't public — `POST /api/spaces`, `PATCH /api/spaces/{id}` |
| `LOCAL_AREA_UNAVAILABLE` | 409 | — | `PATCH /api/spaces/{id}` area keys on a space hosted by another household |
| `LOCAL_AREA_DELEGATED` | 409 | — | An area on a space with delegated admin authority on, or turning that on for a local community |
| `PLACE_PIN_GLOBAL_ONLY` | 422 | — | A place pin (`place: {lat, lon}`) on a space that isn't global — `POST /api/spaces`, `PATCH /api/spaces/{id}` |
| `SUBSCRIBER_READ_ONLY` | 403 | `action` (`post` / `comment` / `react` …) | A follower writing to a space |
| `SPACE_ARCHIVED` | 403 | — | Any write to an archived space (posts, pages, tasks, stickies, calendar) |
| `INVALID_LINK` | 422 | — | `POST` / `PATCH /api/spaces/{id}/links…` with an empty label or a URL that isn't an absolute http(s) address (the detail names the rule, never the value) |
| `SPACE_NOT_PUBLIC` | 409 | — | Listing a private or household space on a GFS (`POST /api/spaces/{id}/publish/{gfs_id}`, `PUT /api/spaces/{id}/gfs-servers`) — only global spaces are listed; a private space takes channel servers only while its owner's `private_gfs` is on |
| `TIER_CHANGE_REQUIRED` | 409 | — | Listing a **public** space on a GFS (same routes): public is visible only to connected households, and listing it on a GFS is making it global — a tier change |
| `GFS_SELECTION_REQUIRED` | 422 | — | `POST /api/spaces` for a global space with no `gfs_ids` while a GFS is connected |
| `GFS_SELECTION_INVALID` | 422 | `reason` (`unknown` / `too_many` / `not_global` / `malformed` / `unsupported`), `max` (with `too_many`) | A GFS choice that can't be used: `POST /api/spaces`, the `set_public_tier` proposal, `PUT /api/spaces/{id}/gfs-servers`, `POST /api/spaces/{id}/publish/{gfs_id}` |
| `NOT_PAIRED` | 403 | — | `POST /api/public_spaces/{space_id}/join-request` when the host household isn't a confirmed connection |
| `AGE_RESTRICTED` | 403 | `min_age` | §CP.F1 age gate on joining a space |
| `DM_SELF` | 422 | — | `POST /api/conversations/dm` to yourself |
| `GROUP_TOO_SMALL` | 422 | `min` | `POST /api/conversations/group` with fewer than 3 people |
| `APP_CHALLENGE_HANDLED` | 409 | — | Declining a game invite that no longer waits (taken in by the app, declined, withdrawn or expired) |
| `DM_TOO_LONG` | 422 | `max` | Sending / editing a message over the length cap |
| `INVALID_MEDIA_URL` | 422 | `field` (`media_url` / `image_urls` / `cover_url` / `cover_image_url`) | A media field that isn't a local upload (`api/media/<name>`, the `url` `POST /api/media/upload` returns): `media_url` on `POST /api/conversations/{id}/messages`, `POST /api/highlights/frames`, `POST /api/moments`, feed / space posts and comments; `image_urls` on feed / space posts and `POST /api/bazaar`; `cover_url` on calendar events; `cover_image_url` on pages. A third-party URL would leak every viewer's IP and every receiving household drops it (see [principles](principles.md#no-third-party-fetches-from-user-content)). The detail names the field and the rule, never the value |
| `DM_BLOCKED` | 403 | — | The recipient blocked you (a guardian block reads the same) |
| `DM_YOU_BLOCKED` | 403 | — | You blocked the recipient |
| `DM_NOT_ALLOWED` | 403 | — | A protected account messaging someone its guardian blocked |
| `DM_GROUP_NOT_ALLOWED` | 403 | — | A group that a guardian block rules out |
| `GROUP_MEMBER_UNSUPPORTED` | 422 | `reason` (`legacy_group` / `not_paired` / `too_old`), `name` | Adding a remote person to a group |
| `RSVP_PAST` | 422 | — | `POST /api/calendars/events/{id}/rsvp` for an occurrence that has ended |
| `POLL_CLOSED` | 409 | — | Voting on a closed poll, or one past its `closes_at` |
| `HIGHLIGHT_AUDIENCE_MISMATCH` | 409 | `audience_kind`, `audience_count` (today's highlight) | `POST /api/highlights/frames` naming another audience than today's highlight — a day's frames share one audience, so the frame is refused rather than shown to today's people (change it for every frame with `PATCH /api/highlights/{id}/audience`) |
| `HIGHLIGHT_NOT_TODAY` | 409 | — | `PATCH /api/highlights/{id}/audience` on an earlier day's highlight — only today's highlight changes who sees it |
| `HIGHLIGHT_AUDIENCE_EMPTY` | 422 | — | `PATCH /api/highlights/{id}/audience` with `audience_kind: users` and nobody picked |
| `HIGHLIGHT_AUDIENCE_PEER_TOO_OLD` | 409 | — | `PATCH /api/highlights/{id}/audience` that takes people away from a paired household below v_70 (it drops out, or keeps fewer of its people) — it can't keep the removal yet; nothing is changed or sent. Widening is never refused |
| `MOMENT_RATE_LIMIT` | 429 | `next_allowed_at` | Posting more than one top-level moment per 15 minutes. `next_allowed_at` (UTC ISO 8601) is when the next one can go — the same value `GET /api/moments/limits` gives before writing |
| `BID_TOO_LOW` | 422 | `floor_amount` (the listing's stored units — cents, or whole units for JPY / KRW / ISK), `currency` | `POST /api/bazaar/{id}/bids` under the floor |
| `OWN_LISTING` | 422 (bids) / 403 (offers) | — | A seller bidding on / offering for their own listing |
| `LISTING_NOT_ACTIVE` | 422 (bids) / 409 (offers) | — | A bid or offer on a sold, expired or cancelled listing |
| `LISTING_THREAD_CLOSED` | 409 | — | `POST /api/bazaar/{id}/ask`, or a message in an "Ask the seller" thread, more than 14 days after the listing was sold, expired or withdrawn |
| `SELLER_HOUSEHOLD_TOO_OLD` | 409 | — | `POST /api/bazaar/{id}/ask` when the seller's household is below v_74 — nothing is opened or sent |
| `BAZAAR_FAQ_TEXT` | 422 | `question_max`, `answer_max` | `POST` / `PATCH /api/bazaar/{id}/faq…` with an empty, too long or non-text question (one line, ≤ 200) or answer (≤ 500) |
| `BAZAAR_FAQ_FULL` | 409 | `max` | `POST /api/bazaar/{id}/faq` when the listing already shows 10 questions |
| `BAZAAR_FAQ_USED_UP` | 409 | `max` | `POST /api/bazaar/{id}/faq` when the listing already had 30 questions, removed ones included (each removal is kept for the life of the listing); the live ones can still be edited |
| `BAZAAR_FAQ_RATE_LIMITED` | 429 | — | More than 30 changes to one listing's questions and answers in an hour |
| `LISTING_THREAD_TEXT_ONLY` | 422 | — | A media, location or highlight-reply message in an "Ask the seller" thread |
| `LISTING_THREAD_NO_CHANGES` | 422 | — | An edit, delete or reaction in an "Ask the seller" thread (v1) |
| `LISTING_THREAD_RATE_LIMITED` | 429 | — | A message in an "Ask the seller" thread over the per-person cap: 30 an hour, 100 a day in one thread |
| `IMAGE_TOO_LARGE` | 422 | `max_mb` | `POST /api/me/picture`, `/api/spaces/{id}/members/me/picture`, `/cover`, `/icon` over the size limit |
| `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` | 413 | `max_mb` | `POST /api/gallery/albums/{id}/items`, `POST /api/backup/import` over the size limit — refused while streaming, so a chunked body (no `Content-Length`) is bounded too. `POST /api/media/upload` answers the same code without params |
| `IMAGE_UNREADABLE` | 422 | — | The same picture endpoints and `POST /api/media/upload`, when the file isn't a supported image or the image library can't open it (the library's error text and the file name stay in the server log) |
| `INVALID_REACH` | 422 | — | `POST /api/pairing/initiate` / `accept` with an unknown pairing `reach` |
| `GFS_NOT_CONNECTED` | 422 | — | `POST /api/pairing/initiate` with `reach` `url_gfs` / `gfs` and no active GFS connection that relays envelopes |
| `GFS_NOT_SHARED` | 422 | — | `POST /api/pairing/accept` of a code whose GFS this household is not connected to, when the pairing has to travel through it |
| `KEYWRAP_INVALID` | 422 | — | `POST /api/pairing/accept` of a GFS-reach code whose key-wrap key is missing, unlabelled or not bound to its identity |
| `FEDERATION_BASE_MANAGED_BY_PLATFORM` | 422 | — | `PUT /api/admin/federation/external-url` where the platform owns the address (the Home Assistant App) |

## HFS — Authentication & self

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/auth/token` | Issue a bearer token (standalone mode). |
| POST | `/api/auth/redeem-password-reset` | Public: redeem a one-time admin-issued reset token to set a new password. Body: `{token, new_password}`. 204 on success; 410 when expired / already used; 422 for short password / missing fields. Same per-IP rate limit as `/api/auth/token` (5 / 15 min). |
| POST | `/api/admin/users/{username}/issue-password-reset` | Admin: mint a single-use, 1h-TTL reset token for a user. Returns `{token, expires_at, username}` once — admin hands the resulting `/reset-password?token=…` URL to the user out-of-band. Standalone mode has no SMTP, so this is the only recovery path. |
| GET | `/api/admin/auth-audit` | Admin: read the auth audit log — append-only trail of login attempts (success + failure), reset issues, reset redeems, and Home Assistant unlocks of a wall panel's privacy lock (`display_ha_unlock`). Query `?limit=N` (default 100, max 500). |
| GET | `/api/me` | Current user profile. Also carries the caller's **own** protection state — `protected` (bool) and `restrictions` (the capability ids the server refuses, see *Protected accounts* below; `[]` when unprotected). Never `is_minor` / `declared_age`, and no other user payload (`/api/users`, members, profiles) carries `protected`. |
| GET | `/api/me/protection` | The caller's own protection: `{protected, restrictions, guardians: [{user_id, username, display_name}]}` — what is limited and who to ask. `{protected: false, restrictions: [], guardians: []}` for an unprotected account. |
| PATCH | `/api/me` | Update profile fields. Body: any of `{"display_name", "bio", "preferences", "tz"}`, or the status keys `{"status_emoji", "status_text", "status_clear_after"}`. Status: `status_text` is one line of at most 80 characters, `status_emoji` a single emoji (at most 16 code points, no whitespace, not plain ASCII); both blank or `null` clears the status. `status_clear_after` is `null` (keep until changed), `"30m"` / `"1h"` / `"4h"`, `"today"` (midnight in the user's `tz`), or an ISO-8601 instant with an offset that is in the future and at most 7 days away; the response's `status.expires_at` is the resolved UTC deadline. A status key that is absent keeps its current value, so `{"status_clear_after": "1h"}` alone re-times the current status; setting emoji/text without `status_clear_after` means no expiry. Invalid input → 422, nothing saved. A change fires `user.status_changed` to the household and `USER_STATUS_UPDATED` to paired households; a once-a-minute sweep clears a status at its deadline (same fan-out), and reads already treat an expired status as unset. `tz` is validated against the IANA database (unknown name → 422); the SPA's cold-start probe sends it once on first login so personal calendar events default to the user's local wall clock. `preferences` is shallow-merged into the free-form preferences blob (`null` removes a key); one key is the server's: `ha_automation_events` (boolean, default off — anything else → 422) sends the person's notifications and their own activity to Home Assistant automations: `socialhome.notification_new` `{user_id, username, notification_id, type, link_url}` and the other personal `socialhome.*` events (DMs they get, their posts, tasks, calendar events, listings, status and presence — the full list and payloads in `docs/architecture.md`, "Home Assistant automation events"), each with ids, a kind and a link only, never names, titles or text (`ha` / `haos`, `automation_events` capability). A person who doesn't opt in is on none of them. Turning it on is `403 ACCOUNT_PROTECTED` / `ha_automations` for a protected account. |
| POST | `/api/me/username` | Rename the caller's login username. Body: `{"username"}`. The cryptographic `user_id` is unaffected. Invalid / reserved / taken → 422; HA-managed (HA-source) accounts → 403. |
| POST | `/api/me/handle` | Set the caller's public `@handle`. Body: `{"handle"}`. Returns `{handle}` on success. Per-household case-insensitive uniqueness enforced. Invalid / reserved / taken → 422. Unlike `/api/me/username`, **all** users (including HA-managed) may set their own handle. |
| GET | `/api/me/picture` | Download current user's avatar. |
| POST | `/api/me/picture` | Upload avatar (multipart). |
| DELETE | `/api/me/picture` | Remove avatar. |
| POST | `/api/me/picture/refresh-from-ha` | HA-mode only: re-fetch from HA user profile. |
| GET / PATCH | `/api/me/notification-settings` | Settings → Notifications (D4). `GET` → `{kinds, changed}`. `kinds`: the eight kinds in Settings order — `messages` (Messages and calls), `replies` (Replies and mentions), `needs_you`, `plans` (Plans and tasks), `about_you`, `games` ("Your turn" in a game), `reactions` (Reactions and follows), `activity` (Activity in your spaces) — each `{kind, choice, default, choices}` where a choice is `push` (a row and a push), `in_app` (a row, no push) or `off` (nothing). Defaults: `games`, `reactions` and `activity` `in_app`, the rest `push`. `needs_you` offers only `push` / `in_app`, `messages` only `push` / `off` (chat messages never make a row). `changed` ("Muted and changed"): every space, chat and post the caller set off its default or muted, spaces then chats then posts, each `{target, id, level, default_level, place, name, muted_until, then_level, space_id, space_name, snippet}` — `target` `space` / `chat` / `post`; `id` the space id, conversation id or post `object_key`; `level` what applies now (`all` / `mentions` / `muted`); `muted_until` while muted (`9999-…` = until turned back on) and `then_level` the level that comes back; `place` the space type, the chat kind (`dm` / `group` / `household` / `space`) or `post` / `space_post`; `name` the space or chat name (`null` for the household chat and an unnamed one); `snippet` a muted post's first 80 characters (only for a post the caller can still see). `PATCH` body `{kind, choice}` sets one kind → `{kinds}`; a kind or choice that doesn't exist (e.g. `needs_you` `off`) → 422. Stored in `users.preferences_json["notif_kinds"]`. Every row honours it: `off` → no row, `in_app` → a row without a push. Local only. |
| POST | `/api/me/notification-settings/muted-posts` | Mute a post (D10, a row's "…" menu). Body `{object_key}` — `post:<id>` or `space_post:<id>` (anything else → 422; a post that doesn't exist or sits in a space the caller isn't in → 404). → `{object_key, muted: true}`. No row about that post reaches the caller (a mention in one of its comments still does); activity on it keeps the mute alive and it lapses after 90 days without any. At most 200 (the quietest go first). Stored in `users.preferences_json["notif_muted_posts"]`. |
| DELETE | `/api/me/notification-settings/muted-posts/{object_key}` | Unmute a post (URL-encoded key). Always `{object_key, muted: false}`. |
| GET | `/api/me/notify-targets` | Selectable push notify targets for the notification-settings dropdown. HA mode lists the household's `notify.*` entities (`[{entity_id, name}]`); other platforms return `[]`. |
| GET | `/api/me/export` | Initiate a data-export job. |
| GET | `/api/me/corner` | "My Corner" aggregated feed — one payload for the home screen (`/`) and `/corner`: `unread_notifications`, `unread_conversations`, `upcoming_events` (from now, next 7 days, ≤ 8), `presence`, `tasks_due_today`, `bazaar`, `followed_space_ids`, `followed_spaces_feed`, plus `today_timetable` — `[{timetable_id, name, color, tz, date, lessons: [...]}]`, the caller's assigned timetables in effect today, then the space timetables they pinned (`preferences.timetable_home_pins`, while still a member and the space feature is on) (today in each timetable's tz; ≤ 3, ≤ 24 lessons each; cancelled lessons included, untitled template slots left out — a timetable with no filled lesson today is skipped; `[]` when `feat_timetable` is off). A lesson is the `/api/timetables/day` effective-lesson shape plus `start_at` / `end_at` (UTC ISO 8601) — and `today_events`, the caller's events overlapping today's household-tz day (≤ 20, same shape and visibility as `upcoming_events`, including events that already ended; an all-day event only on the dates it covers in its own tz, end exclusive). Each slice fails soft to empty. `presence` leaves out the same people `GET /api/presence` does: those the caller blocked, and everyone a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from the caller, either way round. Each `presence` row is `{username, user_id, display_name, state, picture_url, zone_name, latitude, longitude, gps_accuracy_m, status}`, where `status` is the person's emoji + text status `{emoji, text, expires_at}` — same shape and rule as `GET /api/presence`: `null` when unset or already past `expires_at`. |
| GET / POST / DELETE | `/api/me/tokens[/{id}]` | Manage the caller's own API tokens (browser sign-ins appear here too, labelled `web`). `GET` returns `{base_url, tokens: [{token_id, label, created_at, last_used_at, expires_at, revoked_at}]}` (revoked rows omitted); `base_url` is the origin an external client reaches this Social Home at — derived from `PlatformAdapter.get_public_base_url()` (the admin-set external URL or `[standalone].external_url`), `null` when the only public route is Home Assistant's inbox forwarder or nothing is configured. `POST {label, expires_at?}` returns `201 {token_id, token}` (403 `ACCOUNT_PROTECTED` / `api_tokens` for a protected account; listing and revoking stay open) — the raw token appears only in this response (only its SHA-256 is stored). `DELETE` returns `204` and only revokes a token the caller owns; another user's id is a silent no-op (admins use `/api/admin/tokens/{id}`). |

Admins also have:

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET / DELETE | `/api/admin/tokens[/{id}]` | List / revoke any user's tokens. |
| GET | `/api/admin/ha-users` | HA-mode: list HA users for provisioning. |
| POST | `/api/admin/ha-users/{username}/provision` | Create a Social Home user from an HA user. |

## HFS — Users

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/users` | List all users on this HFS. Someone a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from the caller, either way round, shows by name only: `status` reads as unset and `picture_hash` / `picture_url` / `bio` / `last_seen_at` as `null` (the directory itself keeps everyone). `preferences_json` is on the caller's own row only (`GET /api/me` carries it too). |
| GET | `/api/users/{user_id}` | Fetch a user profile. |
| PATCH | `/api/users/{user_id}` | Admin-only update (or self). |
| GET | `/api/users/{user_id}/picture` | Fetch another user's avatar. 404 `NOT_FOUND` for a caller a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from that user, either way — the same answer as no picture. |
| GET | `/api/users/{user_id}/export` | Admin-only export of another user's data. |

### Personal user aliases (§4.1.6)

Viewer-private renames of other users (local or remote). Aliases never federate — only the requesting user sees them. Resolution priority `space_display_name > personal_alias > display_name` is applied server-side wherever a user reference is rendered (currently the space-members endpoint; other endpoints follow incrementally).

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/aliases/users` | List the viewer's personal aliases. |
| PUT | `/api/aliases/users/{user_id}` | Set or update the viewer's alias for a target user. |
| DELETE | `/api/aliases/users/{user_id}` | Clear the viewer's alias for a target user. |

### Personal user blocks (§Privacy)

Voluntary adult-to-adult block list. Distinct from the parent-driven CP block (`/api/cp/minors/{minor_id}/blocks`). The block stays local to the viewer's household — it is never sent anywhere, so every rule below is enforced here, on receipt for a remote person.

- **Hidden from the blocker:** the blocked person's household-feed posts (`GET /api/feed` and the live `post.created` frame), highlights, moments, presence / status / online state (`GET /api/presence`, the `/api/me/corner` presence slice and the live `presence.updated` / `user.status_changed` / `user.online|idle|offline` frames), their friends-list entry and person sheet, and every notification about something they did — posts, comments, @mentions, DMs, follows, moment replies and reactions, task assignments and completions, calendar events, bazaar bids and offers, app challenges, join requests, new members and invite answers. The same holds across a guardian block (§CP.F2). Notices nobody caused (a deadline, an expiry, a moderation queue item, a decision on your own request or content) are not withheld.
- **Both ways, local or remote:** neither can open or continue a 1:1 DM (an inbound `DM_MESSAGE`, `DM_HISTORY_CHUNK` row or new reaction from a remote person the local party blocked is refused, nothing stored), call the other 1:1 (an inbound 1:1 `CALL_OFFER` is refused too), or open / play an app session with the other. A 1:1 with them drops out of the blocker's conversation list.
- **Not hidden:** a block doesn't hide what the blocker shares — the blocked person still sees the blocker's posts, highlights, moments and presence (their own household can't know about the block). Group DMs, group calls, spaces (posts, comments, chat, map) and household-feed comments ignore personal blocks.

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/blocks` | List the caller's blocks, newest first: `[{user_id, blocked_at, display_name, personal_alias, picture_url, household}]`. `display_name` is the person's own name and `personal_alias` the caller's nickname for them; `picture_url` is signed; `household` is the own household's name for a local user, the paired household's shown name (admin alias wins) for a remote one, `null` once that household is no longer paired. An id nobody knows any more (its household was unpaired) has `display_name: null` — clients show "Unknown person", never the id. |
| POST | `/api/blocks` | Add a block. Body: `{user_id}`. Self-block → 422. |
| DELETE | `/api/blocks/{user_id}` | Remove a block. Idempotent. |

### Momentum (§Momentum)

Household-broadcast posts that fan to a 3-hop peer mesh. Replies are themselves moments, linked via `parent_moment_id`. Rate-limited at one top-level moment per author per 15 minutes; replies and reactions are exempt. Default visibility: 24h; 7d for moments authored by anyone the viewer follows.

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/moments` | List visible moments (block-aware, follow-aware). A protected (child) account never sees a moment that arrived through a GFS (`received_via: "gfs"` — a stranger's public moment another household member follows): not here, in `/archive`, `/hashtags`, a moment's replies, or `/{id}` (the unknown-id 404), and its `moment.created` frame skips them (§CP.R). Each moment carries `author`: `author` is `{display_name, personal_alias, picture_url, household}` — the name this household holds for the author (a paired household's member included), the caller's nickname for them, a signed picture and the household's name; `display_name` is `null`, never the id, for someone nobody here knows, and `picture_url` is `null` across a guardian block (§CP.F2). An author followed only through a GFS (`received_via: "gfs"`) is named from the public follow instead: their signed public name, their GFS-mirrored picture through `api/gfs/{gfs_id}/moments/users/{user_id}/picture` (signed) and an extra `bio` key; `household` is `null`. Added on read only — the federation payloads are unchanged. Every moment (here, in `/archive`, and on `/{id}` with its replies) also carries `reactions_reach` (v_67): `author` when the caller's reaction reaches the author's household — it is this one, a paired one, or further away along the moment's return tokens — and `household` when it is seen in this household only (no route: an older household on the path, a moment from a GFS that can't take reactions). A moment that came through a GFS says `author` (v_68) when that GFS proves `moment_reactions` and the author's household named its key-wrap key in a signed moment. Each also carries (v_69) `reaction_counts` (`{emoji: n}`), `my_reaction` (the caller's own emoji or `null`) and `reaction_totals_at`: for a moment from another household whose reactions reach the author, once its household sent totals, `reaction_count` / `reaction_counts` are those totals (everyone's reactions, never who) — plus the caller's reaction when made after them — and `reaction_totals_at` is when they were sent (Unix ms); otherwise the reactions held here and `null`. Every item also carries `hop_count` (1 = written here or on a paired household, 2–3 = relayed that many hops). **Query:** `?before=<created_at of the last row>` and `?limit=N` (default 50, ≤ 100) page the list newest first; `?tag=<name>` keeps one hashtag (matched like `/archive?tag=`); **`?top_level=1`** leaves replies out — each thread is listed once, under its root, so replies never use up the page — and adds `latest_reply` to each root: the newest reply the caller may read (the same rule as the replies on `/{id}`; same item shape, without counts) or `null`. Without `top_level` the list holds replies too and has no `latest_reply` (older clients). |
| POST | `/api/moments` | Create a moment. Body: `{content, media_url?, media_type?, duration_ms?, parent_moment_id?, is_public?}`. `media_url`, when present, must be a local upload (`api/media/<name>`) — anything else is `422 INVALID_MEDIA_URL`. `is_public` publishes the moment through the author's GFS registrations; on a reply it holds only when the thread's root moment is public — a reply to a moment that isn't public is stored as not public whatever the body says (the response's `is_public` shows what was stored). |
| GET | `/api/moments/archive` | Full retention-window list. Optional `?tag=<name>` filters to moments tagged with that hashtag (any script — letters, marks, numbers, `_`; matched NFC + lowercased, a leading `#` is ignored). Each moment carries `author`, as on `GET /api/moments`. **`?scope=own`** returns the caller's own archive instead (decision 6a): every moment they wrote on this household and haven't deleted — top-level moments and replies, live or past their expiry (kept here for their author only) — newest first, paged with `?before=<created_at of the last row>` and `?limit=N` (default 50, ≤ 100); same per-item shape (`author`, `reaction_count`, `reply_count`). **`?scope=saved`** returns the moments the caller saved and may still read (decision 6b) — live or past their expiry, block / protection / `max_hops` filters applied — newest save first, paged with `?before=<saved_at of the last row>` and `?limit=N`; each item is the inbox item plus `saved_at`. Any other `scope` is `422 INVALID_SCOPE`. Every moment item (inbox, archive, detail, replies) carries `saved_by_me` and `can_save` (Save is offered only where the author's delete can still reach the copy). |
| GET | `/api/moments/archive/usage` | `{used_bytes, cap_bytes}` — how much media the caller's own moments and the moments they saved hold here (file + a video's poster) and the household's per-person archive cap (`0` = no limit). Past the cap the hourly retention tick drops the oldest archived pictures first (the text stays). |
| GET | `/api/moments/limits` | `{next_allowed_at}` — when the caller may post their next top-level moment (UTC ISO 8601, 15 minutes after their last one), or `null` when they may post now. Replies and reactions are exempt and don't move it. The composer reads it before writing (decision 13) so it can say "You can post your next moment at 13:24"; the `429 MOMENT_RATE_LIMIT` carries the same field. |
| GET | `/api/moments/hashtags` | Trending hashtags inside the viewer's visibility window; a count is the number of top-level moments the tag view lists (replies are not counted). Returns `{"hashtags": [{"tag", "count"}, …]}`; `?limit=N` (default 20, capped at 50). |
| GET | `/api/moments/{id}` | Detail incl. replies + reactions. The moment and every reply carry `author`, as on `GET /api/moments`. **Audience gate:** the caller must be able to read the moment — its author (while it is live; a moment written here also after its expiry, from the author's archive — a reply to it is then `422`), or anyone `GET /api/moments` shows it to (no personal or guardian block between them, younger than 24 h or the caller follows the author — directly or through a GFS — not expired, within the caller's `max_hops`; one shared SQL rule). **Replies** follow the same rule per reply, without the 24-hour window (a reply stays while its thread is readable): the list holds only the replies the caller may read (their own, or ones not hidden by a personal or guardian block, child protection, `max_hops` or expiry), and `reply_count` — here and on `GET /api/moments` / `/archive` — counts only those, so a blocked or guardian-blocked person's reply leaves no trace. Otherwise `404 NOT_FOUND` `moment not found`, byte for byte the same as for an unknown id. The same gate guards `PUT`/`DELETE …/reaction`, a reply (`POST /api/moments` with `parent_moment_id`) and `DELETE` (an admin may still delete any moment held here; a reader who is not the author gets `403`). |
| DELETE | `/api/moments/{id}` | Author or admin delete. |
| PUT | `/api/moments/{id}/reaction` | Set / change reaction. Body: `{emoji}` — exactly one emoji of ≤ 32 bytes, else `422 INVALID_REACTION`. Reaches the author's household when the moment says `reactions_reach: "author"` (v_67: routed back hop by hop to an author further away; v_68: a moment that came through a GFS goes back through that GFS, sealed to the author's household — both after a 3 s debounce); otherwise it stays here. |
| DELETE | `/api/moments/{id}/reaction` | Clear own reaction. |
| PUT | `/api/moments/{id}/save` | Save someone's moment (decision 6b): it stays with the caller past its expiry until its author deletes it (or the caller unfollows them / the household unpairs its origin). Idempotent; `{moment_id, saved: true}`. The unknown-id `404` for a moment the caller can't read; `422 OWN_MOMENT` for their own (already archived); `422 SAVE_UNAVAILABLE` unless a delete can still reach the copy — a housemate's moment, a paired v_66+ origin household, or a GFS that proved `moment_delete_log` (`can_save` on the item says so up front). |
| DELETE | `/api/moments/{id}/save` | Remove it from the caller's saved moments. Always `200 {moment_id, saved: false}` — no read needed, says nothing about the moment. |
| POST | `/api/moments/{id}/report` | Report a moment. Body: `{category, notes?}`. |
| GET | `/api/moments/follows` | List who I follow: `[{user_id, created_at, display_name, personal_alias, picture_url, household}]` — same person fields as `GET /api/blocks`; `picture_url` is `null` for someone a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from the caller, either way. |
| POST | `/api/moments/follows` | Follow a user. Body: `{user_id}`. The household follow: stored here only, never sent anywhere. The SPA's person sheet uses it for its own people, paired households' people and relayed authors — never for someone known only through a GFS (decision 7, see `/api/moments/public/follows`). |
| DELETE | `/api/moments/follows/{user_id}` | Unfollow. |
| POST | `/api/highlights/{id}/report` | Report a highlight (same `content_reports` queue). |

## HFS — Household feed

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/feed` | Newest-first list of household posts. Each entry carries the post fields (`id`, `author`, `type`, `content`, …) plus a `latest_comment` field — the most recent non-deleted comment on the post (or `null` when there are none). Used by the SPA to render an inline preview line under each card without an N+1 fetch. |
| GET | `/api/feed/posts` | Paginated post list. |
| POST | `/api/feed/posts` | Create a household post. Body: `{type, content?, media_url?, location?, pinned?, no_link_preview?}`. `type` ∈ `text\|image\|video\|file\|poll\|schedule\|location`. When `type='location'` the body **must** include `location: {lat, lon, label?}` — server truncates `lat`/`lon` to 4 decimal places (~11 m) at the boundary, `label` is optional and capped at 80 characters. `bazaar` posts are space-scoped; `event` posts are auto-created by the calendar bridge. A `text` post whose content contains a web link gets a server-built `link_preview` (see *Link previews* below) unless `no_link_preview: true`; the client can only opt out — any preview fields in the body are ignored. |
| POST | `/api/link-preview` | The composer's live link card. Body `{url}` → `{"preview": {url, title, description, site_name, thumbnail_url} \| null}`. Built by the **author's** household through the SSRF-guarded fetcher (see *Link previews*), cached ~15 min per URL — creating the post reuses the result. `thumbnail_url` is a signed local `api/media/…` URL of the page image re-encoded to WebP without metadata. Any member; 403 `FEATURE_DISABLED` when the household admin turned previews off (`allow_link_preview`); 422 on a missing `url`; `{"preview": null}` for a link that yields no card, cannot be fetched, or when the member's fetch budget is spent. |
| GET / PATCH / DELETE | `/api/feed/posts/{id}` | Read / edit / delete one post. `GET` is the post's permalink (the `/post/{id}` page a notification opens): the feed's shape, **404** once deleted or when the caller blocked the author. |
| GET / POST / DELETE | `/api/feed/posts/{id}/reactions[/{emoji}]` | List reactions; add / remove own. |
| GET / POST | `/api/feed/posts/{id}/comments` | List / add comments. |
| PATCH / DELETE | `/api/feed/posts/{id}/comments/{cid}` | Edit / delete own comment. |
| POST | `/api/feed/posts/{id}/save` | Bookmark. |
| GET | `/api/feed/saved` | List bookmarks. |
| GET | `/api/me/feed/read` | Caller's scroll-restoration watermark. Returns `{last_read_post_id, last_read_at}`. |
| POST | `/api/me/feed/read` | Mark a post read. Body: `{"post_id": "..."}` (or `null` to clear). 404 on unknown post id. |
| GET | `/api/me/subscriptions` | Caller's subscribed spaces — `{subscriptions: [{space_id, subscribed_at}, ...]}`, newest first. A subscription = a read-only member row (`role='subscriber'` in `space_members`); the caller receives the same content-delivery stream as real members but is blocked on post / comment / reaction writes. Distinct from the dashboard "Spaces you follow" widget, which pins spaces the user is already a full member of — see `corner_service` + `preferences_json['followed_space_ids']`. |
| GET | `/api/me/join-requests` | Caller's outstanding (`pending`) outbound join-requests — `{pending_space_ids: [...]}`. The Spaces page's Discover merges these so a "Request to join" stays "Request pending" across reloads instead of reverting. Scoped to the caller's own `space_join_requests` rows. |

## HFS — Spaces

See [protocol/spaces.md](./protocol/spaces.md) for the federation
events these routes fire.

**Read access.** Every read of a space's content or roster under
`/api/spaces/{id}/…` needs a **seat** in that space — any `space_members`
role: owner, admin, moderator, member, or a read-only follower
(`subscriber`). Being a user of the same household is not enough. A
**banned** user holds no seat: banning drops the seat and records the ban in
one transaction, and every seat check (`space_member_guard.seat_of` /
`require_seat`, the space seat gate, the landing page) treats a seat row that
survived a ban as no seat. A caller
with no seat gets `403 {"error": {"code": "FORBIDDEN", "detail": "Not a space
member."}}`. On the detail, feed, members, proposals, theme and post-comment
reads (enforced by `SpaceService.require_reader` /
`space_member_guard.require_seat`) an unknown space id answers the **same**
403, so those reads are no oracle for which ids exist. Calendar, tasks, pages,
stickies, polls, bazaar, gallery, links, zones, bots, timetables, presence and
notification prefs check the seat with their own gates (same 403); chat
answers 404 to a follower. Reactions (`POST` / `DELETE …/posts/{pid}/reactions`)
need a seat too. Every route that takes a post id — comments, reactions, polls,
schedule polls — binds it to the **path** space: another space's post is
**404**, so a seat in one space never reaches another space's post. The one
exception to the seat rule is `GET /api/spaces/{id}` on a space discoverable to
the caller — a `public` / `global` space, or a `household` space **this
household hosts** (household scope is open to every local user to find and
join, whatever its join mode): a caller with no seat gets its landing fields
only (see the row below), unless they are a protected minor below the space's
`min_age` (the same 403, as the directories hide it from them too). A
`private` space, and a household-tier space another household hosts, stay 403. The
signed-URL media routes (`cover`, `icon`, `members/{uid}/picture`) are
capability URLs and stay outside this gate.

**No existence oracle.** In front of every route under
`/api/spaces/{id}/…` (and `/api/spaces/{space_id}/…`) sits the **space seat
gate** (`routes/space_seat_gate.py`, a middleware after auth): a caller with
no seat in the path space gets that same `403 {"error": {"code":
"FORBIDDEN", "detail": "Not a space member."}}` before the route runs —
reads, writes and admin routes alike (bans, compat, invite tokens, the
join-request queue, GFS servers, archive, sync, moderation, reports…). An
unknown id has no seats, so it answers **exactly** the same: no space route
tells a local user whether an id exists, and the space's own wording ("admin
or owner required", …) is only ever shown to someone with a seat (a
follower's seat counts). It lets through without a seat: a **household
admin** (who lists every space anyway — `GET /api/admin/spaces` — and may
take a global space off a GFS), a **signed media URL** (the signature is the
grant), and the routes meant for reaching a space you aren't in yet, each
with its own uniform gate: the landing page (`GET /api/spaces/{id}`),
`POST …/join-requests` and `POST` / `DELETE …/subscribe`. This also means
the [space chat](./protocol/space-chat.md) answers a caller with no seat
`403` (a follower, who has a seat, still gets its `404`).

**Followers see less.** A follower reads the posts but is an outsider to
the space's *people* (the chat refuses them too): `GET …/members` lists them
the members only — never another follower, nor themselves — without
presence (`is_online`, `is_idle`, `last_seen_at`), `joined_at` or
`location_share_enabled`; and `GET …/proposals`, `…/presence` (the map
pins) and `…/zones` need a **member's** seat (owner, admin, moderator,
member — `space_member_guard.require_member_seat`): a follower gets the
outsider's `403 "Not a space member."`, and an unknown id the same. The
live `space.proposal.updated`, `space_zone_changed` and
`space_location_updated` frames skip followers likewise, and so do the
roster frames about a follower — `space.member.joined`,
`space.member.left` and `space.member.profile_updated` whose subject holds
a follower's seat (here or on another household; an unknown subject counts
as one) reach the members only — and the "X joined" bell for a new
follower rings the members only. A frame about a member still reaches
everyone. `GET /api/spaces/{id}` carries `my_role` so a follower's SPA
still knows who it is.

**Space CRUD**

**Member count.** Every `member_count` a space surface returns — `GET /api/spaces` rows, `GET /api/spaces/{id}` (seated and landing), `GET /api/household/spaces`, `GET /api/local_invites`, and the public-space directory a household publishes to its paired peers — is one number: local and live remote seats, **never a follower** (`subscriber`) and never a tombstoned remote seat; what the space's people list shows a member (`space_repo.MEMBER_COUNT_SQL`).

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/spaces` | List every non-dissolved space the caller holds a seat in (a follower's included), sorted by name — the sidebar and the Spaces page. Each row carries the same `features` block as `GET /api/spaces/{id}`, plus, read at request time (no stored counters): **`my_role`** (`owner` / `admin` / `moderator` / `member` / `subscriber` — the Spaces page lists a `subscriber` under Following), **`member_count`** (see *Member count* above), **`last_activity_at`** (the newest feed post that isn't deleted or hidden from the feed, else when the space was created here; naive UTC `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`), **`icon_url`** (the uploaded icon, signed; `null` without one) and **`needs_you`** — the same total as the space's More badge: items held for review + open reports + join requests + open proposals awaiting the caller's vote (see `attention` below), for an owner, admin or moderator; `null` for a member or a follower. **`unread_posts`** — posts by others in the feed (not deleted, not hidden from the feed) since the caller last viewed it (`POST /api/spaces/{id}/feed/read`); `0` before the first visit. |
| POST | `/api/spaces` | Create a new space. Body: `{name}` (required), `description?`, `emoji?`, `space_type` (`private`\|`household`\|`public`\|`global`, default `private`; the API accepts any of the four, the SPA only offers `global` in the create dialog when a global server is connected; a **`household`** space seats **every active local user** as a `member` at once — everyone in the home is a member automatically, see [Household spaces](#household-spaces)), `join_mode` (default `invite_only` — the **membership** gate only; ignored for a `household` space, which is always stored `open`; readability is the separate `features.allow_subscribers` opt-in, not settable at create time, so a new space is never publicly readable until its owner PATCHes it on), `lat?` / `lon?` / `radius_km?` — make a **public** space a [local community](./protocol/local-communities.md): households join only while the home they share with this household lies inside the circle. All three or none; the centre is truncated to 4 dp (`lat` ±90, `lon` ±180), `radius_km` is 0.5–50 (one decimal), numbers only — anything else is `422`, and an area on any other tier is `422 LOCAL_AREA_PUBLIC_ONLY`. The creator is seated as owner whatever their own home. `place?` — `{lat, lon}`: pin a **global** space to a place (4 dp), for sorting Discover by distance only; any other tier is `422 PLACE_PIN_GLOBAL_ONLY`. `category?` (one of the 20 discovery values — `general`, `local`, `community`, `non_profit`, `family_parenting`, `learning`, `books_writing`, `hobby_crafts`, `music_arts`, `food_drink`, `sports_outdoors`, `health_wellbeing`, `nature_garden`, `pets_animals`, `travel`, `gaming`, `tech`, `faith_spirituality`, `politics_activism`, `work_career`; applied for public/global; a household or GFS that predates a value shows it as `general`), `min_age?` (`0`\|`13`\|`16`\|`18`; applied only for public/global tiers, ignored otherwise), `gfs_ids?` — the GFSs (this household's active connection ids, at most 8) a **global** space is listed on. A global space needs at least one while any GFS is connected (`422 GFS_SELECTION_REQUIRED`; with none connected it is created "global, not listed"); an unknown / inactive id or more than 8 is `422 GFS_SELECTION_INVALID` (`reason: "unknown"` / `"too_many"`), and `gfs_ids` on any other tier is `422 GFS_SELECTION_INVALID` (`reason: "not_global"`) — a public space is visible only to connected households and never touches a GFS. Checked before anything is stored. A new space starts with **feed, chat and calendar** only (`features` `todo`, `pages`, `gallery`, `stickies`, `bazaar`, `location`, `timetable` off): every other section is opt-in, set with `PATCH /api/spaces/{id}` `{features: {…}}` — the SPA asks "What is it for?" right after create. Existing spaces keep their features; an absent features key on an older row or peer still reads on. |
| GET / PATCH / DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}` | Read / update / **dissolve**. GET needs a seat (any role, followers included); a caller with **no** seat gets, for a `public` / `global` space or a `household` space this household hosts, the landing fields `{id, name, description, emoji, space_type, join_mode, category, features: {allow_subscribers}, member_count, local_area, place}` (what its directory entry already publishes — the SPA's `/spaces/{id}/about` page) and `403 FORBIDDEN "Not a space member."` for a private space, another household's household-tier space, or an unknown id. The full GET response includes `category` (the §23.50 discovery taxonomy, normalized to `general` when unset). PATCH accepts `category` (plus the usual `name`/`description`/`emoji`/`join_mode`/feature toggles) and **`allow_here_mention`** (boolean, owner/admin only like every config edit; non-boolean values are ignored) — whether owners and admins may page everyone with `@here`; GET returns it. **Retention:** `retention_days` (positive integer = soft-delete feed posts older than N days; `0` or negative = keep forever, GET returns `null`) and `retention_exempt_types` — a list of post types the sweep never deletes. Valid values are the `PostType` enum: `text`, `image`, `video`, `transcript`, `poll`, `schedule`, `file`, `bazaar`, `event`, `location`, `highlight_share`; the list is de-duplicated and sorted, and an unknown value or a non-list is `422` (nothing is stored). The SPA offers `image`/`video`/`file`/`poll`/`schedule`/`event`/`bazaar`/`location`. GET returns both, including on a space hosted by another household (the host's values, mirrored over `space_meta`; only the host runs the sweep). Every PATCH field is optional and an absent field is left unchanged; the SPA sends only the fields the admin edited. The `features` block carries **`allow_subscribers`** (default `false`) — the **owner-only** opt-in that makes a public/global space publicly readable (a space admin who is not the owner gets `403 owner required` if they try to change it, exactly as for `delegated_admin_authority`; a forwarded cross-household `update_config` has it pinned to the stored value on the host): with it off nothing is relayed to a connection server, no content key is sealed to a follower, and `POST /api/spaces/{id}/subscribe` is refused `403 this space does not allow subscribers`. It is independent of `join_mode`, and flipping it on a `global` space immediately re-publishes the space's metadata to every GFS that lists it — never to another (which is also what purges existing subscriber seats when it goes off; the household's own local `role='subscriber'` rows are dropped at the same time, so a follower is not left reading a space the owner withdrew). The `features` block also carries **`gfs_publish_mode`** (`"trusted"` default / `"strict"`, v_50) — the **owner-only** choice of how member households publish over a connection server: `trusted` = identified (the server learns which household posted, never the content), `strict` = anonymous under the epoch's writer group key (the server learns only that some publisher of the space posted, and refuses identified publishes into it). A non-owner gets `403 owner required`; any other value is `422 UNPROCESSABLE`; a forwarded cross-household `update_config` has it pinned on the host, and a member household takes it only from the owner household's own `SPACE_CONFIG_CHANGED`. Switching to `strict` rotates the space content key (the rotation hands every v_50 publishing household the new writer key and tells each connection server the mode and the key's pin); switching back to `trusted` tells each connection server at once. GET returns it in `features`. It applies to a **private** space too once that space uses an opaque connection-server channel (v_51 — its owner turned `private_gfs` on and it has remote members); GET returns **`gfs_private_channel`** (boolean) — `true` when this private space uses such a channel, which is when the SPA shows the owner the choice. The `features` block also carries **`private_gfs`** (boolean, default `false`) — the **owner-only** option "use the connection server for this space" of a **private** space (ignored on other tiers). Off: the space never touches a connection server — no `gfs`-type invite link, no opaque channel, no subscription. On: `gfs`-type invite links are allowed, the owner registers the space's opaque channel and every member household connected to that server takes a seat (paired and mesh-only members included), so members reach each other while the host is offline. A non-owner gets `403 owner required`; a non-boolean is `422 UNPROCESSABLE`; a forwarded cross-household `update_config` has it pinned on the host, and a member household takes it only from the owner household's own `SPACE_CONFIG_CHANGED`. **Turning it off while households that joined through an invite link are still members answers `409 PRIVATE_GFS_LINK_MEMBERS`** with `error.households: [{instance_id, display_name, members: [{user_id, display_name}]}]` and changes nothing — remove them first. Turning it off otherwise deletes every `gfs`-type invite link of the space, unregisters the channel and rotates the space content key. New private spaces are off; migration 0079 turned it on only for existing private spaces that already had link-joined members. A space left off keeps its pre-0079 invite links as grandfathered `via: "gfs_legacy"` links (listed by `GET …/invite-tokens`), still redeemable through the relay; the first household that joins through one that way turns `private_gfs` on automatically. The `features` block is **merged onto the space's current features** — a partial body changes only the keys it names and leaves the rest alone. It carries **`chat`** (boolean, default `true` — every space, existing and new, has a [chat](./protocol/space-chat.md); an owner or admin turns it off, no owner-only pinning; off hides `GET /api/spaces/{id}/chat` and refuses its reads and writes, the messages are kept). It carries the per-feature access levels **`posts_access`**, **`pages_access`**, **`tasks_access`**, **`stickies_access`**, **`calendar_access`** (`open` / `moderated` / `admin_only`). Raising a level off `open` while a member household is below protocol v_42 — or setting one to `moderated` while a member household is below v_43 (federated moderation) — answers **`409 PEERS_TOO_OLD`** with `error.households: [{instance_id, display_name, proto_version}]` and changes nothing; re-send the same PATCH with **`"force": true`** to apply it anyway. The answer carries **`forwarded`** — `true` when the space is hosted by another household and the edit was only forwarded to it (the host applies it, and re-checks `PEERS_TOO_OLD` itself; a refused access level stays as it was while the rest of the edit applies). GET also returns **`my_role`** — the caller's own role in the space (`owner` / `admin` / `moderator` / `member` / `subscriber`; absent from the no-seat landing fields), so the SPA needs no roster lookup for it — and **`member_count`** (see *Member count* above; the landing fields carry the same number) — and **`gfs_servers`** — where a **global** space is listed, read-only, for the space's members (not followers) and the owner household's admins only; anyone else gets `null`: the owner's signed list as `[{display_name, inbox_url, connected}]` (`connected`: this household holds an active connection to that GFS), `null` for any other tier or while no list is known — and **`gfs_unlisted`** (boolean): the space is global and the owner's signed list is empty — not merely that this household can't reach any entry (a remote admin's approved proposal, or none chosen yet), so the SPA can say so. GET also returns **`attention`** — what waits for the caller (the space home's "Needs you" card and the filled badge on More), read at request time: `{pending_review, reports, join_requests, proposals}` for an owner, admin or moderator, `null` for a member or a follower (absent from the no-seat landing fields). `pending_review` counts the items held for review (§4.3, any feature) and `reports` the open reports the caller may review; `join_requests` and `proposals` are for owners and admins only (a moderator gets `0` / `[]`). Each proposal is the `GET …/proposals` view plus `required` (the approvals it needs, = `needed`) and the per-reader **`voted_by_me`** — `true` when the caller proposed it or already voted (on a member household's mirror only the proposer is known), so the SPA never asks a voter to vote twice. PATCH ignores `space_type` (publication tier is proposal-gated — see below). On a `household` space a PATCH `join_mode` is ignored (it stays `open`); a tier change **into** `household` (an executed `set_public_tier` proposal) sets `join_mode` to `open` and seats every active local user, and one **out of** `household` resets `join_mode` to `invite_only`. **Local community.** PATCH `{lat, lon, radius_km}` makes a **public** space a [local community](./protocol/local-communities.md) or moves its area (same rules as create: all three, 4 dp centre, radius 0.5–50 km, `422` otherwise; another tier is `422 LOCAL_AREA_PUBLIC_ONLY`); `{lat: null, lon: null, radius_km: null}` clears it (any tier). Owner / admin only, on the household hosting the space — a space hosted elsewhere answers **`409 LOCAL_AREA_UNAVAILABLE`**. A space with *delegated admin authority* on cannot get an area, and an area blocks turning it on (**`409 LOCAL_AREA_DELEGATED`**): a delegated admin's household would seat people without the host's check. Existing members stay when the area moves or goes. The area is host-local: no `config_sequence` bump, no config change sent to member households; it re-sends the paired households' directory (`SPACE_DIRECTORY_SYNC`, v_59). A tier change out of `public` drops it. A body with only the area keys touches nothing else and answers `{id, local_area}`. GET returns **`local_area`** — `{lat, lon, radius_km}` or `null` — to everyone at this household who may read the space, the no-seat landing fields included (the centre never leaves this household: paired households get only the radius, see `GET /api/peer_spaces`). **Place pin.** PATCH `{place: {lat, lon, radius_km?}}` pins a **global** space to a place, for sorting Discover by distance only (4 dp; another tier is `422 PLACE_PIN_GLOBAL_ONLY`, a malformed point `422`); `radius_km` is the owner's optional **radius hint** ("best for people within 10 km", 0.5–50 km, one decimal, `422` outside; absent / `null` = no hint) — a hint only, never checked at join or follow; `{place: null}` removes it. Owner / admin, on the hosting household (`409 LOCAL_AREA_UNAVAILABLE` elsewhere). Host-local like the area (no `config_sequence` bump); every GFS that already lists the space is re-published so its directory shows the pin — only a GFS that advertises the signed `place_pin` capability receives it. A tier change out of `global` drops it. A body with only `place` answers `{id, place}`. GET returns **`place`** — `{lat, lon}` (plus `radius_km` when there is a hint) or `null`. Never defaulted to the household's home; joining never checks it. DELETE opens a `dissolve` **approval proposal** (v_16) — a permanent hard delete that needs a majority of admins to approve (executes immediately for a solo-admin space). |
| GET / POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/proposals` | Multi-admin approval (quorum) for critical actions (v_16). GET (needs a member's seat — a follower, like anyone without a seat, gets `403 FORBIDDEN`) lists open proposals + tally; each carries `voted_by_me` (the caller proposed it or already voted — see `attention` above),  `proposed_by_label` (the signed proposer's display name, `null` when unknown) and its `params` never echo a proposer copy. POST `{action, space_type?, gfs_ids?}` opens a `dissolve` or `set_public_tier` proposal. `gfs_ids` (a move into `global` only) names the GFSs it is listed on when it executes; it is checked like on create and kept only when the proposer is the space's **owner** — a co-admin's or a remote admin's `gfs_ids` are dropped, and if such a proposal passes the space is "global, not listed" until the owner picks servers. The owner joining an open proposal with `gfs_ids` sets the servers it executes with (nobody else can replace them); servers disconnected by then are skipped. Any admin may propose; executes once a majority of admins approve. `remote_admin_action` is not proposable (422) — only the host's forward gate opens one, for a forwarded admin action while delegation is off: the owner alone approves it, it runs as the signed sender's seat, a household holds at most 20 open at a time, and a newer `set_member_role` request for the same seat replaces the older one. A protected admin can't propose — or approve — a move to `public` / `global` (403 `ACCOUNT_PROTECTED` / `public_spaces`); rejecting stays open. Nobody can move a space a protected account owns to `public` / `global` (403 `This space can't be made public.`; an open one is rejected). `POST /api/spaces` with `space_type` `public` / `global` is refused the same way. |
| POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/proposals/{pid}/vote` | Admin approves / rejects an open proposal (`{approve}`). A reject cancels it; a majority of approvals executes it. |
| POST / DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}/archive` | Archive (read-only, reversible) / unarchive. Owner or admin. |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/summary` | What each section holds, for the meta line of More's section rows — read at request time, no stored counters. Any seat (a follower's included); no seat, a banned seat, an unknown id and a household admin without a seat all get `403 FORBIDDEN "Not a space member."`. Returns `{tasks: {open, mine}, pages: {count}, gallery: {albums, items}, stickies: {count}, bazaar: {active}, calendar: {upcoming, days}}`; a section the space has turned off is `null`. `tasks.open` counts tasks not done and not archived, `mine` those of them assigned to the caller; `gallery.items` sums the albums' photos and videos; `calendar.upcoming` counts the occurrences (a recurring event once per occurrence) under way or starting in the next `days` (30) days. |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/compat` | Owner / admin only. Per-space protocol-version compatibility of member households (#319 ¶5). Returns `{"ours": <int>, "min_member_proto_version": <int\|null>, "lagging_features": [...], "lagging_feature_keys": [...], "behind_members": [{instance_id, display_name, proto_version, lacking_features, lacking_feature_keys}]}`. `lagging_features` are the shared-space features unavailable because the weakest known member household lacks them; `behind_members` lists each lagging household and the space features it's missing. Each `*_features` list holds English labels; its `*_feature_keys` sibling holds the stable snake_case slugs of the same features in the same order (e.g. `fast_media_transfer`), which the SPA translates as `capability.<slug>`. Slugs never change once shipped; the English lists stay for older clients. Member households that have never advertised capabilities (mid-handshake) are excluded — they aren't genuinely behind. `min_member_proto_version` is `null` when there are no known remote members. |
| POST | `/api/spaces/join` | Join a space via an invite token. Body: `{token}` plus, for a code minted on another household, `issuer_instance_id` (routes the redeem over federation) and `space_id`. Only `token` is required. A code minted for someone the issuer has never federated with also carries the §D2b bootstrap block — `issuer_identity_pk`, `issuer_keywrap_pk`, `issuer_keywrap_sig` (all three required for the block to count), `issuer_proto_version`, `expires_at`, and `gfs` (base URL of the connection server that served the invite). Those are used **only** when neither a direct pairing nor a mesh route reaches the issuer: the redeem is then sealed to `issuer_keywrap_pk` and relayed by instance id through that connection server. A `subscriber` (Follower) link redeems across households like any other (v_30): the redeeming user lands at `role='subscriber'` locally, the household is seated as a read-only follower on the host, the host gossips that seat to every other member household, and every space-content write it sends is refused by each of them (§24.11 `check_space_writer`; the one opt-in is comments under `allow_subscriber_comment`). An `admin`/mod link does not grant admin on redeem: the user is seated as a `member` and the response carries `pending_role: "admin"`, with a pending elevation filed for an owner to approve (see the join-requests routes below). **Local community:** `share_location: true` — the person agreed to send this household's home rounded to ~1 km with the redeem; the SPA sends it only after a `403 LOCATION_REQUIRED` and the consent prompt. Near the edge of the area the answer is `403 JOIN_REQUEST_SENT` (a pending request the admins decide; this household keeps its side of it). 422 `REDEEM_DENIED` on a refusal (bad token, ban, no relay that can carry the envelope), 504 `ISSUER_TIMEOUT` when the issuer never answers. |
| POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/ownership` | Owner only (403 for anyone else, followers included): hand the space to `{to_user_id}`, who becomes owner while the caller stays on as admin. The new owner must be a full member of THIS household — the space authority key, owner statement and signed GFS list stay with the hosting household, so a member from another household is refused (422 `OWNER_NOT_LOCAL`), as is a follower (422 `OWNER_IS_FOLLOWER`) or the owner themself (422 `OWNER_IS_SELF`). A `public` / `global` space can't be handed to a protected account (403 — make it private first). Member households learn the new owner from the `ownership_transferred` config change; the new owner gets a `space_ownership_transferred` notification. |
| GET | `/api/admin/spaces` | Admin-only: list all spaces on this HFS. Each row carries `owner_active` (the owner's account here is still active) and `hosted_here` (not a stub of a space hosted elsewhere). |
| GET | `/api/admin/spaces/{id}/ownership` | Household admin only (403 otherwise): `{owner_active, candidates: [{user_id, display_name, role}]}` — the space's local full members with an active account, who could take it over. |
| POST | `/api/admin/spaces/{id}/ownership` | Household admin only: hand a space hosted here whose owner has **left the household** (account deactivated or deleted) to `{to_user_id}`. While the owner is active it's 409 `OWNER_ACTIVE` — they hand it over themselves (`POST /api/spaces/{id}/ownership`). The new owner follows the transfer rules (422 `OWNER_NOT_LOCAL` / `OWNER_IS_FOLLOWER`); the departed owner's seat drops to `member`. Member households learn it from the `ownership_transferred` config change, and the new owner is notified. |
| POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/feed/read` | The caller viewed the space's Feed now: `204`; the `unread_posts` of `GET /api/spaces` counts from here (migration 0103's `space_members.feed_read_at`). Any seat, a follower's included; no seat or an unknown id → `403 FORBIDDEN "Not a space member."`. The SPA calls it when the Feed section is on screen. |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/feed` | Space feed summary. Needs a seat (any role, followers included) — `403 FORBIDDEN "Not a space member."` otherwise. |
| POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/sync` | Trigger a re-sync with the space hosts. |
| POST / DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}/subscribe` | Subscribe / unsubscribe to a public or global space. Idempotent. Subscribe adds the caller as `role='subscriber'` in `space_members` (read-only member — receives content, cannot post / comment / react). For an id with **no local row**, subscribe first mirrors the listing from the first paired GFS that serves it (`GET {gfs}/gfs/spaces/{id}`) and seats a `space_type=global` stub with the GFS-served authority key (TOFU-pinned) before registering on the GFS relay — no GFS knows it, or the listing fails validation → unchanged 404; a paired GFS that errors → **502 `GFS_UNAVAILABLE`**. A private space (or another household's household space) the caller holds no seat in answers the **same 404** as an unknown id — no existence oracle; a member of one, and a local user of a household space this household hosts, get 403. Unsubscribe is a no-op for users who aren't subscribers (won't demote real members); when it removes the **last** local member of a provable GFS mirror it also unsubscribes from every paired GFS and purges the stub with its content. Returns `{subscribed}`. |

### Household spaces

A `household` space means **everyone in your home is a member automatically**.
On the household that hosts it, every **active** local user (not soft-deleted;
protected accounts included, unless below the space's `min_age`; never a user
banned from the space) gets a `member` seat when the space is created, when it
moves into the household tier, and when a new local user is provisioned. A
member may leave (`DELETE /api/spaces/{id}/members/{user_id}`) and **stays
out** — nothing re-seats on a restart — and finds the space again under
`GET /api/household/spaces`, where joining is instant. `join_mode` is always
`open` (the database refuses anything else for a household space it hosts,
migration `0101`). Automatic seats publish the usual roster event but **no**
"X joined" notification. Remote households are never seated automatically.

**Members**

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET / POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/members` | List / invite. GET needs a seat (any role, followers included) — `403 FORBIDDEN` otherwise; a **follower** gets the reduced roster (members only, no presence / `joined_at` / `location_share_enabled` — see *Followers see less* above). Each listed row (local and remote seats) carries `mention` — the exact @-token (without `@`) a composer inserts to mention that member, unique in the space (`handle`, or `handle@<user_id prefix>` when two members share a handle; `null` when the member has no token-safe handle) — and remote rows add `household_name` (the paired peer's display name, `null` if unknown). Every row has `is_owner`; on a member household the owner's mirrored seat is reported with `role: "owner"` once the host's roster named it. Someone a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from the caller, either way, shows by name only (`picture_hash` / `picture_url` `null`, offline). |
| GET / PATCH / DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}/members/me` | Self member profile. |
| POST / DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}/members/me/picture` | Space-specific avatar. |
| GET / PATCH / DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}/members/{user_id}` | Admin-only ops. `PATCH {"role":"admin"\|"moderator"\|"member"}` changes a role: the owner sets any of them, an admin moves a member only between `member` and `moderator` (403 otherwise; 422 for any other role). On a member household the change is forwarded to the space's host (v_47): **202** `{"user_id","role","forwarded":true}` — the host re-checks it and the new role arrives with its roster update (a refused one never does); 409 `HOST_TOO_OLD` (`feature: "role_change"`) when the host is below v_47; 503 `HOST_UNREACHABLE` (`reason: "unreachable"` / `"unknown_host"`) when the forward reached nobody and was not queued (also for every other forwarded admin action). |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/members/{user_id}/picture` | Fetch a member's space avatar. Anyone who may read the space (`require_reader`); anyone else — a household admin with no seat included — gets `403 FORBIDDEN "Not a space member."`. Signed-URL capable: a signed URL needs no bearer or seat (it is minted only behind the read gate, or for a pending invite's icon). 404 for a caller a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from that member, either way. |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/authors` | The authors this household knows only from their published profiles (v_60 `author_profile` items — what a GFS follower with no roster has): `[{user_id, display_name, bio, picture_url}]`, `picture_url` signed. Anyone who may read the space (`require_reader`). Across a guardian block (§CP.F2) `bio` and `picture_url` are `null`. The SPA falls back to it after the roster and the household. |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/authors/{user_id}/picture` | The WebP of a cached author profile (≤ 24 KB). Anyone who may read the space (`require_reader`, as `GET …/authors`): anyone else — a household admin with no seat, a protected account its guardian took out of the space — gets `403 FORBIDDEN "Not a space member."`. Signed-URL capable: the signed URL is the per-file capability `GET …/authors` and the space routes' person summaries mint only behind that read gate, so it needs no bearer. 404 when none, or across a guardian block. |

**Invites / joins / moderation**

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/invite-tokens` | Mint an invite link. Body `{role?, uses?, ttl_seconds?, publish_to_gfs?, via?}`; `via` is the link's type — `"gfs"` (a household that never met us redeems it through the connection-server relay) or `"internal"` (`"gfs_legacy"` appears only on listed pre-0079 links and is never mintable — `422`) (only households paired with us or reachable over the mesh; never touches a connection server, and its `code` carries no key-wrap key). Omitted: `"internal"` on a private space whose `features.private_gfs` is off, `"gfs"` otherwise (every public / global space, unchanged). `"gfs"` on a private space with `private_gfs` off → **`409 PRIVATE_GFS_OFF`** (nothing written); an unknown `via`, or `"internal"` with `publish_to_gfs`, → `422`; `role` is `member` (default) / `subscriber` / `moderator` / `admin` and is the **issuer's** decision — the redeemer can't ask for more. Admin or owner may mint `member` / `subscriber` / `moderator` (the same people who may promote to moderator); **owner only** may mint `admin`; `owner`, an unknown or a non-string role is `422 UNPROCESSABLE` with the detail `role must be 'member', 'subscriber', 'moderator' or 'admin'`. A `moderator` link seats a moderator straight through on every redeem path (local, paired, mesh, link-joined — no pending elevation; an `admin` link seats a member plus a pending elevation); a redeeming household below federation v_41 is seated as a `member` instead (never refused) and the join returns `role: "member"`. `publish_to_gfs` is a paired connection server's id — the link's blob is parked there; **global spaces only** (a GFS lists no other tier): a public space → `409 TIER_CHANGE_REQUIRED`, a private / household one → `409 SPACE_NOT_PUBLIC`, nothing written (an unpublished code still redeems through the relay). `ttl_seconds` defaults to `604800` (7 days); **both an explicit `null` and `0` mean never expires** (limited only by `uses`) — `null` is the service's spelling, `0` is what the SPA's "Never" picker sends, and the route normalises both. A negative value is `422`. Returns `201 {token, role, via, uses, uses_remaining, expires_at, created_by, created_at, code, gfs}` where `uses` is the minted total (so a client can render "3 of 10 left"), `code` is the `socialhome://invite#<blob>` string, and `gfs` is `null` or `{gfs_id, gfs_token, url, gfs_url}` (`url` is the shareable /join page, `gfs_url` the server's base URL). `403` when the role isn't permitted for the actor; on a member household's stub of a space hosted elsewhere the mint is **forwarded to the host** (v_52), which re-checks the actor's live admin seat and mints the link in its own table (publishing to its own connection to the chosen server) — the response is the host's link; `503 HOST_UNREACHABLE` when the host does not answer within 20 s, `409 HOST_TOO_OLD` (`feature: "invite_link"`) for a host below v_52, nothing is written locally; `422` for `owner` / an unknown role / an unpaired server / a server that can't host invite links (nothing is persisted — publish happens first). |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/invite-tokens` | List the space's live invite links (admin or owner), newest first, same shape as above under `{tokens: [...]}`. Expired and exhausted links are excluded — they grant nothing. `role: "admin"` links are listed to the owner only. Each link carries `created_by_name` — the minter's name as this household holds it (`null` when unknown, never the id). On a member household's stub of a space hosted elsewhere the list is **the host's** (forwarded, v_52 — every live link, whoever minted it); `503 HOST_UNREACHABLE` / `409 HOST_TOO_OLD` as for the mint. |
| DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}/invite-tokens/{token}` | Revoke one invite link (admin or owner; any admin may revoke any of the space's links except an `admin` link, which only the owner revokes — `403`). `204`, idempotent.
