| DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}/schedule-polls/{pid}/slots/{slot_id}/response` | Retract. |
| POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/schedule-polls/{pid}/finalize` | Author finalizes. |

## HFS — Conversations (DMs)

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/conversations` | List the caller's conversations. Each row carries `members[]` (other participants only — caller is filtered out) and `member_count` so the inbox renders avatar stacks + peer-name fallbacks (`Anna · Bob`) without N+1 follow-up fetches. Also returns the caller's own `last_read_at` (ISO 8601 or `null`) — the SPA uses it to find the first-unread message in the loaded thread window and anchor the entry scroll on a "New messages" divider. `managed_here` says whether this household keeps a group's member list (it created the group) — the SPA offers add / remove / rename only then. `muted_until` is the caller's own mute (UTC ISO 8601, `9999-12-31T23:59:59+00:00` = until they turn it back on), `null` when not muted or once the time has passed. `notif_level` is the caller's own group level (`"all"` \| `"mentions"`; always `"all"` for a 1:1). System chats (the [household chat](#household-chat)) are never listed here and never count toward the DM badge. `bazaar_listing` is `null` except for an "Ask the seller" thread (v_74): then `{post_id, space_id, title, mode, status, price, start_price, winning_price, currency, image_urls (first photo, signed), seller_user_id, role ("buyer" / "seller"), other_user_id, writable, closes_at}` — the listing card, the caller's side and whether the thread still takes messages. |
| POST | `/api/conversations/dm` | Get-or-create 1:1 DM. Body `{username}`. |
| POST | `/api/conversations/group` | Create group conversation (≥3 participants total — creator + ≥2 others). Body `{members: [username, ...], member_user_ids?: [user_id, ...], name?: string}`. `member_user_ids` may name people from directly paired households at v_37+ — this household becomes the group's authority (see [cross-household groups](./protocol/dm.md#group-conversations-across-households)). A person who can't join is refused with 422 `GROUP_MEMBER_UNSUPPORTED` and a message naming them and why (not paired / their household needs an update). |
| GET | `/api/conversations/{id}` | One conversation's metadata for the caller — exactly the shape of a `GET /api/conversations` row (`type`, `name`, `members`, `member_count`, `managed_here`, `unread`, `last_read_at`, `muted_until`, `notif_level`, …), so a thread loads its own header without fetching the whole inbox. 404 when it doesn't exist or is a system chat (the [household chat](#household-chat) — its metadata is `GET /api/household/chat`); 403 when the caller isn't in it (never was, left, or a 1:1 hidden from their list by a block). |
| PATCH | `/api/conversations/{id}` | Rename a group — body `{name: string \| null}`. Only a member on the group's authority household; 403 otherwise. |
| POST | `/api/conversations/{id}/members` | Add people to a group — body `{usernames?: [...], user_ids?: [...]}`; 201. Only a member on the group's authority household (403 otherwise); 422 `GROUP_MEMBER_UNSUPPORTED` as above. |
| DELETE | `/api/conversations/{id}/members/{user_id}` | Remove someone from a group. Only a member on the group's authority household (403 otherwise); 404 when they are not in it. The removed person's household gets the new roster and drops the group. |
| PUT / DELETE | `/api/conversations/{id}/mute` | Mute / unmute a conversation **for the caller only**. `PUT` body `{duration: "1h" \| "8h" \| "1w" \| "forever"}` → `{muted_until}`; `DELETE` → `{muted_until: null}`. Members only (403); 422 for any other `duration`. While muted, new messages still arrive and count toward `unread`, but create no `dm_message` bell row and no push for the caller. A time in the past reads as unmuted (no scheduler). Local only — a mute is never federated. |
| GET / PUT | `/api/conversations/{id}/notif-prefs` | The caller's own notification level for a **group** conversation. Body / response `{level}` where level ∈ `"all"` \| `"mentions"`. `all` (default) → a `dm_message` bell for every message by someone else; `mentions` → only messages that @-mention the caller. A mentioned member (either level) gets one `dm_mention` bell ("{sender} mentioned you in {chat}") **instead of** the `dm_message` one; an edit rings only the members it newly mentions. Mentions resolve against the conversation's seats on this household only. An active mute wins over both. Members only (403); `PUT` → 422 for another level or a 1:1. Push carries the title only (§25.3). Local only, never federated. |
| POST | `/api/conversations/{id}/leave` | The caller leaves. A 1:1 is hidden for them; a group is left for good (on another household's group, the authority is told with `DM_GROUP_LEAVE`). |
| GET / POST | `/api/conversations/{id}/messages` | List / send. |
| PATCH / DELETE | `/api/conversations/{id}/messages/{mid}` | Edit / delete **your own** message (in a [space chat](#hfs--spaces) the space's owner, admins and moderators may also `DELETE` anyone's message — it federates as `SPACE_CHAT_MESSAGE_DELETED` naming the moderator). `PATCH` body `{content}` → `{id, content, edited_at}`; `DELETE` → `{ok: true}` (soft delete: the row stays as "message deleted"). Sender only (403); a message that isn't in this conversation is 404; empty content, a deleted message, or a voice note / call entry (not the sender's text) is 422. Text, media captions and location pins are editable; a location is re-validated and rounded like on send. Both federate (`DM_MESSAGE` carrying `edited_at` / `DM_MESSAGE_DELETED`), open threads patch the bubble live (`dm.message_updated`), and an edit notifies only the people it newly @-mentions. |
| POST | `/api/conversations/{id}/{read\|unread}` | Unread state. `read` always advances the caller's own read watermark (their `unread` counts, the "New messages" divider, the thread's bell rows) and, by default, bulk-upserts `conversation_delivery_state` rows to `read` for every non-own message (the others' read ticks); returns `{ok, marked}`. Optional body `{"receipt": false}` — the caller turned read receipts off — moves only the watermark: no `read` rows, `marked: 0`. A non-boolean `receipt` or a non-object body is 422. |
| POST | `/api/conversations/{id}/messages/{mid}/delivered` | Stamp the caller's delivery state for one message — idempotent; `read` supersedes. See [DM reliability](./protocol/dm.md#reliability--read-receipts--delivery-state-125). |
| PUT / DELETE | `/api/conversations/{id}/messages/{mid}/reactions/{emoji}` | Add / remove the caller's emoji reaction on a DM message. `{emoji}` is URL-encoded so multi-byte glyphs survive routing. Membership-gated; fans out a `dm.message_reaction` WS frame to every conversation member and federates as `DM_MESSAGE_REACTION` to remote peers. |
| GET | `/api/conversations/{id}/messages/{mid}/reactions` | Reaction roster for one message — `{reactions: [{user_id, emoji}]}`. Membership-gated. Leaves out reactions by anyone a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from the caller, either way, and is empty for a message from such a person — the same set the message list attaches and the live `dm.message_reaction` frame reaches the caller with. |
| GET | `/api/conversations/{id}/delivery-states` | Per-message delivery/read rows for the whole conversation. Optional `?message_ids=a,b,c`. |
| GET | `/api/conversations/{id}/gaps` | §12.5 sequence holes detected for this conversation — `{gaps: [{sender_user_id, expected_seq, detected_at}]}`. Members only. |
| GET | `/api/conversations/{id}/calls` | Call history in this conversation (members only). Each row carries `initiator_display_name` — the caller's name from `users` / `remote_users`, else the conversation seat's roster name (a group member of a household never paired with); `null` when unknown, never the id. |

### Household chat

The household chat is a *system chat*: a group conversation the household
makes itself (`conversations.system_scope = 'household'`) holding every
active local user. It is never federated and never appears in
`GET /api/conversations` or the DM badge. Reading and posting go through the
`/api/conversations/{id}/...` routes above with its `conversation_id`
(messages, edit / delete, reactions, `read` / `unread`, `members`, `mute`,
`notif-prefs`, `delivered`, `delivery-states`); access is decided live —
an active local user while `feat_household_chat` is on — not by seat rows.
With the toggle off every one of them answers 403 (`FEATURE_DISABLED`,
section `household_chat`); the messages are kept. The group-management
routes (`GET` / `PATCH /api/conversations/{id}`, `POST .../leave`,
`POST .../members`, `DELETE .../members/{user_id}`) answer 404 for it, calls
answer 409 `calls_unavailable`, and its messages fire no HA
`socialhome.dm_received` event. A
bell for it reads "{sender} in Household chat" and opens `/?tab=chat`.

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/household/chat` | The household chat for the caller: `{enabled, conversation_id, unread, notif_level, muted_until, last_read_at}`. Creates the chat on first use and reconciles its seats (one per active local user; new seats start at `notif_level: "all"` with nothing unread; an account that comes back (deprovisioned, then reactivated) also starts with no unread backlog — what was said while it was away is readable, but not counted as unread, keeping its own level and mute). `unread` follows the caller's level: every unread message by someone else at `all`, only the unread ones that @-mention the caller at `mentions` (so the badge never counts chatter the caller asked not to hear about); the same rule holds for `GET /api/spaces/{id}/chat`. `muted_until` is the caller's own mute while it is on, else `null`. `last_read_at` is the caller's read watermark (UTC, `null` = never read) — the SPA anchors the "New messages" divider on it. With `feat_household_chat` off: `{enabled: false, conversation_id: null, unread: 0, notif_level: null, muted_until: null, last_read_at: null}` and nothing is created. Active local users only (403 otherwise). |

## HFS — Presence, notifications, search

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET / POST / DELETE | `/api/presence` | Own presence. `GET` rows now also carry session-presence fields `is_online`, `is_idle`, `last_seen_at` so the UI can render the green / amber dot without a separate fetch, `status` (`{emoji, text, expires_at}`, or `null` when unset or expired), `picture_url` (signed avatar URL, or `null` for initials), `updated_at` (UTC time of the member's last fix, `null` when unknown) and `located_since` (UTC time the member's place last changed — point, state or zone — "here since …"; the last fix on a row from before it was recorded) for the map pins. Leaves out people the viewer blocked and everyone a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from the viewer, either way round. |
| POST | `/api/presence/location` | Location update (rate-limited 10/min **per person posted**; an unknown name or a remote user's id is `404`). Body `{username, latitude?, longitude?, accuracy_m?, zone_name?}` — your own row, or any member's for an admin. Coordinates are stored at 4 decimals and dropped when `accuracy_m` > 500. A stated zone is kept as sent: the Home Assistant integration always sends the `zone_name` key (socialhome-client `post_location`), `null` for HA's not_home / unknown / unavailable — that `null` means "not in a zone" and stays `away`. **Only a body with neither the `zone_name` key nor a `state`** gets the server-side match: a usable GPS point is matched against the [household zones](#hfs--presence-notifications-search) (nearest centre within its radius): the Home zone gives `zone_name: "home"` / state `home`, another zone its name / state `zone`, none leaves state `away`; a point too inaccurate to store never guesses a zone. `204`. |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/presence` | Presence visible in this space — for a member's seat only (a follower, a caller with no seat and an unknown id all get `403 FORBIDDEN "Not a space member."`). Carries GPS only — `zone_name` is stripped at the household boundary (§23.8.6). Each entry's `picture_url` is signed (or `null`); `updated_at` is the member's last stored location write (UTC ISO 8601, `null` when unknown) and `located_since` when their place last changed — "here since …": a local member's presence row, another household's member's pin time (the sender's time of the change; pins are sent on change only). Another household's member also carries `stale` — `true` while we hold that household as unreachable, so the pin is the last place it sent. No entry for anyone a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from the caller, either way. |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/members` | Roster for this space (needs a seat, any role — `403 FORBIDDEN` otherwise). For a member each row carries `is_online`, `is_idle`, `last_seen_at` alongside the member metadata so the SPA renders the same dot on space pages. |
| GET | `/api/conversations/{id}/members` | Roster for one DM / group DM — members only (403 otherwise); a group lists only people still in it. Each row carries `user_id`, `username`, `display_name`, `picture_url` (signed avatar URL or `null`), `is_self`, `is_online`, `is_idle`, `last_seen_at`, `presence_shared` (`false` for a seat on a household this one isn't paired with — an "Ask the seller" thread, a group seat — whose presence never reaches it: the header shows no status rather than "Offline"), and `instance_id` / `household_name` (`null` for this household's people; `household_name` is `null` too for a group member on a household this one isn't paired with) so the thread header can render a WhatsApp-style "Online" / "Last seen 2 h ago" line AND surface the peer's avatar next to the TopBar title without a follow-up fetch. `mention` is the exact @-token (without `@`) the composer inserts to mention that member (`null` when they can't be mentioned by token). A [space chat](./protocol/space-chat.md) keeps no seats for other households: its roster adds the space's writers on other households from the space roster (never a follower, a banned or a removed seat), so the composer can mention them. Someone a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from the caller, either way, stays in the roster by name only — `picture_url` `null`, offline, `last_seen_at` `null` — and the same goes for the `members` preview on `GET /api/conversations`. |
| POST | `/api/conversations/{id}/messages` | Send a message into a DM / group DM. Body: `{ content, type?, media_url?, file_name?, mime_type?, file_size_bytes?, reply_to_id? }`. `type` ∈ {`text`, `image`, `video`, `file`, `transcript`, `location`} — defaults to `text`. A `location` message carries its pin as a JSON object in `content`: `{lat, lon, label?, accuracy_m?}` — the server rounds `lat`/`lon` to 4 dp, buckets `accuracy_m` up to a coarse radius, trims and caps `label` at 80 characters, and rejects anything malformed or out of range with 422 (see [DM location messages](./protocol/dm.md#location-messages)). Media types (`image` / `video` / `file`) require `media_url` (typically obtained from `POST /api/media/upload` immediately before) and may carry the file metadata triple. `media_url` must be a local upload reference (`api/media/<name>`; a leading `/` and a signature query are dropped) — anything else (`javascript:`, a remote URL, another path) is **rejected with 422 `INVALID_MEDIA_URL`**. `GET …/messages` serves `media_url: null` for any older stored row whose `media_url` is not in that shape. **Rejected with 422 `MEDIA_REQUIRES_DIRECT_PAIRING`** when the conversation requires the multi-hop `DM_RELAY` route — media only flows over directly-paired federation, see [DM media](./protocol/dm-media.md). |
| GET | `/api/conversations/{id}/messages` | List messages in a conversation. Each row carries the same shape as the POST body above, plus `id`, `sender_user_id`, `created_at`, `edited_at`, `deleted`, `reactions` (`[{user_id, emoji}]`, empty on a deleted message; a guardian block (§CP.F2) hides a blocked person's messages and reactions from the caller, either way, like the live frames), and the cross-household `media_sync_status` (`null` / `'pending'` / `'failed'`) so the SPA knows when to render the preview-spinner overlay on a media bubble. |
| GET / POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/zones` | List or create a per-space display zone (§23.8.7). `GET` needs a member's seat (a follower, a caller with no seat and an unknown id get `403 FORBIDDEN "Not a space member."`); `POST` admin/owner only. Body: `{name, latitude, longitude, radius_m, color?, icon?}`. `name`: 1–64 characters after trimming, no control characters; `color`: `#RRGGBB` or `null`; `icon`: a single emoji or `null`; `latitude` / `longitude`: finite, within ±90 / ±180 (stored at 4 decimals); `radius_m`: 25–50 000 — otherwise 422. |
| PATCH / DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}/zones/{zone_id}` | Update or delete a per-space zone. Admin/owner only. Partial update; `color: null` / `icon: null` clears, omitting fields leaves them. `icon` must be a single emoji or `null` — otherwise 422. |
| GET / POST | `/api/household/zones` | The household's own zones (Home, School, Work…) — **local to the household, never federated**. Behind the household `presence` feature toggle (`403 FEATURE_DISABLED`, section `presence`, while it is off). `GET` for any household member: `{zones: [Zone]}` with the Home zone first, then by name (A–Z, any case). `POST` household admins only (`403 FORBIDDEN` otherwise). Body `{name, latitude, longitude, radius_m, color?, icon?, is_home?}` — the same field rules as space zones (`name` 1–64 characters, no control characters, **unique ignoring case** → `409 ZONE_NAME_TAKEN`; centre finite and stored at 4 decimals; `radius_m` 25–50000; `color` `#RRGGBB` or `null`; `icon` one emoji or `null`; `is_home` a boolean) → `422` otherwise; at most 50 zones (`409 ZONE_LIMIT`, `params.max`). `201` with the Zone. **Zone** = `{id, name, latitude, longitude, radius_m, color, icon, is_home, external_id, read_only, created_at, updated_at}` (`external_id` `null` = created in Social Home; set = mirrored by a platform sync such as Home Assistant's `zone.home`, then `read_only: true`; timestamps UTC ISO 8601). At most one Home zone: creating one with `is_home: true` moves the flag — unless the current Home is a synced one (`409 ZONE_READ_ONLY`). A `household_zones_changed` WebSocket frame follows every change. |
| GET / PATCH | `/api/household/zones/sync` | The household-zone sync from the platform (Home Assistant's `zone.*` entities on ha / haos). Household admins only (`403 FORBIDDEN`), behind the `presence` feature (`403 FEATURE_DISABLED`). `GET` → `{available, enabled, last_synced_at, last_error}`: `available` — the platform has zones to sync (the `zone_sync` capability; `false` on standalone); `enabled` — the household's option, **on by default**; `last_synced_at` — UTC ISO 8601 of the last run that read the platform (this process lifetime, `null` before the first or while off); `last_error` — why the last run failed (`null` when it worked or while off). `PATCH {enabled: bool}` stores the option and syncs right away — on pulls the platform's zones, off removes the synced zones (zones created in Social Home stay) — and answers the new status. `422` for a non-boolean `enabled` or when `available` is `false`. While on, the sync also runs at startup and every 5 minutes; a platform that can't be read keeps the synced zones and reports `last_error`. |
| PATCH / DELETE | `/api/household/zones/{zone_id}` | Edit or delete a household zone. Household admins only. PATCH is partial — omit a field to leave it, `color: null` / `icon: null` clear; same validation as `POST`. `is_home: true` makes it the Home zone (the old Home loses the flag); `is_home: false` on the Home zone, and DELETE of the Home zone, answer `409 ZONE_IS_HOME` — mark another zone as Home first. A synced zone (`read_only`) answers `409 ZONE_READ_ONLY` to both. `404 NOT_FOUND` for an unknown id. DELETE answers `204`. |
| PATCH | `/api/spaces/{id}/members/me/location-sharing` | Member-self-service opt in or out of GPS sharing for this space (§23.8.8). Body: `{enabled: bool}`. Returns `{location_share_enabled: bool}`. Opting **in** needs a member's seat: a follower gets `403 SUBSCRIBER_READ_ONLY` (`params.action: "share location"`) — opting out always works. No seat → `403 "Not a space member."`. |
| GET | `/api/notifications` | Paginated list, newest first. Query: `limit` (1–50, default 50), `before` — a `created_at` value; only rows created strictly before it are returned — and `unread=1` for unread rows only (the page's Unread tab; `before` pages through them the same way). The SPA's "Show older" passes the oldest loaded row's `created_at` (URL-encoded: the `+00:00` offset) until a page comes back shorter than `limit`. Each row: `{id, type, title, body, link_url, read_at, created_at, object_key, space_id, params, new_count, total_count, kind}`. `kind` is the Settings kind the type falls under (`/api/me/notification-settings`; `null` for a type none files) — the row's "…" menu turns it down. `link_url` is the deep-link target — the bell renders unread items as anchors. **Who, what and where** (migration `0129`): `object_key` is the thing the row is about (`<kind>:<id>` — `post:`, `space_post:`, `feed:household`, `space_feed:`, `space_comment:`, `task:`, `event:`, `dm:`, `listing:`, `moment:`, `highlight:`, `space:`, `space_household:`, `moderation:`, `followers:`, `app:`, `app_session:`, `join_request:`, `space_invite:`, `pairing_request:`, `report:`), `space_id` the space it lives in, `params` `{actors: [{id, name}], actor_count, new, total, space_name?, flags?, handled?}` (who acted since the row was last read, newest first, at most 20; `actor_count` distinct people; `new` events since last read, `total` events in the row; `space_name` the place's name — the space's, a group chat's for a row about a chat, or for a game invite (`app_challenge`, "{name} invited you to play {app}") the app's) — `new_count` / `total_count` lift `params.new` / `params.total`. A row from before `0129` has `object_key` / `space_id` / `params` `null` and counts 1 / 1 (render it from `title`). **One row per (type, object)** while it stays active: new activity on an object whose row saw activity in the last 7 days updates that row — fresh `title` / `link_url`, `created_at` moved up, unread again — instead of adding one; after 7 quiet days the next event starts a fresh row. The unread count counts rows, not events. A row that was still unread keeps the count as is (`notification.new` with `bumped: true`); a read row **re-opens** (`bumped: true, reopened: true`, followed by a `notification.unread_count` frame with the new count). **Push (D8):** the event that starts a row, or re-opens a read one, pushes; a repeat on a row that is still unread is silent — no second push, no second `socialhome.notification_new` on the Home Assistant event bus — except that a `dm_mention` row alerts again after 5 quiet minutes, and every `call_missed` pushes (it replaces that call's ring on the device, quietly). In-app rows (activity, reactions — the defaults of their kinds) never push; a person who sets that kind to Push in `/api/me/notification-settings` gets the push, one set to Off gets no row. **Chat messages make no row (D3):** Chats keeps its own unread badge; a plain message only pushes — the first one the recipient has not read, and the first after 5 quiet minutes in the conversation (tag `dm_message:<link>`). An @-mention in a group still makes a `dm_mention` row; `POST /api/conversations/{id}/read` reads it, and every device of theirs gets a `notification.unread_count` frame. Deleting the object (a post, space comment, task, moment, highlight; a calendar event before its cancellation row) deletes its rows for everyone — a decision about the author's own content (`post_moderated`, `moderation_decided`) stays; leaving a space (or being removed or banned) deletes that person's rows of it, and a dissolved space everyone's, except the `space_dissolved` notice; every recipient who lost an unread row gets a `notification.unread_count` frame. Action items ("Needs you", see `GET /api/notifications/needs-you`) — `space_join_requested`, `moderation_pending`, `space_report` (all linking the space's `/moderation` queue), `space_invite`, `pairing_request`, `app_challenge` — are **handled for every recipient** at once: a join request when an admin approves or declines that request, the review queue rows once their queue has nothing of that kind left (a post reviewed or expired, a report resolved), an invite when the invitee accepts or declines it, a pairing request when an admin approves or declines it, a game invite when your app takes it in (`accepted`), you decline it, the challenger withdraws it (`withdrawn`) or it expires after 14 days. Each of those rows then carries `params.handled` `{outcome, by}` — `outcome` one of `approved`, `declined`, `accepted`, `expired`, `withdrawn`, `done`; `by` `{id, name}` of who handled it (`null`: the recipient themself, or nobody) — and reads as read; each recipient's devices get a `notification.unread_count` frame naming it in `handled`. An applicant on this household hears another household's answer to their join request (`space_join_approved` / `space_join_denied`) from the answer this household already receives. Retention: at most 500 rows per user — the prune drops read rows first, then the oldest unread ones, never an unread action item; rows older than 90 days are purged, except unread action items. Space post / comment rows (`space_post_created`, `space_comment_added`, `space_mention`) go to the space's members only, per their `/api/spaces/{id}/notif-prefs` level (an edit of a post / comment adds a `space_mention` row only for members it newly mentions); `dm_mention` rows ("{sender} mentioned you in {chat}") go to a group member a message — or an edit — newly mentions, per `/api/conversations/{id}/notif-prefs`, and clear on read. **New posts group per feed (D5):** `post_created` rows are about `feed:household` and open `/feed`, `space_post_created` rows about `space_feed:{space_id}` and open `/spaces/{id}` — one row per feed whose `params` count the posts (`new`, `total`) and name who posted (`actors`, `actor_count`); visiting the feed (`read-by-link` with `/feed` or `/spaces/{id}`) reads it, deleting one post leaves it, leaving the space removes it; a mention or `@here` in a post stays a row about that post. The household feed is "Everything" for everyone: posts (`post_created`) and comments (`comment_added`) are in-app rows for the whole household, and a comment pushes the post's author (`post_replied`) and the author of the comment it answers (`comment_replied`) instead. A reaction to your post (`post_reacted` — household feed or space, one row per post), moment (`moment_reacted`) or highlight (`highlight_reacted`) is an in-app row, no push (unless Reactions and follows is set to Push). Rows from before this release with type `dm_message` still read and clear as before. **Calls and small decisions:** a call that rings a local person pushes `call_incoming` — no row — "{caller} is calling" / "… in {chat}", tag `call_missed:/dms/{id}`; a call nobody answered (it rang 90 s, or the caller hung up first) is a `call_missed` row about `dm:{id}` — one per chat, its `new` counting the calls — and a push "Missed call from {caller}" (/ "… in {chat}") that replaces the ring. Both under Messages and calls; neither for a muted chat. Being put in a group chat is a `chat_added` row ("{name} added you to {chat}", or "You were added to {chat}" when the group's household doesn't say who), not for the person who added you and never for a 1:1. Opening the chat (`POST /api/conversations/{id}/read`) reads `call_missed` and `chat_added` rows too. A poll closed by its author is a `poll_result` row for everyone here who voted in it, about the post (`post:` / `space_post:`), "{author} closed a poll you voted in" / "… a poll in {space}" — a close from another household (`SPACE_POLL_CLOSED`) tells this household's voters. A poll that reached its `closes_at` closes on its author's household (and so for every member household) and is a `poll_result` row naming nobody for every voter, the author too: "A poll you voted in has ended" / "A poll in {space} has ended"; past its deadline a poll reads `closed: true` and a vote is refused (`409 POLL_CLOSED`). A date poll whose time was picked is a `poll_scheduled` row for everyone here who answered it, not the organiser, about the post: "{organiser} picked a time for a date poll you answered" / "… a time in {space}" — never the title or the time; a peer's `SPACE_SCHEDULE_FINALIZED` tells this household's people without adding a second calendar entry. A report reviewed by the space's or household's moderators is a `report_answer` row for its reporter on this household, about `report:{id}` — "Your report (in {space}) was reviewed", never who reviewed it, what they decided or whom it was about. Every title names only people, chats and spaces, in the recipient's language (§25.3, D11). `calendar_event_created` rows are **scoped to the event's audience**: personal-calendar events notify the calendar's owner only (and not when the owner created the event themselves); space-calendar events are an in-app row (no push) for the space's members at level `all`, except the creator. The household-wide fanout from earlier builds is gone. A space event's feed card (the calendar bridge's `event` post) rings nobody: the event's own `calendar_event_created` row is its one bell. `link_url` opens the exact thing (`socialhome/services/notification_links.py`): a new-posts row its feed (`/feed`, `/spaces/{id}`), a household post `/post/{id}[?comment={cid}]`, a space post `/spaces/{id}?post={pid}[&comment={cid}]`, a task `/organize?list=…&task=…` or `/spaces/{id}/tasks?list=…&task=…`, a listing `/bazaar?listing={id}`, a space event the space's Calendar, a declined join request the space's `/about` page, a declined invite the space's Members settings. |
| GET | `/api/notifications/unread-count` | Count. |
| GET | `/api/notifications/needs-you` | The caller's **Needs you** items (D12): requests that wait for them, read or not, newest first, at most 100 — `{items, count}`, each item a row as in `GET /api/notifications`. Types: `space_join_requested` (one row per request, `object_key` `join_request:<request_id>`; `params.flags` lists why it can't be approved with one tap: `edge` — a local community's "near the edge", `unpaired` — from a household this one is not paired with, `admin` — an admin grant, `age` — the space has an age limit), `moderation_pending` / `space_report` (one row per space's review queue, `space:<id>`), `space_invite` (`space_invite:<space_id>`, opens `/spaces` where the invite card asks a local community's location consent — never a one-tap join) `pairing_request` (household admins, `pairing_request:<id>`, opens `/connections`) `app_request` (a member asked for an app, one row per app, `app:<app_id>`, opens `/apps/<app_id>/about`; resolves once the app is installed or an admin answers "Not now"), `app_update` (household admins: an installed app has a newer compatible version — written by the daily update check, one row per app, `app:<app_id>`, the row's `body` is the version it offers, `params.space_name` the app's name, opens `/apps/<app_id>/about`; the next day's check adds nothing, an even newer version re-opens the same row; it resolves when any admin updates the app (`POST /api/apps/{app_id}/update`, "Handled by …"), the app is uninstalled, or the update is gone from the catalog or needs a newer Social Home; pushes as any Needs-you row does) and `app_challenge` (a game invite, one row per invite, `app_session:<app_id>/<session_id>`, opens `/apps/<app_id>`; declined with `POST /api/apps/{app_id}/sessions/{session_id}/decline`) and `app_challenge_continue` ("Anna wants to continue your Chess game": an old game picked up again, the same Play / Decline) and `app_challenge_waiting` (D10: a game invite for an app this household doesn't have, or turned off — `app_session:<app_id>/<session_id>`, `params.flags` `["app_missing"]` or `["app_off"]`, `params.space_name` the app's name from this household's catalog / install, opens `/apps/<app_id>/about`; one row per (invitee, app, sender person) — a further invite from them moves the row on to the newest session and counts it in `params.total`, silently — and at most 5 senders' rows per invitee and app; the SPA offers "Ask an admin" / "Ask a grown-up" (`POST /api/apps/{app_id}/requests`) or, for an admin, "See Chess", and Decline (`POST /api/apps/{app_id}/sessions/{session_id}/decline`); once the app is added or turned on it resolves (`done`) and the invite comes back as an `app_challenge` row with Play; nothing is ever sent to the inviter). Each item is checked against the request it asks about: one answered, expired or gone, a queue that emptied, or a recipient who is no longer an admin there leaves the list and its row resolves (`handled.outcome: "done"`); a `dm_contact_requested` row from an older build (1:1s between paired households need no request) resolves as `done` on the first look; a game invite whose app is off or that is no longer kept resolves as `done`, one older than 14 days as `expired` (a waiting `app_challenge_waiting` too). Reading a row never takes it off this list; only handling it does. The SPA pins them above the timeline (3, then "Show N more", grouped per space). |
| POST | `/api/notifications/{id}/read` | Mark read. |
| POST | `/api/notifications/read-all` | Mark all read. |
| POST | `/api/notifications/read-by-object` | The caller saw these things. Body `{"object_keys": ["post:p1", "dm:c1", …]}` (1–20 keys, each containing `:` and ≤ 256 chars, else `422`): every unread row of the caller whose `object_key` is one of them is marked read, whatever its type. `{ok, marked}`; when `marked > 0` every device of the caller gets a `notification.unread_count` frame. |
| POST | `/api/notifications/read-by-link` | The caller saw these pages. Body `{"links": ["/post/p1", "/spaces/s1?post=p2", …]}` (1–20 in-app paths starting with `/`, ≤ 512 chars each, else `422`): every unread row of the caller whose `link_url` opens one of them is marked read. A row that picks a part of the page out (`?comment=`, `?reply=`) counts as that page, so `/post/p1` reads `/post/p1?comment=c1` too; `/spaces/s1` (the space's feed) does **not** read its posts' rows (`/spaces/s1?post=…`). The SPA calls it once a post page or comment sheet, a moment, a highlight or a space's feed is on screen. `{ok, marked}`; when `marked > 0` every device of the caller gets a `notification.unread_count` frame. |
| GET | `/api/search` | Full-text search (posts, comments, spaces, users). Query `q` (≥ 2 chars), `type?` (`posts` / `people` / `spaces` / `pages` / `messages`), `scope?`, `space_id?`, `limit?`, `offset?`. Answers `{hits: [{scope, ref_id, space_id, title, snippet}], counts: {scope: n}}`. **Access, hits and counts alike:** a row tied to a space (space posts, pages, spaces) is returned and counted only for a caller with a **seat** there (any role, a follower included); DM `message` rows are never returned or counted (the index carries no conversation id to check against). A `space_id` the caller has no seat in — or an unknown one — answers `{hits: [], counts: {}}`, exactly like a query with no matches. Fails closed: without the space directory or a caller, nothing space-bound is returned; an error reading the caller's seats fails the request. |

## HFS — Pairing

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/pairing/initiate` | Admin-only. Generate a QR payload. Optional body `{reach?: "url" \| "url_gfs" \| "gfs", gfs_id?: string, share_home?: boolean}` — empty / unparseable = `url`, the classic code. `share_home` — whether this household shares its home location with the household that will scan the code: the code carries `home_lat` / `home_lon` (4 dp) only when it is on, and the pair row created when the peer-accept arrives gets the same switch; absent = the household's `share_home_default` (`GET /api/household/preferences`), a non-boolean → 422 `UNPROCESSABLE`. The code never says what was chosen. The base URL comes from the platform adapter (`[standalone].external_url` or the HA integration's pushed base); `url` / `url_gfs` return 422 `NOT_CONFIGURED` if unset. `url_gfs` / `gfs` name one of this household's active GFS connections that relays envelopes (`gfs_id` when it does, else the first) and add `reach`, `gfs: {url, instance_id}`, `keywrap_pk`, `keywrap_sig`, `keywrap_suite`, `proto_version` to the payload (`gfs`: `inbox_url` is `""`); 422 `GFS_NOT_CONNECTED` without such a connection. Unknown `reach` → 422 `INVALID_REACH`. See [protocol/pairing.md](./protocol/pairing.md#reach--pairing-through-a-gfs). |
| POST | `/api/pairing/accept` | Admin-only (signed-in; `401` without credentials, `403` for a non-admin). Scanner posts its side of the DH: the scanned code plus `share_home?: boolean` — whether the `PAIRING_PEER_ACCEPT` carries this household's home location (and the new row's `share_home`); absent = the household's `share_home_default`, a non-boolean → 422 `UNPROCESSABLE` before anything is stored or sent. Clients set it after the code's own fields (the SPA always does), so a `share_home` key planted in a code never decides it. `422 INVALID_PEER_URL` when the code's inbox URL is not a usable household address. For a GFS-reach code: `422 GFS_NOT_SHARED` when the code has no inbox URL (or this household has none) and this household is not connected to the code's GFS — nothing is sent; `422 KEYWRAP_INVALID` when the code's key-wrap key is not bound to its identity; `422 INVALID_REACH` for an unknown reach. |
| POST | `/api/pairing/confirm` | Admin-only. Confirm SAS-verified pair. |
| POST | `/api/pairing/introduce` | Admin-only. Introduce self to an intermediary. |
| POST | `/api/pairing/auto-pair-via` | Admin-only. Ask a mutual peer to relay. `target_instance_id` must be a household id (32 lowercase base32 characters) — anything else is `422`. Stores a provisional `pending_sent` row (`pending_kind: "request"`); the request is answerable for 24 h. |
| GET / POST | `/api/pairing/auto-pair-requests[/{id}/{approve\|decline}]` | Admin-only. Auto-pair queue; a request is kept for 24 h. Approve stores the requester's row `pending_sent` (`pending_kind: "approval"`) and acks it through the introducer — the pairing confirms when the requester's first authenticated envelope arrives (see [`protocol/pairing.md`](protocol/pairing.md#flow--auto-pair-via-relay)). An introduction is queued only when its id derives from its key and its timestamp is within 24 h and not past the ±300 s skew window. Approve answers `410 REQUEST_EXPIRED` past 24 h from the requester's signed timestamp and `409 PAIRING_CONFIRMED` for a household already paired. |
| GET | `/api/pairing/connections` | Paired peers. Readable by any signed-in member (the dashboard map renders it read-only); every write under `/api/pairing/*` is admin-only (`401` without credentials, `403` for a non-admin). Now also carries `home_lat` / `home_lon` per row (4dp-truncated, `null` when unset) so the SPA can render a household map without a follow-up fetch. Each row carries `instance_id`, `display_name`, `status`, **`pending_kind`** (which handshake a pending row is: `"code"` — a pairing code shown or scanned, `"request"` — we asked a connected household to introduce us, `"approval"` — we approved an introduction and wait for the requester; `null` once confirmed), `reachable`, **`transport`** (`"rtc"` when the WebRTC DataChannel is open, `"https"` when running on the HTTPS-inbox fallback, `null` when the peer is unreachable or pending), **`share_home`** (`true` / `false` — whether this household's home coordinates are shared with the peer; defaults to `true`), **`queued_envelopes`** (integer — federation envelopes still `pending` in the outbox for that peer, i.e. waiting to be sent and retried automatically once the peer is reachable; `0` when there is no backlog, which is what tells an admin a long-dark household apart from a momentary drop), and **`dropped_envelopes`** (integer — envelopes in the terminal `failed` state: a PERMANENT rejection or an exhausted retry budget. These are **not** retried and are purged 24 h after going terminal, so a non-zero value is delivery loss, not a queue). The two counts are deliberately separate: reporting only the pending one renders dropped messages as "queued for delivery". Also **`last_relay_accepted_at`** (naive UTC `"YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS"` or `null` — when the connection-server relay last *accepted* an envelope for this peer; held in memory, so `null` again after a restart until the next relay send) and **`relay_only`** (bool — `true` when that acceptance is newer than `last_reachable_at`, or there has never been a proven delivery: the peer's recent traffic has only been handed to the relay, which answers a uniform 202 whether or not the household is online, so it is "accepted", not "delivered"). The GFS fallback (v_54, see [`protocol/gfs-relay.md`](protocol/gfs-relay.md#turning-the-fallback-on-for-an-existing-pair)): **`gfs_relay`** (bool — this household's own opt-in for the peer), **`gfs_routes`** (integer — GFSes both households were proven to use; a count, never which ones), **`peer_keywrap_known`** (bool — we hold the peer's key-wrap key, i.e. its admin turned the fallback on too, or the pair was made with a GFS reach) and **`gfs_relay_available`** (bool — the fallback can work with this peer at all: confirmed, directly paired, v_53+, and either its key is known or it runs v_54+; `false` means the other household needs a newer Social Home). Local operator info only — nothing new is sent to the connection server. Whitelisted fields only. |
| GET | `/api/connections` | Alias of the above. Returns the same shape including `share_home`, `queued_envelopes`, `dropped_envelopes`, `last_relay_accepted_at`, `relay_only`, `gfs_relay`, `gfs_routes`, `peer_keywrap_known`, `gfs_relay_available` and `local_alias` per row. |
| DELETE | `/api/pairing/connections/{instance_id}` | Admin-only. Unpair — first sends the peer a signed `UNPAIR` (first attempt bounded to 5 s), then drops its queued outbox envelopes, the mesh hints it announced and its row, and pushes a `connection.removed` WS frame to every household member. Space membership is kept. Returns `200 {ok: true, peer_notified}` — `peer_notified: false` when the peer was unreachable: the connection is gone locally at once, and the `UNPAIR` stays queued for up to 30 days behind an `unpairing` tombstone that grants no trust (see `docs/protocol/pairing.md` § Unpairing); `404` for an unknown peer. A pairing that is still pending (`pending_sent` / `pending_received`) is cancelled instead, exactly as `DELETE /api/pairing/pending/{instance_id}` does — no `UNPAIR`, no tombstone; should it be confirmed while that runs, it is unpaired. |
| DELETE | `/api/pairing/pending/{instance_id}` | Admin-only. Cancel a pairing that was never confirmed (the "Cancel pairing" action on a pending connection card). One transaction deletes the `pending_pairings` session(s) behind it and the row — only while it is still pending — with its queued envelopes, DM / space media and mesh hints, so the code, the SAS, the peer's signed peer-accept / peer-confirm and a late trust-relay ack are refused from then on; an outstanding trust-relay request is forgotten. Only after that, when the removed row held the peer's key, the peer gets one encrypted, signed `PAIRING_ABORT` `{token, reason: "cancelled"}` (a trust-relay approval gets `UNPAIR` instead — its requester may already be confirmed) (one attempt to its inbox, bounded to 5 s, never queued). Pushes `connection.removed`. `200 {ok: true, peer_notified}` — `peer_notified: false` when the other household could not be told (or there was no channel to tell it on); `404 NOT_FOUND` when there is no pending pairing with that household (unknown, already cancelled — a second cancel publishes nothing); `409 PAIRING_CONFIRMED` when it is confirmed, including one confirmed while the cancel ran — then nothing was removed or sent. See [`protocol/pairing.md`](protocol/pairing.md#cancelling-a-pending-pairing). |
| GET | `/api/pairing/connections/{instance_id}/transport-detail` | Admin-only. Returns `{"last_relay": {"via": <iid>, "ts": <iso>} \| null, "inbox_url": <str> \| null}` — the most recent DM that relayed via a third household within the last 24h, and the peer's inbox address. `inbox_url` is set only for a confirmed, directly paired (`manual`) peer that isn't relay-only — never for a `space_session` household (the connection server shields addresses between link-introduced households). The member-readable connections listing never carries it. Powers the SPA's Manage detail panel. |
| PATCH | `/api/pairing/connections/{instance_id}` | Admin-only. Accepts `{"share_home": bool}` — flip whether this household's home coordinates are shared with the peer. Setting `false` immediately fires a one-shot `LOCAL_HOME_LOCATION_CHANGED` with null coords to revoke the peer's pin; setting `true` fires the current coords to restore it. Idempotent. Also accepts `{"gfs_relay": bool}` (v_54) — turn the GFS fallback on or off for this peer: on sets our opt-in, sends the peer our key-wrap key inside an encrypted `INSTANCE_CAPABILITIES_UPDATED` and probes for shared GFSes (an off → on switch always probes; sending `true` again repeats the key hand-over with an ordinary, throttled probe — a retry); off clears the opt-in, drops the peer's GFS routes at once and tells the peer (`gfs_relay: false` in the same encrypted announcement) so it drops its routes to us too. All fields are validated before any is applied, and the switch is applied before `share_home`, so a combined body that answers an error changes nothing. Routes form only once both households have turned it on (see [`protocol/gfs-relay.md`](protocol/gfs-relay.md#turning-the-fallback-on-for-an-existing-pair)). Confirmed, directly paired households only: `409 GFS_RELAY_NOT_ALLOWED` for a household met through an invite link or a pairing still in progress, `404 NOT_FOUND` for an unknown peer, `422` for a non-boolean. Both fields may be combined; the response is the updated row (same shape as the list). |
| PATCH | `/api/pairing/connections/{instance_id}/alias` | Admin-only. Body `{alias: string\|null}` — set or clear the local-only rename of the peer (cap 80 chars; whitespace-only clears). Returns `{instance_id, display_name, local_alias, effective_display_name}`. Never federated. |
| GET / POST | `/api/pairing/relay-requests[/{id}/{approve\|decline}]` | Admin-only. Relay-request queue. |
| GET | `/api/friends` | Connected-people dashboard payload (non-admin). Returns `{instance, households[], totals}` — the local household block + every confirmed remote household with its member list (joining `remote_instances` × `remote_users`) plus household coordinates, per household `reachable` + `unreachable_since` (UTC time it stopped answering, `null` while reachable), and `supports_group_dm` (v_37+: its people can be put in a group chat). Every member row (local and remote) carries `user_id`, `display_name`, `personal_alias` (the viewer's private nickname or `null`), `picture_hash` / `picture_url` (signed), the session presence triple `is_online` / `is_idle` / `last_seen_at`, `status` — `{emoji, text, expires_at}` (each `null` when unset; `expires_at` is a UTC ISO-8601 deadline) or `null` when unset or already past `expires_at` (dropped server-side even before the expiry sweep) — and `bio` (`null` when empty); local rows add `username`, remote rows `instance_id` / `remote_username`. Local status + bio come from the user record (same source as `/api/presence`); remote ones from the `remote_users` cache — `bio` via `USER_UPDATED` / `USERS_SYNC`, `status` via `USER_STATUS_UPDATED`. People the viewer blocked, and everyone a guardian block (§CP.F2) separates from the viewer either way round, are left out entirely; so are deprovisioned remote users and inactive local accounts. Whitelisted fields only — `routing_secret` / `key_self_to_remote` / `remote_inbox_url` / identity public keys never appear. |
| GET | `/api/friends/{user_id}` | One person for the SPA's person sheet — the member row `GET /api/friends` would list for them (same keys, signed `picture_url`) plus `is_local` and `household` `{instance_id, display_name, is_own}`. Visible set is exactly the overview's: active local users and non-deprovisioned people of socially paired households. 404 `NOT_FOUND` — without saying why — for unknown ids, people the viewer blocked, guardian-block (§CP.F2) counterparts either way round, deprovisioned remote users, inactive local accounts, and people of households met only through a shared space or a pending pair. |
| GET | `/api/admin/federation/compat` | Admin-only. Federation-compatibility panel. Returns `{"ours": <int>, "peers": [...]}` where `ours` is this build's advertised `proto_version` and each peer carries `instance_id`, `display_name`, `proto_version`, `status`, `last_reachable_at`, `capabilities_known` (bool — `false` ⇒ peer is paired but has never advertised capabilities, so its `proto_version` is the conservative default rather than a confirmed value), `lacking_features` (plain-language English labels of the features the peer's version is below, e.g. "Member lists shared between households"), and `lacking_feature_keys` (the stable snake_case slugs of the same features in the same order, e.g. `roster_gossip`; the SPA translates them as `capability.<slug>` and falls back to the English label for a slug it doesn't know). Slugs never change once shipped. Confirmed peers only, ordered by display name. |
| POST | `/api/admin/federation/resync` | Admin-only. Ask a peer to re-broadcast state for a named scope (§319.6). Body `{instance_id, scope}` where `scope` is `"capabilities"`, `"space:<id>"`, or `"calendar:<id>"` (the latter two replay membership-gated content via the §4.4 resume path). Returns `{"status": "ok", "instance_id", "scope"}`. 400 `UNPROCESSABLE` on a missing `instance_id` / unrecognised scope; 409 `PEER_TOO_OLD` when the peer's advertised `proto_version` is below v_19 (it has no resync handler). |
| GET | `/api/admin/federation/external-url` | Admin-only. The admin-set federation inbox base URL. Returns `{base, effective, source, manual_supported}` — `base` is the stored value (`null` when unset), `effective` is what `PlatformAdapter.get_federation_base()` actually resolves (the peer-facing URL, i.e. `base` + `/federation/inbox`), and `source` is `"manual"` / `"auto"` / `null` — the source of `effective`. The two differ whenever an automatic source is also present, so the UI can say which one is in effect rather than leave an admin guessing. `manual_supported` is `false` where the platform owns the address (`PlatformAdapter.honours_manual_federation_base` — the Home Assistant App, where the integration supplies it); there a stored `base` is still reported but never in effect, so `source` is `"auto"` (or `null`) and the SPA hides the field. |
| PUT | `/api/admin/federation/external-url` | Admin-only. Upsert `{"base": "https://..."}`. Validates the scheme (http/https), strips a trailing slash, and strips a trailing `/federation/inbox` so pasting a full inbox URL doesn't double it. `{"base": null}` (or an empty string) deletes the row, handing control back to the deployment's automatic source. On a value change, fans out `URL_UPDATED` to every confirmed peer so their cached `remote_inbox_url` tracks the move. Returns `{ok, base, changed, peers_notified}`. 422 `UNPROCESSABLE` on a non-http(s) value. 422 `FEDERATION_BASE_MANAGED_BY_PLATFORM` (setting or clearing) where `manual_supported` is `false` — nothing is stored and no `URL_UPDATED` goes out. |
| GET | `/api/admin/federation/ice-servers` | Admin-only, read-only. The ICE servers the **federation transport** is currently using, **with secrets removed**: each entry is `{urls, kinds, has_credentials}` — `credential` is an HMAC of `webrtc_turn_secret` under the recommended coturn setup, so it is never returned, and `username` (an `expiry:user_id` pair) is reduced to the `has_credentials` flag. Also returns `has_turn`, `turn_usable` (the same conclusions the boot diagnostics warn about) and `pulls_from_home_assistant` (`PlatformAdapter.provides_ice_servers`: the HA-backed platforms pull HA Core's list, which replaces the config-derived one). Purely diagnostic — whether RTC can traverse a network is otherwise visible only in a log warning. |
| GET | `/api/admin/diagnostics` | Admin-only. Support bundle an operator can attach to a bug report: build/version, deployment mode, whether a federation base resolves (host only), the peer table with reachability timestamps (`last_reachable_at` / `unreachable_since` / `capabilities_seen_at`) plus `last_relay_accepted_at` / `relay_only` (accepted by the connection-server relay vs. delivered — same meaning as on `/api/connections`), outbox backlog grouped by peer and status with `max_attempts`, redacted ICE servers, and the schema migration version. Built from an **allow-list** of fields — never by subtracting `SENSITIVE_FIELDS` from rows — so a column added later is absent by default rather than leaked. Excludes peer display names, peer inbox paths (they embed a per-pair secret; only scheme+host is kept), home coordinates, TURN credentials and all user content. `?download=1` adds a `Content-Disposition` filename. |
| PATCH | `/api/admin/instance` | Admin-only. Rename the household — set the federated instance display name. Body `{display_name}` (1–80 chars after trimming). Persists `instance_identity.display_name` and re-broadcasts it to every confirmed peer via `INSTANCE_CAPABILITIES_UPDATED`, so paired households see the new name without re-pairing. Returns `{"display_name"}`. 422 `UNPROCESSABLE` on a missing/blank/over-length name. |
| GET | `/api/admin/home-location` | Admin-only. The household's home (Admin › Settings › Our home location): `{latitude, longitude, from_platform}` — `null` coordinates when none is set. With `from_platform: true` (the adapter's `HOME_LOCATION_SYNC` capability — Home Assistant) the value is Home Assistant's `/api/config` latitude / longitude, read again on every call (stored + announced when it moved). |
| PUT | `/api/admin/home-location` | Admin-only. Set the home: body `{latitude, longitude}` (numbers, ±90 / ±180, not `0, 0`), stored at 4 dp in `instance_identity.home_lat` / `home_lon`. A change is announced as `LocalHomeLocationUpdated` (paired households with `share_home` on get `LOCAL_HOME_LOCATION_CHANGED`; the household's tabs a `local.home_changed` frame). Returns the GET shape. 422 `INVALID_HOME_LOCATION`; 409 `HOME_LOCATION_FROM_PLATFORM` when Home Assistant owns it. |
| DELETE | `/api/admin/home-location` | Admin-only. Remove the home (announced with `null` coordinates). Returns the GET shape. 409 `HOME_LOCATION_FROM_PLATFORM` when Home Assistant owns it. |

## HFS — Calls & WebRTC

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/webrtc/ice_servers` | STUN/TURN config (alias: `/api/calls/ice-servers`). |
| GET / POST | `/api/calls` | List / initiate. Body `{conversation_id, call_type, sdp_offer}` for 1:1, or `sdp_offers: {user_id: sdp}` (one offer per callee) for a group mesh; a callee with no offer is not rung. Response carries `participants` (everyone invited, caller included). 422 `too_many_participants` above 6 people (`MAX_CALL_PARTICIPANTS`). 409 `calls_unavailable` in a system chat (the household chat) — `POST /api/calls/{id}/join` answers the same, and an inbound `CALL_OFFER` naming one never rings. |
| GET | `/api/calls/active` | Current active call. |
| POST | `/api/calls/{id}/{answer\|join\|decline\|hangup}` | Lifecycle. `answer {sdp_answer}` answers the caller; a second answer from the same callee (another device) is 409 `already_answered`. `answer {sdp_answer, to_user}` answers another participant's mesh-leg offer and is relayed to them only. `join {sdp_offers}` offers mesh legs to named participants (`call.peer_join`) — used by a late joiner and by a callee opening legs to higher-id callees. In a group call `decline` / `hangup` take only that participant out (frames carry `by` + `over`); the call closes once fewer than two are left. `to_user` must be another participant (403). |
| POST | `/api/calls/{id}/ice` | Trickle ICE candidate. Optional `to_user` targets one mesh leg; without it the candidate fans out to every other participant. |
| POST | `/api/calls/{id}/quality` | Report RTT / jitter / loss. |

## HFS — Push

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/push/vapid_public_key` | Public VAPID key for `pushManager.subscribe()` (any signed-in user). |
| POST | `/api/push/subscribe` | Register (or re-register) this browser's subscription. Body is `PushSubscription.toJSON()` plus an optional `id`; the SPA sends a stable id derived from the endpoint (SHA-256) so it can delete the row later without the server ever echoing the endpoint. An upsert never rewrites another user's row. Returns `{id}`. |
| DELETE | `/api/push/subscribe/{sub_id}` | Remove one of your own subscriptions (204; 404 if unknown or not yours). |
| GET | `/api/push/subscriptions` | List own subscriptions. |

## HFS — GFS connections & public spaces

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET / POST | `/api/gfs/connections` | List / connect (admin). Each listed row carries `connected` / `last_error` (live WebSocket health) `possession_proven` (as last observed in this process: `true` the server at `inbox_url` proved the pinned key with `POST /gfs/proof`, `false` an older GFS without the proof or a failed one, `null` not checked yet — never a probe), `proof_required_at` (UTC ISO 8601 of the first successful proof, migration 0094 — from then on the WebSocket reconnect's bulk reconcile runs only after a fresh proof — a verified failure skips it, a retryable answer retries it after 15 s / 1 min / 5 min and then hourly; `null` = never proven) and `envelope_relay` (bool: the server proved, under its signed capability block, that it relays household envelopes; `true` only for an `active` row, read from this process's capability cache, warmed on pair, once in the background at startup and on each WebSocket reconnect, so a list read never contacts a GFS; the pairing screen offers the `url_gfs` / `gfs` reach only when a row has it). Connect with a scanned or pasted GFS pairing code `{gfs_url, token}`. Registration sends `{token, instance_id, public_key, display_name}` plus the key-wrap fields — no household address, so no External URL is required. `201` + the connection (`status` `active`, or `pending` when the GFS approves households by hand). Errors answer the same codes as the open sign-up step for the same causes: `409 ALREADY_CONNECTED` (this household already has that GFS — nothing is sent), `502 GFS_UNREACHABLE` (no answer, or a 5xx from `/gfs/info` / `/gfs/register`), `422 GFS_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` (the address answers, but not as a GFS — a 4xx or a non-descriptor from `/gfs/info`; retrying won't help), `422 GFS_PROOF_FAILED` (the GFS's signed `/gfs/info` offers `proof_of_possession` and its `POST /gfs/proof` answer does not verify against that key at the address used — an impostor replaying the descriptor, a relay, or a GFS whose `base_url` differs from the pairing address — or it offers none while this household holds a proven connection under the same key; refused before `/gfs/register`, so no token or identity is sent; a 429 from `/gfs/proof` is `503 GFS_BUSY`; any other non-200 — 404 / 405 / 5xx, a redirect, a 403 / 400 — is `502 GFS_UNREACHABLE`, never `GFS_PROOF_FAILED`), `422 GFS_PAIRING_FAILED` (a missing field, an unusable GFS URL — https unless loopback / LAN — or the GFS refused the token with a 4xx). Details are the household's own plain sentences, never the GFS's words. |
| GET / POST | `/api/gfs/connections/default` | Admin. The onboarding "Connect to the GFS" step. **GET** answers from local facts only and never contacts the GFS: `{url, available, reason, connection}` — `url` is `[gfs] default_url` / `SH_GFS_DEFAULT_URL` (default `https://gfs.social-home.io`), `reason` is `null` when `available`, else `disabled` (empty `url`) or `already_connected` (`connection` then holds that row, `status` `active` / `pending`). No External URL is needed — registration sends no household address, so the step is offered in the Home Assistant App before one exists. **POST** (no body) pairs through the GFS's open sign-up: `GET /gfs/info` once, verify the signed `open_signup` capability against the key in that response (the key it then pins, TOFU — same as a QR scan; https unless loopback / LAN), `POST /gfs/signup-token`, then `POST /gfs/register` with exactly the fields QR pairing sends (`token`, `instance_id`, `public_key`, `display_name`, the key-wrap fields — never an `inbox_url`). `201` + the connection (`status` `active`, or `pending` when the GFS approves households by hand). Errors: `404 GFS_DEFAULT_DISABLED`, `409 ALREADY_CONNECTED`, `409 GFS_SIGNUP_CLOSED` (no verified capability, or the token endpoint said no), `502 GFS_UNREACHABLE`, `503 GFS_BUSY` (the GFS rate-limited the token), `422 GFS_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` (`/gfs/info` presents another public key than the pinned one — the shipped default pins the project GFS key `33cf798c…12ab0e` for the shipped URL only; an operator override pins only their own `[gfs] default_public_key` / optional `default_instance_id`), `422 GFS_PROOF_FAILED` (failed proof of possession, as for a pairing code), `422 GFS_PAIRING_FAILED` (unusable URL, or the GFS refused the registration). Details are the household's own plain sentences, never the GFS's words. |
| GET / DELETE | `/api/gfs/connections/{id}` | Inspect / disconnect. |
| POST | `/api/gfs/connections/{id}/appeal` | Appeal a ban. |
| GET | `/api/gfs/publications` | Spaces published to GFS. |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/publications` | This space's GFS publications, each with `status` (`active`/`pending`/`banned`). Admin. |
| POST / DELETE | `/api/spaces/{id}/publish/{gfs_id}` | List a **global** space on one more GFS / withdraw it from one — an edit of the owner's GFS choice. POST is the space **owner**'s alone, on the owner household (`403` otherwise, also for a household admin); it returns the publication object `{space_id, gfs_connection_id, published_at, status}`. `409 TIER_CHANGE_REQUIRED` for a **public** space (public is visible only to connected households — listing it on a GFS is making it global, a tier change), `409 SPACE_NOT_PUBLIC` for a private or household space, both refused before anything is sent; `422 GFS_SELECTION_INVALID` (`reason: "too_many"`, `max: 8`) past 8 servers; `422 GFS_PUBLISH_FAILED` for an unknown / inactive connection or a GFS that is unreachable or rejects. DELETE is open to the owner **and** a household admin (a withdrawal only narrows what a GFS learns; the admin Spaces tab uses it). A household admin's withdrawal is keyed on the publication row this household holds, so a leftover listing of a dissolved space, or of a space hosted elsewhere, can still be withdrawn; `404` when this household holds no listing of the space on that GFS (that GFS is never contacted); `422 GFS_UNPUBLISH_FAILED` when the GFS can't be reached (the row stays, and a withdrawal that must happen — the space left the global tier or was dissolved — is retried at every start and on every reconnect to that GFS once it passes the proof-of-possession gate). Members get the new signed GFS list after either. A space that leaves the global tier, or is dissolved, is withdrawn automatically from the GFSs that list it — only those are contacted. |
| GET / PUT | `/api/spaces/{id}/gfs-servers` | The GFSs a **global** space is listed on — or, for a **private** space whose owner turned `private_gfs` on (v_57), the GFSs that carry its private channel (never a listing). Space **owner** only, on the owner household (`403` otherwise). GET → `{space_type, mode, gfs_ids, max, unlisted, servers, listings, available}` (`mode`: `listing` or `channel`): the connection ids listing it, the cap (8), whether it is global but listed nowhere (the owner's signed list is empty), the members' signed list (`[{display_name, inbox_url, connected}]`), `listings` — every connection holding a listing of the space whatever its state (`[{gfs_id, display_name, inbox_url, connection_status, listing_status}]`, so one on a suspended or pending connection can still be withdrawn), and `available` — the active connections a listing can be added on (`[{gfs_id, display_name, inbox_url, listed, listing_status}]`). PUT `{gfs_ids: [...]}` replaces the set: publishes to the added servers, withdraws from the removed ones, then sends members the new list once → `{listed, failed: [{gfs_id, reason}]}` (`publish_failed` / `unpublish_failed`; one unreachable GFS never aborts the rest). `gfs_ids` is required — a missing or `null` list is `422 GFS_SELECTION_INVALID` (`reason: "malformed"`), never "none"; an id that is not an active connection is `reason: "unknown"`, more than 8 `reason: "too_many"`. Servers for a public space → `409 TIER_CHANGE_REQUIRED`; an empty list on a space that is no longer global only withdraws. No quorum: the tier change is proposal-gated, the servers of a global space are the owner's call. **Channel mode** (private + `private_gfs`): `gfs_ids` are the chosen channel servers, `listings` is empty, `unlisted` is `false`, every `available` row carries `channel_capable`; PUT signs the choice into the members' list, then registers the channel on exactly those servers (withdrawing it from the others) and re-grants the members → `{listed, failed: [{gfs_id, reason: "channel_failed"}]}`; a server that doesn't prove `private_channels` is `422 GFS_SELECTION_INVALID` (`reason: "unsupported"`); a private space with the option off is `409 SPACE_NOT_PUBLIC`. `GET /api/spaces/{id}` shows such a space's channel servers in `gfs_servers` (read-only, members). |
| GET | `/api/public_spaces` | Aggregated directory. A pinned global space carries its `lat` / `lon` (the owner's place pin as its GFS lists it, re-validated to 4 dp; `null` otherwise) and `radius_km` — the owner's radius **hint** beside the pin, or `null` — the SPA sorts by the distance to this household's home and compares it with the hint, both in the browser, and sends that home nowhere. Each row carries the host's real `join_mode` (how to become a posting member) **and** `allow_subscribers` (whether the content is publicly readable at all) — both normalised fail-closed, so an older connection server that reports neither reads as `invite_only` + not-readable and the SPA offers no Subscribe button. `host_display_name` is the host household's name as a pairing told us, or `null` when we hold none — a GFS directory carries no host name (the GFS shields households from each other), so an unpaired host is `null`, never its instance id; the SPA then leaves the host out. `host_is_paired` is whether that host is a confirmed peer. |
| POST | `/api/public_spaces/refresh` | Force-poll GFS. |
| POST | `/api/public_spaces/{space_id}/join-request` | Ask to join. Body `{host_instance_id, message?, share_location?}` → `202 {request_id}`. `share_location: true` — the person agreed (the SPA asks before a join to a [local community](./protocol/local-communities.md)): the `SPACE_JOIN_REQUEST` carries this household's home rounded to ~1 km (`join_location`), inside its encrypted payload, to the host only (`403 HOME_NOT_SET` when this household has none). Without it nothing about the home is sent, and a local community's host drops the request. |
| POST | `/api/public_spaces/{space_id}/hide` | Hide locally. |
| POST / DELETE | `/api/public_spaces/blocked_instances/{id}` | Block a GFS. |
| GET | `/api/peer_spaces` | Spaces advertised by directly-paired peers. Each row: `{space_id, host_instance_id, host_display_name, host_is_paired, name, description, emoji, member_count, join_mode, min_age, category, local_community}` — `local_community` is a [local community](./protocol/local-communities.md)'s `{radius_km}` (from a v_59 host) or `null`: "within X km", never a centre (it stays on the host) and no verdict — the host decides on the ~1 km location a join carries, with the person's consent. `host_display_name` is `null` for a peer whose pairing carried no name — never its instance id. |

**Public-Momentum (§Momentum-public)**

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET / POST | `/api/moments/public/registrations` | List / opt this user into the directory on a GFS. `POST` body `{gfs_id, default_share?}`: without `default_share` a new registration doesn't make new moments public by default (each moment is public only when its author switches it on) and a re-registration keeps its setting. Each row carries `delivery` (v_62): `"encrypted"` — public moments go through this GFS, encrypted from it — or `"unsupported_server"` — the GFS has not proved `encrypted_moments` in its signed `/gfs/info` block, so no public moment is sent through it (never in plaintext) — and `followers_unable`: follower households there on an older Social Home (they declared no moment suites and are sent none), from the GFS's signed `follower_stats` counts; `null` when unknown or not encrypted. |
| DELETE / PATCH | `/api/moments/public/registrations/{gfs_id}` | Deregister / flip `default_share`. |
| GET / POST | `/api/moments/public/follows` | List / follow public author. Body `{gfs_id, followed_user_id}`. The GFS learns the follow; the SPA offers it only for someone known through that GFS alone (Discover, a moment that came through it) — a paired person gets the household follow instead (decision 7). |
| DELETE | `/api/moments/public/follows/{gfs_id}/{user_id}` | Unfollow. |
| POST | `/api/moments/{id}/share-link` | The author's per-moment share link for their own public moment. Optional body `{gfs_id}` (a string; `400` otherwise). Uploads the 4 KB sealed share copy to that GFS on first use — and the moment's encrypted file, if that GFS didn't take it at publish — and returns `{url}` — `{viewer}/v/<hash>/#g=<GFS>&a=<author>&m=<moment>&b=<share blob>&k=<item key>`, the key in the fragment only. `404` for an unknown, someone else's or non-public moment, and for a reply whose parent isn't public; `409 SHARE_LINK_UNAVAILABLE` when no viewer address is set, no registered GFS keeps encrypted media, the file is still processing, or the GFS refused an upload. See `docs/protocol/momentum_public.md`, "Share links". |
| GET | `/api/gfs/{gfs_id}/moments/users` | Proxy GFS directory; passes `?q=<substr>` through (Momentum › Discover sends none: it filters in the browser, so the GFS never sees a search). Each row's `picture_url` is replaced with our own signed picture-proxy URL (or `null` without a mirrored picture) — the household's self-reported URL never reaches the browser. |
| GET | `/api/gfs/{gfs_id}/moments/users/{user_id}/picture` | Proxy GFS-mirrored avatar bytes (used by Discover cards). Accepts the `?exp=&sig=` signed URL from the directory response. |

## HFS — Child protection

`/api/cp/*` — see `socialhome/routes/child_protection.py`.
Guardian-scoped operations: manage guardians, list minor's spaces and
conversations, set age gates, read audit logs. All require the minor
or their guardian (household admins have an override).

### Protected accounts (§CP.R)

A household admin places a user under child protection
(`POST /api/cp/users/{username}/protection`). On top of the space age gate
(§CP.F1), guardian blocks (§CP.F2) and direct-pair-only DMs (§CP.F3), the
**server** refuses a fixed set of surfaces for that account — every refusal
is `403 {"error": {"code": "ACCOUNT_PROTECTED", "capability": "<id>", "detail": …}}`,
enforced in the owning service (so every route and API client gets the same
answer), and lifted the moment protection is disabled:

| Capability | Refused | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `bazaar` | `POST /api/bazaar`, `POST /api/bazaar/{id}/bids`, `POST /api/bazaar/{id}/offers`, `POST /api/bazaar/{id}/ask` and every message in an "Ask the seller" thread; accepting an offer or bid on the account's own listing. Another household never reaches a protected seller through Ask: its question is dropped on arrival | Trading means money and contact with other households. Browsing and saving listings stay open. |
| `public_spaces` | `POST /api/spaces` with `space_type` `public` / `global`; proposing or approving `set_public_tier` → `public` / `global` | Public / global spaces are advertised to peers and connection servers with the owner's name. Private / household spaces stay open; joining public spaces keeps its age gate. |
| `public_moments` | `POST /api/moments/public/registrations`, `POST /api/moments/public/follows` | Lists the account in a connection server's public directory / announces it to a stranger as a follower. |
| `public_links` | `POST /api/highlights/{id}/publish` | Puts a highlight on the open web. |
| `api_tokens` | `POST /api/me/tokens` | A personal token is a bearer credential an external tool holds. Signing in is unaffected (browser sessions mint through the platform adapter). |
| `calendar_feeds` | `POST /api/spaces/{id}/calendar/feed-token`; serving an existing feed token | The feed URL is its own credential and exposes the account's schedule to whoever holds it. |
| `ha_automations` | `PATCH /api/me` with `preferences.ha_automation_events: true`; an opt-in stored before protection is dropped, and the event bridge skips the account (every personal `socialhome.*` event) whatever is stored | Home Assistant keeps fired events in its database and shows them to anyone with HA admin, logbook or automation access. |

**What it sees.** Moments come only from the account's own household and
its directly paired households (the circle its own moments reach): one
relayed from 2–3 hops away or delivered through a GFS is left out of every
`/api/moments*` read (inbox, archive, detail — the unknown-id `404` —,
replies and their count, hashtags, saved) and its WebSocket frames. See
`docs/protocol/momentum.md` → *Protected accounts*.

**What was set up before.** Turning protection on also closes what the
account had already opened to the outside. Everything is revoked or closed,
not suspended — lifting protection restores the capabilities, not the old
links:

- personal API tokens are revoked (a password sign-in — a token the platform
  login minted — keeps working);
- public highlight links are unpublished (the local flag clears even when the
  connection server is unreachable, so the link stops streaming at once);
- public-moment directory registrations and public follows are removed,
  and the account's earlier public moments are never streamed to a viewer
  again (even if a connection server still lists them);
- active bazaar listings end as `cancelled` (an existing, federated state
  every viewer already shows as closed); no offer or bid on the account's
  listings can be accepted, and nothing is sold *to* the account — its
  earlier offers can't be accepted (422 `This offer can no longer be
  accepted.`) and its bid never wins an auction;
- `public` / `global` spaces the account owns go `private` (through the
  normal federated config change, which also unpublishes a global space);
- earlier calendar feed tokens stop serving (401).

A step that fails is logged and the admin's request answers 422 (`Protection
is on, but some earlier sharing could not be turned off yet. Try again.`),
so it can be retried (every step is idempotent); protection itself is on
either way.

**Spaces a protected account owns** never go `public` / `global`, whoever
asks — an adult admin's proposal (403, detail `This space can't be made
public.`), a quorum, an open proposal from before protection (it is
rejected on the next vote), or a direct config change. A `public` / `global`
space is not handed to a protected account either (`POST
/api/spaces/{id}/ownership` → 403; make the space private first). The wording
never says why.

**Guardian blocks (§CP.F2)** — `POST /api/cp/minors/{minor_id}/blocks/{blocked_id}`
— apply like a personal block, in both directions, while the account is
protected: the two can't open or continue a 1:1 DM, share a group DM
(creating or adding refuses; the block steps the account out of every group
they share), react to each other, or call; an existing 1:1 drops out of both
lists (and a withheld group message never counts as unread for the
account); typing indicators never cross; their moments and highlights are
hidden from each other — and the account's own moments and highlights are
not sent to a household where someone it has blocked lives (the moment
3-hop relay mesh can still carry a moment there through a third household,
which knows nothing of the block); their statuses, whereabouts and online
state are hidden from each other (`/api/friends`, `/api/presence`, the
status in `/api/users`, and the matching `user.*` / `presence.updated`
frames) — and the account's status and online state are not sent to a
household where someone it has blocked lives (when the block is added, that
household sees the status cleared and the account go offline, once); that
household also gets the account's name only — its picture and bio go out
empty, at once when the block is added, and on every later profile update
or pairing roster — and the full profile, the current status and the online
state go back there when the block is removed or protection is turned off;
on this household the two see each other by name only, either way round —
no picture and no bio in `/api/users`, a space or conversation roster, the
follows list or the calendar invite picker, 404 on their picture, and no pin
on a space map or the `/api/me/corner` presence slice, and no
`space.member.profile_updated` frame about the other (when the block is
added each side's open session gets one name-only `user.profile_updated`
about the other, and the full one when it is removed);
and a blocked person's space posts,
household posts and comments, @mentions, @here, DMs,
follows, moment replies and reactions never notify the
account. Refusals are 403: the blocked person sees exactly a
personal block's `Recipient has you blocked.`, the protected account `You
can't message this person.` / `You can't call this person.`, and a group
that can't hold both `One of these people can't be added to this group.` —
none says why. The block never
leaves the household; inbound DMs, history sync, media blobs and call offers
from a blocked remote person are refused on arrival (see
`docs/protocol/dm.md` → *Guardian blocks*).

The account learns *that* it is protected and *what* is limited from
`GET /api/me` (`protected`, `restrictions`) and `GET /api/me/protection`
(plus its guardians) — never its recorded age. Nobody else learns who is
protected: the `cp.*` WebSocket frames (protection on/off, guardian and
block changes) reach only household admins, the account itself and its
guardians, and carry no age. The account itself also gets
`me.protection_changed` (no data) on each of those changes, and its SPA
reloads `/api/me` + `/api/me/protection` straight away.
Remote households never learn protection state, `space_session` peers can't
send DMs at all (§24.11 peer class), relayed DMs from a protected sender are
already refused (§CP.F3), and remote invites above a space's age gate are
refused on accept.

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
