| GET | `/api/cp/protection` | **Admin-only.** Protection status for every household user — `{users: [{user_id, username, is_minor, declared_age}]}`. `is_minor`/`declared_age` are `SENSITIVE_FIELDS` stripped from `/api/users`; this admin-gated endpoint is their only surface (powers the admin panel's "Protected" column). |
| GET | `/api/cp/spaces/{space_id}/age-gate` | `{min_age}` of a space. A household admin (who sets it with `PATCH`, admin-only) or a caller with a seat in the space reads it; anyone else gets the space gate's `403 "Not a space member."` — for a real id and an unknown one alike, so it is no oracle for which spaces exist or how they are gated. |

Two distinct audit surfaces:

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/cp/minors/{minor_id}/audit-log` | Guardian-driven actions (enable/disable CP, add/remove guardians, toggle blocks). |
| GET | `/api/cp/minors/{minor_id}/membership-audit` | System-driven space-membership changes affecting the minor — \`joined\` / \`removed\` / \`blocked\`. Written automatically by `SpaceService.add_member` / `remove_member` / `ban` when the target user has child-protection enabled. |

## HFS — Reports

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/reports` | File a report (`{target_type, target_id, category, notes?, forward_gfs?, space_id?}`). `target_type` ∈ `post, comment, page, task, sticky, calendar_event, gallery_item, user, space, highlight, moment`. Content inside a space is **space-scoped** automatically (the space is derived from the target); `space_id` names the space for a member report (`user`) and, for content, must match it. A target this household does not hold is 404 and nothing is stored; a space-scoped report needs the reporter to be a member — otherwise the SAME 404 (no existence oracle); a `space` report needs a known space the reporter is in (or a public / global one). `notes` ≤ 1000 characters (422 over). 429 when the reporter's daily cap (20), their pending reports in that space (20) or the space's queue (500) is full; `space_id` on `space` / `highlight` / `moment` → 422. Response `{id, status, federated, forwarded_to_gfs, space_id}` — `forwarded_to_gfs` is `true` only when the report actually goes to an active GFS (never for private-space or feed content; the GFS never gets the reporter or the notes) — `space_id` set means the space's moderators (not household admins) triage it. |
| GET | `/api/admin/reports` | Household admin queue — pending **household-level** reports only (never a space's): a plain list of `id, target_type, target_id, reporter_user_id, reporter_instance_id, category, notes, status, created_at, resolved_by, resolved_at, space_id` (`space_id` always `null` here) plus `reporter_name` and, for a member report, `target_name` — the name this household holds (`users` / `remote_users`), `null` when unknown. |
| POST | `/api/admin/reports/{id}/resolve` | Resolve a household-level report; body `{"dismissed": true}` dismisses it instead. A space report id → 404. |
| GET | `/api/spaces/{id}/reports` | The space's pending reports, for its owner / admins / moderators (403 for anyone else — a plain member, or a household admin without a seat; 404 unknown space). A report about the viewer themself — a member report on them, or a report on an item they authored — is left out, except for the space owner when nobody else holds content authority: then the row is `anonymous: true` (reporter fields `null`) and `dismiss_only: true`. Rows as above plus `reporter_name` (the name this household holds for the person — their per-space name, else their household's (`users` / `remote_users`), else the space's roster seat (kept after they left or were banned) — `null` when nobody here knows them, never the id), `target_preview` (a ≤160-char plain-text glimpse of the item — text / title / caption — or `null`), `target_gone` (the item was deleted, or the member left) and, for a member report, `target_name`. |
| POST | `/api/spaces/{id}/reports/{report_id}/resolve` | Content authority marks one of the space's reports resolved (`{"dismissed": true}` dismisses). 403 as above; a report of another space, or about the caller themself → 404 (the sole-authority owner may dismiss one about themself; resolving it → 403); already decided → 409. The decision is synced to the other reviewer households (`SPACE_REPORT_DECIDED`); a reporter on another household is told it was reviewed by the host (`SPACE_REPORT_ANSWERED`, v_71 — no verdict, no moderator). |

## HFS — Bot-bridge (Home Assistant → Social Home)

Lets HA automations post into spaces and DMs via HTTP. See
[protocol/bot-bridge.md](./protocol/bot-bridge.md) if present; the
CRUD surface for the bot personas themselves is under
**Spaces → Bot personas** above.

| Method | Path | Auth | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/bot-bridge/spaces/{space_id}` | **Per-bot** Bearer token (from `POST /api/spaces/{id}/bots`). User API tokens are rejected. | Post as the SpaceBot the token was issued to. Body: `{title?, message}`. Fails 403 when `space.bot_enabled=false`. The token outlives its maker's seat, so every post re-checks the maker: a bot whose maker is **banned** from the space posts nothing, and a personal (`scope="member"`) bot posts only while its maker holds a member seat (not after they left, not while they only follow) — otherwise `403 FORBIDDEN "This bot may not post in this space."`. A space-scope bot whose maker left keeps posting (the admins rotate or delete it). |
| POST | `/api/bot-bridge/conversations/{conversation_id}` | User Bearer token. | Post a system message into a DM. Fails 403 when `conversation.bot_enabled=false`. |

Both endpoints reject requests carrying `X-Ingress-User` (403) so a
UI-authenticated user cannot impersonate the integration.

## HFS — HA integration bridge

Pushed to by the companion `socialhome` Home Assistant integration. The
integration resolves the externally-reachable URL inside HA
(`external_url` or Nabu Casa Remote UI) and mirrors it here so the addon
can stamp it into new pairing QRs + fan out `URL_UPDATED` to
already-paired peers. Admin Bearer auth (the integration holds the
auto-provisioned token written to `<data_dir>/integration_token.txt`).

There is **no separate download step** under the add-on: `HaBootstrap`
pushes a Supervisor discovery entry on every boot
(`platform/haos/bootstrap.py`, `platform/haos/supervisor.py` →
`POST /discovery`) advertising the add-on's host, port and integration
token, so Home Assistant surfaces the integration for setup on its own.

The integration's value is stored separately from the admin-set one
(`instance_config['ha_federation_base']` vs `['federation_base_url']`)
because they mean different things: the integration pushes *Home
Assistant's* URL and the adapter appends `/api/socialhome/inbox` (an
HA-hosted view forwarding into the add-on), whereas an admin-entered value
is the address Social Home is reachable at directly and gets Social Home's
own `/federation/inbox`. Conflating them would yield an unreachable URL for
one source or the other. An admin-set value wins when present, so typing
one is never a silent no-op; clearing it returns control to the
integration.

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/ha/integration/federation-base` | Current base the addon advertises. Returns `{"base": string \| null}`. |
| PUT | `/api/ha/integration/federation-base` | Upsert `{"base": "https://..."}`. Validates scheme (http/https) and strips trailing slash. When the adapter's **effective** inbox base changes (the pushed URL + `/api/socialhome/inbox`; an admin-set base wins over it), fans out `URL_UPDATED` with that effective base to every confirmed paired peer (never a link-joined household). Returns `{ok, base, changed, peers_notified}` — `changed` is about the stored pushed value. |

### WebRTC ICE servers are pulled, not pushed

There is **no** `/api/ha/integration/ice-servers` endpoint. Earlier revisions of
this page documented a `GET` and a `PUT` there; both were removed and now 404.

Social Home pulls the list itself: `HaIceServerSync`
(`platform/ha/ice_servers_sync.py`) issues the `web_rtc/ice_servers` command
over the **HA Core WebSocket**, once shortly after boot and then every 24 h
(60 s retry after a failure), and applies the result via
`FederationService.set_ice_servers`. Wired for both `ha` and `haos` in each
adapter's `on_startup`.

The push was replaced because the integration's listener only fired on YAML
reloads — so a freshly-rotated Nabu Casa Cloud TURN credential could stay
invisible for hours — and because a failed push left no record on the Social
Home side.

Consequences worth knowing:

* The pulled list **replaces** the config-derived one
  (`webrtc_stun_url` / `webrtc_turn_url` / …) for the federation transport. A
  reply with nothing usable in it is ignored rather than applied, so HA without
  the WebRTC integration cannot blank out an operator's servers.
* The **first fetch attempt** releases the federation transport's
  first-handshake gate, whatever its outcome — list applied, nothing usable,
  or the fetch failed outright. The transport holds its first outbound
  handshake for up to `ICE_PRIME_TIMEOUT_S` (15 s) waiting for that signal, so
  the boot-time outbox drain doesn't build every peer STUN-only just before the
  TURN credentials land; the bound is what stops an unreachable HA Core from
  stalling federation. Platforms that never pull (standalone) release the gate
  at startup instead — `PlatformAdapter.provides_ice_servers`.
* A list arriving **after** peers were built still reaches them. A content
  change bumps the transport's ICE generation, and any peer that has not yet
  opened its DataChannel is retired and rebuilt under the new list on its next
  send. Connected peers are untouched. Every push also clears the per-peer RTC
  retry suppressions, changed or not.
* It reaches the **federation** transport only. The SPA's own
  `/api/webrtc/ice_servers` and the public highlight/moment signalling paths
  still serve the config-derived list, so under HA those can differ.

## HFS — Apps

Admin-installed embedded JS apps from the Social Home Apps catalog.
The catalog is fetched from the `socialhome-apps` GitHub releases
(`apps_catalog_url` / `SH_APPS_CATALOG_URL`). On install the bundle
tarball is downloaded, its `sha256` verified against the catalog, and
unpacked (path-traversal-guarded) under `apps_path/<app_id>/<version>/`
(`apps_path` / `SH_APPS_PATH`, default `<data_dir>/apps`). A bundle is
bounded: at most 8 MiB downloaded, 32 MiB / 256 files unpacked, and a
60 s timeout for the download (`socialhome/domain/apps.py`,
`RELEASE_TIMEOUT_S`). An empty `apps_catalog_url` turns the catalog off:
browse answers `enabled: false`, update checks find nothing, installs are
refused with `409 APP_CATALOG_DISABLED`.

| Method | Path | Auth | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/apps` | Any member | List installed apps. Non-admins see enabled apps only; admins see all. Age-restricted apps (those with `min_age > 0`) are filtered out server-side for protected minors — the SPA never performs client-side age filtering. Each entry includes `min_age` (0/13/16/18; 0 = no restriction), `permissions` (the plain-word ones the household agreed to, from the installed manifest, in the order `storage` / `people` / `federation`; `people` is implied by `federation`), `installed_at` and `installed_by_name` ("Added by Anna"; `null` when that person is gone). |
| GET | `/api/apps/catalog` | Any member | Browse the remote app catalog (fetches `catalog.json` from the configured release URL, cached 24 h; `?refresh=1` re-fetches — admins only, ignored for anyone else). Members see it to ask for an app (D4); a protected account doesn't see apps whose catalog `min_age` is above its age (D5). Returns `{enabled, source_host, apps: [...], checked_at, stale}`. `source_host` is `null` for Social Home's own app list (the SPA says "From Social Home") and the host otherwise ("From apps.example.org, set by your server operator", D6). Each app carries the catalog's v1 fields (`app_id, name, latest_version, description, icon_url, capabilities`) plus the schema-2 ones, `null` / empty when the catalog doesn't say: `i18n` (`{locale: {name, description}}`, the SPA picks the viewer's language), `permissions`, `bundle_size` (bytes), `min_age`, `languages`, `publisher`, `homepage` (text, never fetched), `min_core_version`, `changelog` (`[{version, date, notes: {locale: text}}]`, newest first, at most 5). Receiver bounds: [protocol/apps.md](./protocol/apps.md#catalog-schema-v2). `compatible` is `false` when the app needs a newer Social Home (its `min_core_version` is past this one, or it asks for a permission this one doesn't know) — the SPA says "Needs a newer Social Home" and offers no install. `requested` is `true` when the caller asked for it and nobody has answered yet. The rest: `enabled` is `false` (and `apps` empty) when the operator turned the catalog off with an empty `apps_catalog_url`. `checked_at` is when the catalog last answered (UTC ISO 8601, `null` if it hasn't since start) and `stale` is `true` when the latest try to reach it failed (network, HTTP, timeout or a body that isn't a catalog): the server then serves the last list it held instead of an error, and doesn't try the network again for 5 min unless refreshed. With nothing held, 503 (`APP_CATALOG_UNREACHABLE`) — never a 500. |
| POST | `/api/apps` | Admin | Install an app from the catalog. Body: `{app_id}`. 201 on success; 400 when `app_id` is missing or not a string; 404 if the app is not in the catalog; 409 if already installed, `APP_CATALOG_DISABLED` when the catalog is turned off, or `APP_NEEDS_NEWER_SOCIAL_HOME` (`params.min_core_version`) when the app needs a newer Social Home (see `compatible` above) — also (`params.capabilities`) when the downloaded bundle's manifest names a capability this one doesn't know, checked again after the download so the catalog can't vouch for it; the install's age gate (`min_age`) starts at the catalog's rating (or the bundle manifest's, whichever is stricter), which an admin can change with `PATCH`; 400 (`UNPROCESSABLE`) on a failed sha256 / manifest / path check; 422 (`APP_BUNDLE_TOO_LARGE`) past a bundle limit; 502 (`APP_DOWNLOAD_FAILED`) when the bundle can't be downloaded (timeout, network or HTTP error). |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}` | Any member | One installed app. 404 when missing or disabled (non-admin). |
| PATCH | `/api/apps/{app_id}` | Admin | Update app settings. Body: `{enabled}` and/or `{min_age}`. `min_age` must be one of `0/13/16/18` (0 = no restriction). |
| DELETE | `/api/apps/{app_id}` | Admin | Uninstall an app — removes the bundle from disk; cascades all per-user `app_kv` rows and every session row and held message. Every household a person here had a game or open invite with is told (D9): `APP_SESSION {verb: "close", reason: "ended", sessions: [...]}`, one per household (v_72), or the `declined` / `withdrawn` close for each pending invite to an older one — see [protocol/apps.md](./protocol/apps.md#uninstall-tells-opponents-v_72). |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/uninstall-preview` | Admin | What uninstalling loses (D9), for the uninstall dialog: `{people, games, invites, told, told_more, untold}` — people here with saved data (`app_kv`), games being played and open invites (either way) with people in **other** households, up to three of those people by the name the admin sees anywhere else (`"Ben · Familie Okafor"`; `told_more` counts the rest), and `untold`, games with a household below v_72 that won't hear about it. Counts and names only — never game contents nor who here plays whom. 403 for members, 404 when the app is not installed. |
| GET | `/api/apps/updates` | Any member | List installed apps that have a newer version in the catalog. Returns `{"updates": [{app_id, name, current_version, latest_version, compatible, new_permissions, changelog}], checked_at, stale}` — `compatible: false` when the new version needs a newer Social Home (updating it answers 409 `APP_NEEDS_NEWER_SOCIAL_HOME`); `new_permissions` are the plain-word permissions (`storage` / `people` / `federation`) the new version's catalog entry asks for beyond what the installed one has (empty: same or fewer — the update needs no new yes); `changelog` is "What's new": the catalog's `changelog` entries newer than the installed version and no newer than `latest_version`, newest first, each `{version, date, notes}` with `notes` per locale (the SPA picks the viewer's language). Only a strictly newer version counts: versions compare as numbers (CalVer `YYYY.M.D[.N]`, so `2026.6.4 < 2026.10.28 < 2026.10.28.1`), and a catalog that went back to an older release offers no update; a version either side can't parse never counts. `checked_at` is when the catalog last answered (UTC ISO 8601, `null` if it hasn't since start) and `stale` is `true` when the latest try to reach it failed (network, HTTP, timeout or a body that isn't a catalog): the server then serves the last list it held instead of an error, and doesn't try the network again for 5 min unless refreshed. With nothing held, 503 (`APP_CATALOG_UNREACHABLE`) — never a 500. The SPA says "All apps are up to date" only when `stale` is `false`. The catalog is cached server-side for 24 h: within that window every caller gets the cached result, and the first call after it expires (from any member) fetches the catalog again (a background check also runs daily and turns a newer compatible version into one `app_update` **Needs you** row per admin per app — see `GET /api/notifications/needs-you`; nothing updates on its own). `?refresh=1` bypasses the cache but is **admin-only** — for a non-admin the parameter is ignored and the call behaves as without it. `[]` when the catalog is turned off. |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/update` | Admin | Pull and install the latest catalog version of the app. Body (optional): `{"accept_permissions": ["people", "federation"]}` — the new permissions the admin said yes to (400 `UNPROCESSABLE` when it isn't a list of strings). Returns the serialised `InstalledApp` (`{app_id, name, version, enabled, capabilities, icon}`) on success. Always re-fetches the catalog. When the new version asks for permissions beyond the installed ones (its catalog `permissions`, checked before downloading, and again its downloaded `manifest.json` — the bundle is what runs) that `accept_permissions` doesn't cover: **409 `APP_NEW_PERMISSIONS`** with `params.permissions` naming only the new ones, comma-separated (`"people,federation"`), and the installed version stays (a refused bundle is removed). Same or fewer permissions update straight away. Older versions are never installed (no rollback, D8). Success resolves every admin's `app_update` row for the app ("Handled by …"). When the installed version is already the latest — or newer than the catalog's (no downgrade, see `/api/apps/updates`) — or the catalog is turned off, it returns **200 with the unchanged row** — not an error. 503 (`APP_CATALOG_UNREACHABLE`) when the catalog can't be reached and none is held. 404 if the app is not installed or no longer in the catalog; 400 (`UNPROCESSABLE`) if the integrity check fails; 422 / 502 for an oversized or undownloadable bundle, and 409 `APP_NEEDS_NEWER_SOCIAL_HOME`, as for install (the installed version stays). |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/requests` | A member (not an admin) | "Ask an admin" for a catalog app (D4). 202 `{requested: true}`. The request goes to the household's admins — for a protected account to its guardians (the admins when it has none) — as one **Needs you** row each, `app_request`, about `app:<app_id>`, opening `/apps/<app_id>/about`; every ask for one app joins one row ("Milo and Kiki asked for Chess"), and asking again while it is unread is silent. There is no table: adding the app resolves the rows (`approved`, by the installer) and so does `DELETE`. An app the household has but an admin turned off can be asked for too (D10, "ask to turn it on" from a waiting invite); turning it back on resolves the rows (`approved`). 409 `APP_REQUEST_NOT_NEEDED` for an admin or an app already in the household and turned on; 404 for an app the catalog doesn't have or that is rated above the caller's age (for a turned-off app, also above the household's own age gate for it). |
| DELETE | `/api/apps/{app_id}/requests` | Admin | "Not now": every open request for the app resolves (`declined`) for all who got it. |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/runtime` | Any member (bearer) | Launch payload for a running app. Returns `{app_id, name, entry_url, self_user_id, capabilities, permissions}` where `entry_url` is a short-lived signed bundle URL and `permissions` is what the SPA's bridge offers the app (`storage` / `people` / `federation`, see *App permissions* below). 404 (`NOT_FOUND`) if the app is not installed; 403 `APP_NOT_ENABLED` (with the app's `name` in the error object) if an admin turned it off; 403 `APP_AGE_RESTRICTED` if the caller is a protected minor below the app's `min_age`. The SPA's app host shows a different screen for each. |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/bundle/{tail}` | Signed URL / cookie (no bearer) | Serve bundle static files. The `entry_url` carries a media-signer signature over the bundle prefix as `?exp=&sig=` query parameters; on first access those are exchanged for a short-lived HttpOnly path-scoped cookie so relative sub-resources load without re-signing. Every response carries a strict CSP (`connect-src 'none'`, `worker-src 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'self'`, etc.) ending in `sandbox allow-scripts` — the `AppHost` iframe's exact flags, so a bundle URL opened as a top-level tab still runs in an opaque origin and can't read the SPA's token — plus `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. Path traversal is guarded. Re-checks that the app is enabled on every request. |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/store` | Any member | List the caller's per-user KV entries for this app. Returns `{"items": {key: value}}`. |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/store/{key}` | Any member | Read one KV entry. Returns `{"key", "value"}`. 404 if the key does not exist. |
| PUT | `/api/apps/{app_id}/store/{key}` | Any member | Upsert a KV entry. Body: `{"value": <any JSON>}`. 413 if quota exceeded (500 keys / user, 64 KiB per value, 256-char key). 403 `APP_NOT_ENABLED` if the app is disabled, `APP_AGE_RESTRICTED` for a protected minor below `min_age`. 404 if the app is not installed. |
| DELETE | `/api/apps/{app_id}/store/{key}` | Any member | Delete one KV entry. Returns `{"status": "ok"}`. `store` calls have their own rate-limit bucket (300 / min / user). |

**App permissions.** An app gets only what the household agreed to when
it was installed: `storage` (saves its own data), `people` (lets you pick
people) and `federation` (sends to other households; implies `people`), read
from the installed manifest's `capabilities`. `GET /api/apps`,
`GET /api/apps/{app_id}` and `/runtime` list them as `permissions`. Every route
an app reaches through the bridge checks its permission and answers
`403 APP_PERMISSION_DENIED` (`params.permission`) without it:

| Route | Permission |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/apps/{app_id}/store`, `GET/PUT/DELETE …/store/{key}` | `storage` |
| `GET …/contacts` | `people` |
| `GET …/peers` | `federation` |
| `POST …/sessions`, `POST …/messages`, `POST …/sessions/{session_id}/cancel` | `people`; `federation` too when `target` is in another household |
| `POST …/sessions/{session_id}/accept`, `POST …/sessions/{session_id}/decline`, `POST …/held` | `people` |

A manifest naming a capability this Social Home doesn't know is refused at
install / update with `409 APP_NEEDS_NEWER_SOCIAL_HOME` (`params.capabilities`).
See [protocol/apps.md](./protocol/apps.md#permissions).

**App federation (cross-household, capability v_17+)**

These endpoints let an installed app exchange state with the same app running
in a paired household.  All require a valid member bearer token and that the
app is installed and enabled; they return 404 / 403 otherwise.  The wire
transport (binary `fed-app-v1` DataChannel or `APP_MESSAGE` JSON fallback) is
selected transparently by the server.  See [protocol/apps.md](./protocol/apps.md).

| Method | Path | Auth | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/peers` | Any member | List confirmed peer instances as `[{instance_id, display_name}]`. The SPA uses this to populate the peer picker when starting a cross-household app session. |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/contacts` | Any member (`people`) | The people the **app** may know about (D3): who the caller picked in Social Home's invite sheet and who they play with in this app — the other side of every session this household knows for them (a sent invite, a game taken in with Play), within the 14-day TTL — never the roster, never an inviter still waiting in Needs you. `{contacts: [{instance_id, user_ref, display_name, is_local, online, person_name, household_name}]}`; for someone in another household `display_name` names it (`"Ben · Familie Okafor"`), `person_name` is the bare name and `household_name` the household's (`null` for your own). Filtered by personal and guardian blocks, the DM rule for a protected caller (§CP.F3), and people of other households only with `federation`. `online` is `true` for locally-connected users; always `false` for remote ones. |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/invitable` | Any member (`people`) | Social Home's invite sheet (D3/D5) — the SPA host reads it; the app's bridge never offers it. `{people: [...same shape as /contacts], invites: {open, max_open, today, max_per_day}}` — each person also carries `groups` (their household runs v_76 or later, so they can sit at a group table; always `true` for people of this household); `invites` counts the people tables invited too. Local members (minus the caller and anyone below the app's age rating) and known people of paired households (only with `federation`), minus personal and guardian blocks; a **protected** caller sees only people they can already message (the DM rule: households their own household paired with directly). `invites` is what the caps leave (see `POST /sessions`). |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/sessions` | Any member | Send one game invite to a person picked in Social Home's invite sheet (D3) — the sheet's Send calls it; the app's bridge has no way to. Body: `{target: {instance_id, user_ref, is_local}}`. Returns `{session_id}` (`201`). Sends an `APP_SESSION {verb:"open"}` event; includes `to_user`/`from_user` when the peer is v_18+. Local targets get a direct WS loopback to the target only and an `AppChallengeReceived` notification. `403 FORBIDDEN` when `target` is not in the caller's `/invitable` list; `429 APP_INVITES_OPEN_LIMIT` at 10 invites nobody has answered yet and `429 APP_INVITES_DAILY_LIMIT` at 30 new invites in 24 hours, per person and app (`params.limit`); `400` for a household-addressed `{peer_instance_id}` body (no longer accepted). |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/sessions/{session_id}/accept` | The invitee (409 `APP_GROUP_SESSION` for a group table: answered under `/groups`) | Play one game invite ("Play" in Needs you, D12): only that invite is taken in — every other one keeps waiting — and its row resolves (`accepted`). Removed in one step (`409 APP_CHALLENGE_HANDLED` when it no longer waits: played on another device, declined, withdrawn; also for one past its 14 days, row `expired`, or one from someone a guardian block separates from the caller since, row `done`). Returns `{session: {session_id, from_instance, from_user, payload}}`, which the SPA's app host hands to the app as its launch context (`app.context` → `launch`, see [protocol/apps.md](./protocol/apps.md#launch-context)). Opening an app accepts nothing, and a message in a waiting invite (`POST …/messages`) is refused (`403 APP_SESSION_UNKNOWN`, D3): only Play takes it in. Also `done` for one from a household a protected caller can't message (D5). Replaces the former blanket `GET /api/apps/{app_id}/pending-sessions` drain. |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/sessions/{session_id}/decline` | The invitee | Decline a game invite that waits for the caller: removed in one step (`409 APP_CHALLENGE_HANDLED` when it no longer waits), its row resolves (`declined`), and the challenger's app hears `{verb: "close", reason: "declined"}` — over WebSocket on this household, else as an `APP_SESSION` to theirs (v_18+; best effort, the decline stands). An invite that waits for its app (D10, `app_challenge_waiting`) is declined here too, without the app installed or on and without its permission check: its row resolves (`declined`) and **nothing** is sent — the inviter's invite runs out after 14 days. |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/sessions/{session_id}/cancel` | The challenger | Withdraw an invite. Body `{target}` as for opening it (`400` without one; `403 APP_SESSION_UNKNOWN` unless it is a game this household knows the caller plays with that person). An invite the caller sent is marked withdrawn: it stops counting as open (still counts for the day) and takes no more messages. A local invitee's invite stops waiting (only one this caller opened) and their row resolves (`withdrawn`); a remote invitee's household gets `APP_SESSION {verb: "close", reason: "withdrawn"}` and does the same. The SPA bridge offers it to apps as `app.cancelSession({sessionId, target})`. |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/sessions/{session_id}/continue` | Any member (`people`) | Pick a game this household no longer knows up again (owner decision 2026-10-29) — Social Home's sheet calls it ("Continue game with Ben"), never the app. Body `{target}` (someone the sheet lists). Returns `{session_id}`. The session becomes known with that person (a no-op when it already is) and their household gets `APP_SESSION {verb: "continue"}` — a Needs-you invite (`app_challenge_continue`), kept only if their person has no row for that id. `409 APP_SESSION_TAKEN` when the id is known with someone else or still waits as an invite; `400` for an id outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}`; counts as an invite for the caps (`429`). See [protocol/apps.md](./protocol/apps.md#continue-a-game). |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/messages` | Any member | Send an app-layer message to a person. Body: `{session_id, target: {instance_id, user_ref, is_local}, payload}` where `payload` is any JSON dict. The payload is AES-256-GCM-sealed inside the signed federation envelope — never sent in plaintext. Only in a game this household knows the caller plays with **that** person (a session sent through the invite sheet or taken in with Play, not withdrawn; `is_local` must match): `403 APP_SESSION_UNKNOWN` otherwise — a made-up session id, or an old opponent on a new one (D3). `403 FORBIDDEN` when a block (or the DM rule for a protected caller) now separates them; `400` for a household-addressed `{peer_instance_id}` body. The check is one session lookup plus the block checks, not a roster scan. The recipient's household keeps the latest message per game for them until their app opens (the mailbox, see below). Optional `turn: true` (a bool; anything else → `400`): the move hands the other player the turn — once kept for them in a game they play, they get one "Your turn with … in …" row per game (`app_turn`, kind Games, in app by default; none while they have the app on screen; read when their app takes its held messages). Sealed on the wire beside `to_user`; see [Your turn](protocol/apps.md#your-turn). Own rate-limit bucket (300 / min / user). |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/held` | Any member | The app opened: hand over the caller's **mailbox** (D11) and clear it — `{messages: [{session_id, from_instance, from_user, kind, payload}]}`, the latest undelivered message of each game, oldest first. `kind` is `"message"` (a move) or `"session"` (a close: `{verb: "close", reason: "declined" \| "withdrawn" \| "ended"}` — `ended`: the other household uninstalled the app, v_72). Messages are kept while the app is closed or turned off, only for sessions this household knows for the caller and only from that session's other side; one from someone the caller blocked or a guardian block now separates them from is dropped. A POST because taking empties it; the games it hands over have their "Your turn" rows read. `403 APP_NOT_ENABLED` while the app is off. The SPA bridge hands them to the app as live `app:event`s right after `app.context`, or on `app.held` for an app that pulls (SDK ≥ 1.3) — see [protocol/apps.md](./protocol/apps.md#mailbox). |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups` | Any member (`people`) | Group tables (v_76) the caller has a place at in this app — `{groups: [view]}`, latest change first. A *view* is `{session_id, app_id, state, end_reason, space_id, rev, host (seat), is_host, me (seat or null), my_state (invited / joining / seated / left / removed), min, max, late_join, note, created_at, started_at, expires_at, invited (people asked who haven't answered — host household only, else null), players: [{seat, name, household, is_local, state}]}`: seats and names, never a username. |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups` | Any member (`people`; `federation` for another household) | Open a table with people picked in Social Home's invite sheet (D3). Body `{targets: [{instance_id, user_ref, is_local}], max_players?, lobby_days? (1 / 3 / 7 / 14, default 3), note? (≤ 80, one line)}` → 201 `{group}`. 400 malformed body; 409 `APP_GROUPS_NOT_SUPPORTED` (manifest without `tables`), `APP_GROUP_HOUSEHOLD_TOO_OLD` (a household below v_76); 422 `APP_GROUP_SIZE` (`params.min/max`: host + picked within the app's `players`, ≤ 10), `UNPROCESSABLE` (lobby_days / note); 429 `APP_GROUP_OPEN_LIMIT` (5 waiting) / `APP_GROUP_DAILY_LIMIT` (10 a day) and the invite caps (one per person picked); 403 (as for a 1:1 invite) for someone not in the sheet. |
| GET | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups/{session_id}` | A person with a place at it | `{group}` (the view above); 404 `APP_GROUP_NOT_FOUND` otherwise. |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups/{session_id}/join` | The invitee | Join (`{group}`, `my_state: joining` until the host household seats them). 403 `APP_GROUP_CANT_JOIN` when this household keeps them out (a block, a guardian block, a protected account and a player it may not message — no reason given); 409 `APP_GROUP_FULL` / `APP_GROUP_CLOSED`. |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups/{session_id}/decline` | The invitee | Say no; the host household marks it, the players aren't told. |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups/{session_id}/leave` | A seated player | Leave (before Start the seat is free again; the host leaving closes a lobby, or hands a game to the player seated longest). |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups/{session_id}/start` | The host person | Start (`{group}`): 409 `APP_GROUP_TOO_FEW` (`params.min`) / `APP_GROUP_CLOSED`, 403 `APP_GROUP_NOT_HOST`. Unanswered invites are withdrawn unless the app takes late joiners. |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups/{session_id}/remove` | The host person | Body `{seat}` — take that player out for good (`{group}`). |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups/{session_id}/close` | The host person | Close the table for everyone (`cancelled`). |
| POST | `/api/apps/{app_id}/groups/{session_id}/messages` | A seated player (`people`; `federation` once another household is at the table) | A message at the table (v_76): body `{data, to?: [seat…], turn?: seat}` → `{seq}` (the number when this household is the host household, `null` when it went to the host household). To everyone, or only to the seats in `to` — then sealed to each one's household seat key, the host household passes on ciphertext only. `turn` names whose turn it is now ("Your turn" there, one row per game). 400 malformed body; 413 `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` past 64 KiB; 409 `APP_GROUP_SEAT` (a seat nobody holds, your own, or one without a seat key), `APP_GROUP_CLOSED` (ended, or you aren't seated); 404 `APP_GROUP_NOT_FOUND`; 429 `APP_GROUP_BUSY` (`params.retry_after`: the table's 600 envelopes a minute). Shares the 300 / min `POST /api/apps/*/messages` bucket. See [protocol/app-groups.md](./protocol/app-groups.md#messages-at-a-table). |

## HFS — Household dashboard

A wall-tablet view of the household, served by its **own aiohttp app on
its own port** (default `8100`, fixed at `8100` on the Home Assistant App)
and unlocked with a household-wide dashboard password — see
[architecture.md](./architecture.md#household-dashboard-own-port) and
[operations/household-dashboard.md](./operations/household-dashboard.md).
The admin manages it through these main-app endpoints (admin-only; 403
`FORBIDDEN` for anyone else):

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/admin/dashboard` | Settings + listener status: `{enabled, running, running_port, port, port_fixed, password_set, password_set_at, pin_set, address, url, url_host_allowed, host_port_mapped, locale, idle_return_minutes, night_start, night_end, keep_screen_on, show_status_text, show_household_messages, mention_sound, panel_unlock_from_ha, household_chat_enabled, last_seen_at, people, displays, ha_display_discovery}`. `running` / `running_port` are the live listener (an enabled dashboard whose port was taken at startup reads `running: false`). `port_fixed` is `true` when the platform owns the port (Home Assistant App). `url` is `address` when the admin set one, else a guess: `http://<host the admin reached Social Home under>:<port>` — the host is the first `X-Forwarded-Host` entry, else `Host` (under the Home Assistant App, HA's ingress proxy sets `X-Forwarded-Host` to the name the browser used, e.g. `homeassistant.local:8123`). `url_host_allowed` is `false` when the dashboard's `Host` allow-list would refuse `url`'s host (421) — the UI then asks the admin to set the address. On the Home Assistant App the `url`'s port is the **host** port HA Supervisor publishes the dashboard on (the App's Network settings, read from Supervisor's `/addons/self/info` `network` map), not the container port `8100`; `host_port_mapped` is `true` there when a host port is set, `false` when the App declares the port but no host port is set (the UI then asks the admin to turn the port on under Settings › Apps › Social Home › Network — `url` keeps `8100` meanwhile), and `null` on other platforms, when Supervisor couldn't be asked, or when the installed App release doesn't declare the port at all (`url` keeps `8100`; nothing the admin can change). `pin_set` says whether a privacy-lock PIN exists (the PIN and its hash never leave the server). `panel_unlock_from_ha` says whether Home Assistant may lift a wall panel's privacy lock without the PIN (`POST /api/display/v1/unlock/ha`). `household_chat_enabled` mirrors the household `feat_household_chat` toggle — the messages switch is greyed while it is off. `last_seen_at` (ISO UTC or `null`) is when a signed-in tablet last made a request since this process started. `people` lists every active household member as `{user_id, username, display_name, picture_url, show, picture, picture_effective}` — `picture` is the stored choice (`true` / `false` / `null` = default), `picture_effective` the resolved one (on by default for child-protected minors; the age or minor flag itself never leaves). `displays` is the paired wall panels, as `GET /api/admin/dashboard/displays` lists them. `ha_display_discovery` is `true` when the platform can list wall panels adopted in Home Assistant (`Capability.HA_DISPLAY_DISCOVERY` — `ha` and the Home Assistant App). |
| PUT | `/api/admin/dashboard` | Partial settings patch: `enabled`, `port` (1024–65535, not the main port; 422 `DASHBOARD_PORT_FIXED` on the Home Assistant App), `address` (`http(s)://host[:port]`, optional trailing `/`, no path / query / fragment / userinfo, ≤200 chars; `null` or `""` clears it), `locale` (`en` / `de` / `es` / `fr` / `nl`), `idle_return_minutes` (0 / 1 / 3 / 10), `night_start` / `night_end` (`HH:MM`), `keep_screen_on`, `show_status_text`, `show_household_messages` (bool, default `false` — Home and picture-view pages show the newest household-chat messages, plus unacknowledged `@here` / `@name` mentions), `mention_sound` (bool, default `true` — the tablet plays a short on-device tone for a new mention), `panel_unlock_from_ha` (bool, default `false` — Home Assistant may lift a wall panel's privacy lock without the PIN through the panel's "Privacy lock" entity, `POST /api/display/v1/unlock/ha`; once on, anyone who can run Home Assistant automations or actions can unlock the panels), `people` (`{user_id: {show?, picture?} \| null}`, merged per person; `null` drops the entry). Only a patch touching `enabled` or `port` moves the listener (start, stop, new port); any other edit is just saved — also while an enabled dashboard isn't running because its port was taken at startup. Save + listener change run as one critical section, so concurrent edits can't interleave. 422 `DASHBOARD_SETTINGS_INVALID` (`params.field` names the setting; nothing is saved), 422 `DASHBOARD_PASSWORD_REQUIRED` when enabling without a password, 409 `DASHBOARD_PORT_IN_USE` (`params.port`) when the new port can't be bound — the whole patch is rolled back (on any listener error, not only a taken port) and the listener keeps its old state. Returns the `GET` shape. |
| PUT | `/api/admin/dashboard/password` | Body `{password}` (≥8 chars; 422 `DASHBOARD_PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT`). Stores a scrypt hash, **signs out every tablet and unpairs every wall panel** (one write with the epoch bump). 204. |
| POST | `/api/admin/dashboard/setup-link` | A one-time tablet sign-in link (for a QR code): `{url: "<base>/enter?t=<token>", expires_at}` — `<base>` as `url` above, valid 10 minutes, single use; issuing one replaces any pending link. 409 `DASHBOARD_NOT_RUNNING` ("Turn the dashboard on before creating a sign-in link.") while the dashboard isn't listening. |
| DELETE | `/api/admin/dashboard/sessions` | Sign out every tablet (bumps the session epoch) **and unpair every wall panel** — the epoch and the emptied panel list are one write. 204. |
| PUT | `/api/admin/dashboard/pin` | Body `{pin}` — the tablets' privacy-lock PIN: exactly 4–6 ASCII digits (422 `DASHBOARD_PIN_INVALID`, `params: {min: 4, max: 6}`). Separate from the dashboard password; stores a scrypt hash, signs nobody out; replaces any earlier PIN. 204. |
| DELETE | `/api/admin/dashboard/pin` | Remove the PIN. Locked tablets then count as unlocked (nothing could unlock them otherwise) and the lock button disappears; every locked wall panel is unlocked (its stored flag cleared — setting a new PIN later locks nothing again). 204. |
| GET | `/api/admin/dashboard/displays` | The paired wall panels (e.g. ESPHome displays), oldest first: `{displays: [{id, name, model, firmware, created_at, last_seen_at, locked}]}`. `model` / `firmware` are what the panel reports (printable ASCII, ≤64 chars, `null` if never sent); `last_seen_at` (ISO UTC or `null`) is written at most once a minute per panel. A panel's token and its hash never appear. |
| POST | `/api/admin/dashboard/displays` | Body `{code, name}` — pair the panel showing `code` (6 characters; case, spaces and dashes ignored) as `name` (1–40 characters; no control or format characters such as bidi overrides — emoji joiners are fine; 422 `DASHBOARD_SETTINGS_INVALID`, `params.field: "name"`). Mints a 256-bit token, stores only its SHA-256 and leaves the token for the panel to collect (`GET /api/display/v1/pair/{pair_id}` on the dashboard port). 201 with the display. 404 `DASHBOARD_PAIRING_CODE_INVALID` (wrong, used or expired code — every pending code is compared in constant time), 429 `DASHBOARD_PAIRING_THROTTLED` (`params.retry_after` seconds — see Rate limits), 409 `DASHBOARD_DISABLED` while the dashboard is off, 409 `DASHBOARD_DISPLAY_LIMIT` (`params.max`: 20). |
| PATCH | `/api/admin/dashboard/displays/{display_id}` | Body `{name?, locked?}` — rename a panel (rules as above) and / or lift its privacy lock with `"locked": false` (recovery for a panel nobody can unlock; only the panel itself locks — any other `locked` value is 422 `DASHBOARD_SETTINGS_INVALID`, `params.field: "locked"`). Everything is validated before anything is written; with neither key it is a rename (`params.field: "name"`). 200 with the display; 404 `DASHBOARD_DISPLAY_NOT_FOUND`. |
| DELETE | `/api/admin/dashboard/displays/{display_id}` | Unpair a panel — its token stops working on the next request, and a connect claim still pending for it is dropped. 204; 404 `DASHBOARD_DISPLAY_NOT_FOUND`. |
| GET | `/api/admin/dashboard/displays/ha` | Wall panels adopted in Home Assistant (ESPHome devices whose `esphome.project` is `social-home-io.esphome-dashboard`, read from HA's device and entity registries): `{panels: [{id, name, sw_version, status, can_connect, display_id, display_name}]}`. `id` is HA's device id, `name` its HA name (the user's rename first). `status`: `connected` (paired here — `display_id` / `display_name` say as which display; matched by the MAC the panel reports), `ready`, `offline` (HA can't reach it) or `needs_update` (its firmware has no `connect_social_home` action or publishes no *Social Home key*, or one of those entities is disabled). `can_connect` is `true` while Connect works (also for a connected panel — connecting again replaces its pairing). Never the MAC, the panel key or the HA action name. 404 `DISPLAY_DISCOVERY_UNAVAILABLE` without the capability (standalone); 502 `DISPLAY_DISCOVERY_FAILED` when Home Assistant didn't answer (logged at WARNING). |
| POST | `/api/admin/dashboard/displays/ha/{panel_id}/connect` | Send the panel a connect request through Home Assistant: mints a single-use **claim** (256 bits, only its SHA-256 kept in memory, keyed by the panel's HA id, bound to its MAC, to the public key it publishes in its *Social Home key* sensor and to the current session epoch, valid 10 minutes, at most 20 pending — one per panel, a new Connect replaces it), seals `{claim, url}` to that key (suite `x25519-hkdf-sha256-aes256gcm`, `docs/crypto.md`; `docs/display-api/README.md` for the wire shape) and calls the HA action `esphome.<node>_connect_social_home` with `{sealed}`. `url` is the dashboard address the panel should use: the admin-set `address`, else the guessed tablet URL (see `GET /api/admin/dashboard`). The panel then spends the claim at `POST /api/display/v1/pair/claim` and appears under the paired panels. 202 `{expires_in}`. Errors: 404 `DISPLAY_DISCOVERY_UNAVAILABLE`; 409 `DASHBOARD_DISABLED` while the dashboard isn't running; 409 `DASHBOARD_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` with `params.reason` `port_not_published` (Home Assistant App: the dashboard port isn't turned on) or `no_address` (no address set and the guess is a loopback name, a public IP, or a host the dashboard would refuse); 404 `DISPLAY_PANEL_NOT_FOUND`; 409 `DISPLAY_PANEL_OFFLINE`; 409 `DISPLAY_PANEL_NEEDS_UPDATE` (also for a panel without a usable key); 502 `DISPLAY_DISCOVERY_FAILED` (HA refused or didn't answer — the claim is dropped). Home Assistant's recorder stores the action's data — only the sealed value: neither the claim nor the dashboard address is readable there; the long-lived token never passes through Home Assistant. |

Pages on the **dashboard port** (not under `/api/*`, no main-app bearer
token, no HA ingress — the only credential is the dashboard's signed
session cookie; wall panels use their own API, below):

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET / POST | `/login` | Password form; `POST` (same-origin only) sets the session cookie. Throttled per client (5 failures / 15 min) and globally (30 failures / 15 min). |
| GET / POST | `/enter?t=<token>` | Setup link: `GET` shows a confirm button only (link previewers can't spend the token); `POST` spends it and sets a one-year cookie. |
| POST | `/logout` | Clear this tablet's cookie. |
| GET / HEAD | `/` | **Home** — its Overview tab, the household overview (signed-in only; otherwise a redirect to `/login`): clock, date and who's home, next up, the newest household-chat messages (minus one shown on an `@here` banner), today's swim-lanes (school blocks from timetables, "at school now", today's timetable changes), chores / shopping / three notes, with **See all ›** links to the Messages / Notes tab when it holds more. A tab bar (Overview · Messages · Notes · Map) heads every Home tab while more than Overview shows, with a badge counting the messages the Messages tab lists that are under 15 min old (never on the Messages tab; a message on an `@here` banner is not counted); the `@here` banners sit under it on every tab. Night mode (inside the night window) shows only the clock, the date and the first item of the morning. Carries one inline `<style>` (validated colours only) admitted by its hash in that response's CSP. |
| GET / HEAD | `/messages` | Home's **Messages** tab: the household chat of the last 24 h (at most 30, newest first) under Today / Yesterday in the household zone — the same senders and filters as Home (shown, unblocked people; no location, deleted or future message), a message on an `@here` banner left out until "Got it". Each row is one toggle button that reads the whole message aloud; long text is cut at 220 characters on a word. Only while `show_household_messages` and the household chat are on (it stays with an empty state when there is nothing) — otherwise **404**, the not-found page. At night **303** to `/`. Renews the cookie like `/`. |
| GET / HEAD | `/notes` | Home's **Notes** tab: every household sticky on a flat board. Only while the stickies section is on (an empty state when there are none) — otherwise **404**. At night **303** to `/`. Renews the cookie like `/`. |
| GET / HEAD | `/map` | Home's **Map** tab: who is where — household zones (the household's own, Home first; emoji pin or first letter, radius in the zone colour as the app paints it) with everyone in a zone in one photo-marker bubble at the zone centre (never their own point), everyone outside every zone in a one-face bubble over a dot at their 4-dp point, and a **Who's where** list (face, name, place, "5 min ago"; over an hour old: faded, "last seen 2 h ago"; over a day: gone) that is the accessible equivalent of the map. The map is drawn by `map.js` from `data-*` attributes (no Leaflet, no pan / pinch; tiles from `/tiles/…` below); without JS only the list shows. Only while a shown member has a location under 24 h old (a point or a known zone) — otherwise **303** to `/` (sharing flips at runtime); at night **303** to `/`. Renews the cookie like `/`. |
| GET / HEAD | `/p/{user_id}` | One shown person's page (now / next, school today, today + tomorrow, their chores). **404** (the same page) for an unknown, inactive or hidden person. A person in picture view (`picture_effective`) gets the picture page instead (`_person_picture.html.j2`: emoji tiles with small helper captions, the real titles and times in `aria-label`s) — see [architecture.md](./architecture.md#household-dashboard-own-port) ("Picture view"). |
| GET | `/fragment/home`, `/fragment/home/messages`, `/fragment/home/notes`, `/fragment/home/map`, `/fragment/p/{user_id}` | The `<main>` content of `/`, `/messages`, `/notes`, `/map` or `/p/{user_id}` without chrome, for the page script's 30 s refresh (the tab bar and the alert slot included). Starts with `<div class="dash-view" data-view-key="…">` — a key that changes with what a swap can't (inline colours, night mode, rail, Home's tab, the person's picture view); the script reloads the page when it differs (so night starting on a tab lands on Overview). **401** (never a login page) without a session, **404** once the person is hidden or the tab is off; `/fragment/home/map` answers **303** to `/` like its page (the script follows it to Overview). Never renews the cookie. |
| GET | `/avatar/{user_id}` | A **shown**, active person's profile picture (stored bytes, content type sniffed: WebP / PNG / JPEG / GIF). `Cache-Control: private, no-cache` (every load re-passes the session guard: 423 when locked, 404 once hidden), `ETag` = the picture hash (`If-None-Match` → 304). 404 for hidden / inactive / unknown people and people without a picture (pages show initials). |
| GET | `/tiles/{z}/{x}/{y}` | One map tile for the Map tab, from the main app's tile proxy (`MapTileService` — the same upstream template and in-memory cache as `/api/map/tiles`), so the tablet's browser never fetches from a third party (CSP `img-src 'self'`). Session required: **401** (never a login redirect) without one, **423** while privacy-locked (a tile says which area the wall shows). 1200 per minute per session (429 + `Retry-After`). **404** for coordinates off the grid, **503** when the upstream fails and nothing is cached. `Cache-Control: private, no-cache` with an `ETag` (`If-None-Match` → 304): every use re-passes the session guard, so a lock takes effect at once. Never renews the cookie. |
| POST | `/lock` | Privacy-lock this tablet (session required, same-origin): re-issues the cookie with the lock claim and a new session id, and revokes the incoming session as *locked* (in memory, every copy of it — a late response re-sending one shows the lock screen, it never lifts the lock), 303 to `/`. On an already-locked session: 303 to `/` and nothing else (no new cookie). 409 when no PIN is set. `GET /lock` → 303 `/`. |
| POST | `/unlock` | Form field `pin`. Every outcome is a redirect (Post/Redirect/Get — a reload never re-sends a PIN; `GET /unlock` → 303 `/`). Locked session + right PIN → unlocked cookie, 303 `/`; wrong PIN → 303 `/?pin=wrong` (the lock screen then says "Wrong PIN" and "Tries left: n" — the count read from this lock's own throttle bucket, never from the address, like the panel's `params.remaining`; no count when none is counted); an unlocked session → 303 `/` (no PIN check). Throttled per lock (the locked session's id): the 5th wrong PIN in 15 min **signs the tablet out** — cookie cleared and the session revoked (in memory, every copy), 303 `/login?signed_out=1` — so the lock screen never shows "0 tries left"; with one try left it says so ("Last try — one more wrong PIN signs this screen out."), and a try that finds the budget already spent (racing the fifth) signs out too. Held back for any other reason (a full throttle table) → 303 `/?pin=wait` with `Retry-After`, cookie kept; that lock screen answers 429 + `Retry-After` while the wait lasts. The `?pin=` flags are only read while locked. |
| POST | `/ack` | "Got it" on a household-chat mention (session required, same-origin, unlocked). Form fields `message_id` and, on a person page, `person_id`. The server re-derives everything — the message must be a live household-chat message from a shown, unblocked sender, at most an hour old, within the messages opt-in (chat on, not night), and really `@here` (no `person_id`) or really `@`-mention that shown person — then adds a ✅ reaction to it: as the pseudo-author `household-dashboard` for `@here`, as the person for their own mention (idempotent; open chats update live via `dm.message_reaction`; never federated). 303 to `/` or `/p/{person_id}` (never anywhere the form names); anything else is the not-found page (404, never saying why). 30 per 5 min per session (429 + `Retry-After`). `GET /ack` → 303 `/`. |
| GET / HEAD | `/static/{name}` | Preloaded stylesheet / assets (public). |
| GET | `/healthz` | Liveness (public). |

**Wall panels** (e.g. ESPHome displays) use a small JSON API on the same
**dashboard port**, under `/api/display/v1/`. There the only credential is
a paired panel's `Authorization: Bearer <token>` — the tablet cookie opens
nothing under this prefix, a panel token opens no page outside it, and the
same-origin `POST` check doesn't apply (no cookie authority to ride on).
Errors use the canonical `{"error": {code, detail}}` shape. Panels may send
`X-Display-Firmware` / `X-Display-Model` on any authenticated request
(stored at most once a minute). The board endpoints spell the same views
as the tablet's pages (`dashboard/boards.py`), every user-readable string
pre-formatted in the dashboard locale and household time zone; their JSON
Schemas, fixtures and versioning rules are in
[display-api/](./display-api/README.md). Every board / image answer carries
an `ETag` and `Cache-Control: private, no-cache` — send it back in
`If-None-Match` for a `304`. A panel whose record is **locked** (while a
PIN is set) gets **423** with `{api_version, locked: true, household,
locale, dir, clock, is_night, strings}` (nothing about any person; the
strings include the PIN pad's tries-left / last-try / forgotten-PIN
lines) on every bearer
endpoint but `POST /unlock`, `POST /unlock/ha` and the lock screen's own 🔒 picture
(`GET /emoji/1f512`, a bundled Twemoji); reads are budgeted per panel (429 +
`Retry-After`, see Rate limits). A panel's lock is stored on its record
(`dashboard.displays`), so a panel reboot or a server restart keeps it.

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/display/v1/pair` | Public. Optional JSON body `{model?, firmware?}` (400 `BAD_REQUEST` for anything but an object). Starts a pairing: 201 `{pair_id, code, expires_in}` — `code` is 6 characters from `23456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTWXYZ` (no look-alikes) for the panel to show, `pair_id` a random 128-bit id, both valid 10 minutes. At most 32 pairings wait at once (the oldest is dropped); held in memory, a restart drops them. 409 `DASHBOARD_DISABLED` while the dashboard is off; 429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After` (see Rate limits). |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/pair/{pair_id}` | Public. The panel polls: `{"status": "pending"}` until an admin types the code, then **exactly once** `{"status": "paired", "token", "display_id"}` (the entry is dropped; the panel has 10 more minutes to collect it); 404 `NOT_FOUND` for an unknown, collected or expired pairing. |
| POST | `/api/display/v1/pair/claim` | Public. A panel connected from Home Assistant spends the claim it was sent: JSON `{claim, model?, firmware?}` plus the headers `X-Display-Mac` (the panel's MAC as ESPHome reports it to Home Assistant; case and `:` / `-` separators ignored) and `X-Display-Key` (the panel's X25519 public key, 43-character base64url — the key the claim was sealed to). 200 `{"status": "paired", "token", "display_id"}` (`pair-claim.schema.json` — the poll's paired shape; the token is handed out here, once) and a new display named as in Home Assistant; a display with the same MAC (the panel's earlier pairing) is replaced on the new token's first authenticated request — until then the old token keeps working (a same-MAC display from an earlier, still unused claim is dropped at once). 404 `NOT_FOUND` — the same answer — for a malformed, unknown, expired or already spent claim, one minted before "Sign out all tablets" / a password change, a MAC that isn't the panel's it was minted for, or a missing / other `X-Display-Key` than the one it was sealed to (every pending claim is compared in constant time; a matching claim is spent by that try whatever its outcome). 409 `DASHBOARD_DISABLED` while the dashboard is off; 400 `BAD_REQUEST` for a body that isn't a JSON object; 429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After` (see Rate limits). |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/whoami` | Bearer. `{display_id, name}` — lets a panel check its token. 401 `UNAUTHORIZED` + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` without a valid token (also for any other path under the prefix, so nothing about the API leaks); the token is matched by SHA-256 in constant time against every panel. |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/home` | Bearer. Home's Overview as JSON (`home.schema.json`): `api_version` 1, `view_key` (changes when the layout must be rebuilt: night, rail, locale, settings, theme), household name, the household clock (to the minute, with `tz` / `utc_offset_minutes`), the settings a panel needs (`idle_return_minutes`, `keep_screen_on`, `mention_sound`, night window, `pin_set`), the shown people (id, name, initials, colour + ink, avatar path, where — no coordinates, mention badge), night mode, the tab bar (same availability as the tablet), the `@here` banners (with the `ack` ids) and the Overview cards (who's home, next up, newest messages, today's timeline / list, school now and changes, chores, shopping (6), notes (3)). |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/messages` | Bearer. Home's Messages tab (`messages.schema.json`): the last day of the household chat grouped Today / Yesterday, or the empty state. 404 `TAB_OFF` while the tab doesn't show (chat opt-in off); 409 `NIGHT` at night (the panel goes back to Overview, like the tablet's 303). |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/notes` | Bearer. Home's Notes tab (`notes.schema.json`): every household sticky with its light / dark note colours. 404 `TAB_OFF` (stickies off), 409 `NIGHT`. |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/map` | Bearer. Home's Map tab (`map.schema.json`): "Who's where" (place, when, stale), the bubbles (zone members at the zone centre; someone outside every zone at their 4-dp point — as on the tablet) and the zone pins, plus `image` — `{url, width, height, etag}` of the map picture (`GET …/map.png`, default 748 × 560: the firmware's map area; `etag` is the `ETag` the default picture — default size, light — has now), `null` without a tile source. 404 `TAB_OFF` while nobody shown is located (or presence is off), 409 `NIGHT` (at night this comes first on every tab, so a tab never says whether it would show by day). |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/map.png?w=&h=&scheme=` | Bearer. Home's Map tab as one PNG for panels that can't draw a map: the tablet's framing (`map.js`, ported in `dashboard/map_frame.py` — integer zoom, never closer than 16, people far beyond a regional zoom as edge chips), tiles through the shared tile proxy (same upstream + cache as `/api/map/tiles`), zone bubbles with faces and "+N", photo pins framed in each person's colour, stale faces faded, merged bubbles, and the tile credit (© OpenStreetMap contributors). `w` 200–1920 and `h` 200–1200, clamped (default 748 × 560); `scheme` `light` (default) or `dark`; 400 `BAD_SIZE` / `BAD_SCHEME`. Same rules as `/map` (404 `TAB_OFF`, 409 `NIGHT`, 423 locked; hidden people are never in it). `ETag` per scene, size and scheme (`If-None-Match` → 304 without painting); a picture drawn with tiles or photos missing (the board colour / initials show there) has its own `ETag` and is redrawn after a minute. Tiles are awaited at most 10 s. 503 `TILES_UNAVAILABLE` + `Retry-After` when no tile at all loads (remembered a minute) or there is no tile source; 429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After` past the painting budget (see Rate limits). Painted with Pillow off the event loop, one at a time (one painting per picture in flight); the last 8 pictures are kept. |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/person/{user_id}` | Bearer. A shown person's page (`person.schema.json`): `kind` `reader` (now / next, school today, today + tomorrow, chores) or `picture` (analog clock angles + time-timer wedge, emoji tiles by day part, tomorrow, jobs, who's home, message tiles), plus their own mentions. 404 `NOT_FOUND` for anyone unknown, inactive or hidden. |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/theme` | Bearer. Colours (`light` / `dark` / `night` palettes from `dashboard.css`, with the household theme colour as accent where it reads, as on the tablet), font, locale, text direction and the translated static UI strings a panel needs (tabs, lock / PIN incl. tries left / wait — `{n}` left for the panel — "Got it", night, offline — `{time}` left for the panel — pairing, connect from Home Assistant — `{host}` left for the panel — and the panel's Wi-Fi / address / update / settings screens). |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/avatar/{user_id}?size=N` | Bearer. The person's picture as an N × N PNG (`N` ∈ 48, 64, 96, 128, 192; default 96; 400 `BAD_SIZE` otherwise), transcoded off the event loop and cached by picture hash. Same rule as `/avatar/{user_id}`: 404 for anyone hidden, inactive or unknown and for no picture. `ETag` `"<picture hash>-<N>"`. |
| POST | `/api/display/v1/lock` | Bearer. Privacy-lock this panel: stores `locked` on its record and answers **200** with the lock screen (`lock.schema.json`, the 423 body). 409 `DASHBOARD_PIN_REQUIRED` while no PIN is set (nothing could unlock it — the tablet's `/lock` answers 409 too); an already-locked panel gets the 423. |
| POST | `/api/display/v1/unlock` | Bearer; reachable while locked (with `POST /unlock/ha`). JSON `{pin}`. 200 `{api_version: 1, locked: false}` (`unlock.schema.json`) — also for a panel that isn't locked, where no PIN is checked. The PIN is checked against the household PIN (scrypt, constant time, one check at a time); a wrong one (or a non-string) is 403 `DISPLAY_PIN_WRONG` with `params.remaining` (tries left, at least 1). Throttled per panel like the tablet's per lock: the 5th wrong PIN in 15 min — and any try, even the right PIN, that finds them already spent — **unpairs the panel**: 401 `DISPLAY_SIGNED_OUT` + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`, its token dead; only an admin pairing it again helps. Held back for any other reason (a full throttle table) → 429 `RATE_LIMITED` with `params.retry_after` + `Retry-After`, still paired. 400 `BAD_REQUEST` for a body that isn't a JSON object. |
| POST | `/api/display/v1/unlock/ha` | Bearer; reachable while locked (no 423). No body (one sent is ignored). Home Assistant lifts this panel's privacy lock without the PIN — the panel calls it when its "Privacy lock" entity is unlocked from Home Assistant. Only while an admin turned on `panel_unlock_from_ha` (Admin › Dashboard › Wall panels › "Home Assistant may unlock wall panels", off by default; the 423 lock body and every board's `settings` carry it as `ha_unlock`), else 403 `DISPLAY_HA_UNLOCK_OFF` — checked whether or not the panel is locked, so the panel can report its lock entity as still locked. 200 `{api_version: 1, locked: false}` (`unlock.schema.json`) — also for a panel that isn't locked or when no PIN is set (nothing to lift; nothing logged). A lifted lock does **not** reset the panel's wrong-PIN count (earlier wrong PINs still count until they age out of the 15-min window — only an admin's unlock or the right PIN resets them); it is logged at INFO and written to the auth audit log (event `display_ha_unlock`, the caller's `ip_address`, `metadata: {display_id, display_name}`; see `GET /api/admin/auth-audit`). A refused call (403) is logged at INFO, not audited. 6 calls / 5 min per panel, counted before the setting is checked (429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After`, see Rate limits); 401 for an unknown or revoked token. |
| POST | `/api/display/v1/ack` | Bearer, unlocked. JSON `{message_id, person_id?}` — "Got it" on a mention the panel shows (each mention / `@here` banner carries its `ack` ids). The tablet's `POST /ack` rules: the server re-derives that the wall shows exactly that mention right now (chat opt-in on, not night, shown unblocked sender, within the hour, really `@here` without `person_id` or really naming that shown person) and adds the ✅ reaction (as `household-dashboard` for `@here`, as the person for their own mention; idempotent). 200 `{api_version: 1, acknowledged: true}` (`ack.schema.json`); anything else 404 `NOT_FOUND`, never saying why. 30 per 5 min per panel (429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After`); 423 while locked; 400 `BAD_REQUEST` for a body that isn't a JSON object. |
| GET | `/api/display/v1/emoji/{code}?size=N` | Bearer. A bundled Twemoji PNG (`code` is the Twemoji code the payloads carry, e.g. `1f3ca`; `N` ∈ 24, 32, 48, 64, 72, 96, 128; default 72). An emoji the bundle lacks is 📍 (`1f4cd`). Graphics: Twemoji v17.0.3, CC-BY 4.0. |

**Finding the dashboard (mDNS).** Where the platform shares the host's
network (standalone, `ha` mode — not the Home Assistant App), the running
dashboard announces itself on the LAN as a DNS-SD service, so a panel can
find it without a typed address. The announcement follows the listener
(on with the dashboard, moved on a port change, withdrawn when it stops).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Service type | `_socialhome-dash._tcp.local.` |
| Instance name | `Social Home <id>` (`<id>`: 6 hex characters of a domain-separated SHA-256 of the instance's secret identity seed — stable, and not computable from the public instance id, so paired households or a GFS can't recognise the household on a shared network). On a name clash the responder appends a suffix (`Social Home <id> (2)`). |
| Host / port | `socialhome-<id>.local.`, the dashboard port; A/AAAA = the host's LAN addresses (loopback, link-local and container / VPN interfaces skipped) |
| TXT `api` | `1` — major version of the API under `path` |
| TXT `id` | `<id>` — tells two households apart |
| TXT `path` | `/api/display/v1/` |

Nothing else is announced — no household name, member data or software
version. A panel must ignore unknown TXT keys and accept only `api`
versions it speaks.

**A locked tablet** (cookie lock claim, while a PIN is set) reaches only `/lock`, `/unlock` and the public paths (`/login`, `/enter`, `/logout`, `/static/…`, `/healthz`). Everything else is answered by the session guard: a page view (`GET` / `HEAD`, e.g. `/`, `/p/{id}`) gets **200 with the lock screen** (household name, clock, date, PIN form and who can change a forgotten PIN — nothing about any person; night colours in the night window; the household theme colour as the accent, its inline `<style>` admitted by hash; a 200, not a redirect, so a reload stays locked — with JS it reloads itself after a quiet minute, so removing the PIN or the night window shows without a tap); `/fragment/…` gets **200 with header `X-Dashboard-Locked: 1` and the body `<div data-dashboard-locked="1"></div>`** — page JS must check the header and reload into the lock screen instead of painting it; `/avatar/…` and any other method get **423**.

Every page loads `/static/dashboard.js`, which reloads a page restored from the browser's back/forward cache (`pageshow` with `persisted`), so a page rendered before the lock is never shown again. Only page loads renew the session cookie — an allow-list: `GET` / `HEAD` of `/` and `/p/{id}`; never `/fragment/…`, `/avatar/…`, `/static/…` or a `POST`, whose response could land after a `/lock` and overwrite the locked cookie. Midnight reloads and the idle return first probe the server and stay on the page while it is unreachable. On `/` and `/p/{id}` the script also polls the matching `/fragment/…` every 30 s: `X-Dashboard-Locked` → `location.replace('/')` (the lock screen), 401 → `/login?next=<path>`, 404 → `/`.

## HFS — Storage, backup, misc

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/storage/usage` | Own usage. |
| GET / PATCH | `/api/admin/storage/quota` | Admin quota config. |
| PUT | `/api/admin/moments/archive-cap` | Admin sets the per-person cap on archived moment media. Body `{cap_bytes}` — a whole number ≥ 0, `0` = no limit (default 2 GiB); `422` otherwise, `403` for a non-admin. Returns `{cap_bytes}`. Stored in `instance_config` (`moments.archive_cap_bytes`). |
| POST | `/api/backup/pre_backup` | HA snapshot hook. |
| POST | `/api/backup/post_backup` | HA snapshot hook. |
| GET / POST | `/api/backup/{export\|import}` | Full archive round-trip. |
| POST | `/api/recovery-kit` | Admin-only. Body `{"passphrase": str}` (≥8 chars, in body so it never hits access logs); returns the passphrase-sealed `.shrk` Recovery Kit (trust layer for same-identity disaster recovery — see `docs/crypto.md` "Recovery Kit"). 422 on a short/missing passphrase. |
| POST | `/api/setup/recovery/restore` | **Setup-gated, no bearer** (fresh-box only; 409 once setup is complete). Body `{"kit_b64": str, "passphrase": str}`. Validates the Kit, wipes the auto-minted throwaway identity, restores the Kit's trust layer (same `instance_id`), marks setup complete, and schedules a process restart. Returns `{instance_id, restart_required: true}`. 422 `BAD_KIT` (wrong passphrase / corrupt — generic, no oracle) or `RESTORE_FAILED`. |
| GET / PUT | `/api/theme` | Household theme. `GET` returns `{primary_color, accent_color, surface_color, surface_dark, mode, font_family, density, corner_radius, updated_at}`. `PUT` (admin) takes a partial body of those fields: colours `#RRGGBB` (`surface_*` nullable), `mode` ∈ `light` \| `dark` \| `auto`, `font_family` ∈ `system` \| `serif` \| `rounded` \| `mono`, `density` ∈ `compact` \| `comfortable` \| `spacious`, `corner_radius` 0–24. A bad value is `422 INVALID_THEME` naming the field and the allowed values; nothing is saved. |
| GET | `/api/household/spaces` | Spaces › Discover › Your household: the spaces **this household hosts** that the caller may find, sorted by name — every one they hold a seat in; a `household` space they are not in (they left or were removed — anyone at home may join it again, instantly, via `POST /api/spaces/{id}/join-requests`); a `public` / `global` space they are not in only when its `join_mode` is `open` / `request` and it isn't archived. Never a `private` space, a household-tier space another household hosts, a space the caller is banned from, or (for a protected minor) one below its `min_age`. Each row: `{space_id, name, description, emoji, space_type, join_mode, category, allow_subscribers, min_age, member_count, role, local_area}` — `local_area` is a [local community](./protocol/local-communities.md)'s `{lat, lon, radius_km}` or `null`; `member_count` is the space's member count — see *Member count* above — `role` the caller's seat (`null` = none). |
| GET / PUT | `/api/household/preferences` | Household-wide feature toggles plus `household_name` and `tz` (IANA timezone). `PUT` accepts a partial body: `{"household_name"?: str, "toggles"?: {"feat_feed": bool, "feat_pages": bool, "feat_tasks": bool, "feat_stickies": bool, "feat_calendar": bool, "feat_bazaar": bool, "feat_presence": bool, "feat_gallery": bool, "feat_timetable": bool, "feat_household_chat": bool, "allow_link_preview": bool, "share_home_default": bool, …}, "tz"?: "<iana>"}` (every `allow_*` post-type toggle is accepted too). `share_home_default` (default `true`) — "share our home location with households we pair with": pre-sets the pairing dialog's switch and the `share_home` of `POST /api/pairing/initiate` / `accept` when they don't say, and is the `share_home` of a pair made through an introducer (auto-pair); changing it never touches an existing pair. `tz` is validated via Python's `zoneinfo` — an unknown name returns 422. In ha / haos modes the value is mirrored from HA Core's `time_zone` on adapter startup; explicit operator edits via PUT are still honoured but get overwritten on the next restart. |
| GET / PATCH | `/api/me/preferences` | Per-user sidebar and privacy preferences. `GET` returns `{user_id, hide_highlights, hide_momentum, hide_bazaar, hide_profile_from_followers}`. `PATCH` accepts a partial body of booleans from `{hide_highlights, hide_momentum, hide_bazaar, hide_profile_from_followers}` — a non-boolean value returns 400; other keys are ignored. `hide_profile_from_followers` (default `false`) turns off "Show my picture and bio to followers of global spaces": the person's author profile for a space's GFS followers carries the name only, and flipping it republishes the profile where it was published (see [protocol/discovery.md](./protocol/discovery.md#author-profiles-for-followers-v_60)). Changes apply only to the authenticated user; other household members are unaffected. |
| POST | `/api/media/upload` | Upload a blob. Images/audio return `{url, filename, signed_url}` once processed. **Video transcoding is async** — the upload returns immediately with `{url, thumbnail_url, filename, media_status:"processing", signed_url, signed_thumbnail_url}` before the `.webm`/`.webp` files exist; a background `MediaTranscodeService` worker produces them and pushes a `media.ready` WS frame to the uploader when done. The `.webm` output and its `.webp` poster share one UUID stem (`<stem>.webm` + `<stem>.webp`) so the poster path is derivable server-side from the media URL. List endpoints that surface the video (feed, space feed, momentum, DM messages) carry a `media_status` field (`'processing'` / `'failed'` / `'ready'`; absent ⇒ ready) **and** a signed `media_thumbnail_url` (the poster — the signed `.webp` sibling of `media_url`) on **video** items; the `media.created`/`space.post.created` WS frames carry the same poster. (Gallery items keep their own stored `thumbnail_url`.) |
| GET | `/api/media/{filename}` | Download a blob. Script-capable / unknown types (SVG, HTML, XML, JS, audio / video the server didn't produce itself…) are sent as an `application/octet-stream` attachment; every response carries a sandboxing CSP (see `docs/architecture.md`, "Security headers"). |
| GET | `/api/map/config` | Map rendering config for the SPA: `{tile_url, attribution, max_zoom}`. `tile_url` is a **relative** (no leading slash, so it resolves against the ingress-aware `<base href>`) signed template — `api/map/tiles?z={z}&x={x}&y={y}&exp=…&sig=…` — with the literal `{z}`/`{x}`/`{y}` placeholders left for Leaflet to substitute. One HMAC (7-day TTL) authorises every tile, because the signature covers only the path. 503 `SIGNER_UNAVAILABLE` if the media signer is not wired yet (never an unsigned URL). |
| GET | `/api/map/tiles?z=&x=&y=` | Raster tile proxy. OpenStreetMap 403s browser-issued tile requests (a browser cannot set `User-Agent` / `Referer`), so the backend fetches with an identifying agent string and caches in memory. Auth via the signed `?exp=&sig=` above (Leaflet's `<img>` carries no bearer header) or a normal bearer token. Responds with the tile bytes, the upstream content type, and `Cache-Control: public, max-age=604800`. 400 on missing / non-integer / out-of-grid coordinates, 502 `TILE_UNAVAILABLE` when upstream fails and nothing cached can stand in. Upstream is `map_tile_url` / `SH_MAP_TILE_URL`. |
| GET | `/healthz` | Liveness (public). |

## HFS — WebSockets

| Path | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/ws` | Realtime event stream — posts, comments, presence, typing, calls, notifications. Auth via `Authorization: Bearer` or `?token=`. Frames: `"ping"` → `"pong"`; client `{"type":"typing","conversation_id":"..."}` (or `{post_id, space_id?}` for a comment thread — with a `space_id` the sender must hold a seat in that space, else nothing is sent); client `{"type":"dm.active","data":{"conversation_id":"..."|null}}` — this tab shows that DM thread (no bell row / push for it), or `"app:<app_id>"` while an app is on screen (no "Your turn" row for it); `null` when it goes to the background or closes.
