Envelopes that arrived while the household was offline are drained, oldest first, immediately after the hello verifies — together with any queued member-published items, which drain as the same `{type:"relay", space_id, event_type:"space_item", epoch, writer_cert, payload}` frame a live subscriber gets from `POST /gfs/member-publish` (v_49; identity-free — the publisher is authenticated by the cert and the author signature inside, never by an outer field). It also pushes public-content RTC signalling to the author: `{type:"highlight_signal", kind}` with `kind` ∈ `offer` / `ice` / `relay_offer` (the `relay_offer` carries `{relay_id, highlight_id, token}` for the GFS-relay fallback). On an operator rename it pushes `{type:"server_info_updated", server_name}` to every connected client (on a cluster, on every node), which re-fetches `GET /gfs/info` to update the cached server name in real time (the reconnect-refresh path is the fallback). First client frame must be a signed hello `{type:"hello", instance_id, ts, sig}` within 5 s — see spec §24.12. WebSocket close codes: 4400 protocol violation, 4401 auth failure, 4408 hello timeout, 4409 replaced. An unknown / inactive instance and a bad signature both close 4401 with the one reason `auth-failed` after the same Ed25519 verify work (older GFS builds sent `unknown-instance` / `bad-signature`), so the hello is no registration oracle. Heartbeat is the WS-protocol-level ping (30 s). A hello whose `accepts` lists **`ws_binary1`** gets, on that socket only, every push frame that carries a listed ciphertext (`relay` items, `envelope`, `incoming_public_moment`) as a **binary** frame: `version (1) | u16 header length | JSON header (the frame with the ciphertext cut to its `nonce:` prefix, plus `bin_path`) | raw ciphertext bytes`; the household rebuilds the exact base64url string before verifying anything (`domain/ws_binary_frame.py`). Other sockets keep text frames. It pushes `{type:"moment_reaction", from_instance, sealed}` (v_68) to an author's household only — a follower household's sealed reaction posted to `POST /gfs/moments/users/{user_id}/react`, live, through the node holding the socket, or drained from its 24 h queue after the hello; an older household ignores it. Right after the hello (v_66) it pushes `{type:"moment_delete_certs", certs:[…]}` — the signed delete certificates (`POST /gfs/moments/delete_cert`) of public-moment authors a user of that household follows, written after the follow began, within 30 days, past that household's per-author delivery cursor (opened at its last-seen mark minus 2 h; never skipped by a later mark): ≤ 200 a push and ≤ 100 of one author (round-robin), ≤ 50 a frame, another push a minute later while more is owed; an older household ignores the frame. A hello whose `accepts` lists **`space_item_no_cert`** gets, on that socket only, `space_item` frames without the plaintext `writer_cert` (the follower verifies the cert inside the ciphertext); see `docs/protocol/discovery.md`. |

## GFS — Cluster

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/cluster/sync` | Cluster-node state sync: a signed `NODE_*` frame `{type, from, to, ts, nonce, sig_suite, payload}` (canonical JSON; `to` the recipient's node id, `ts` unix seconds, `nonce` 16 random bytes b64url, `sig_suite` `"ed25519"`) with the Ed25519 signature over those exact bytes in `X-Node-Signature`. Frame types: `NODE_HELLO`, `NODE_HEARTBEAT`, `NODE_SYNC_CLIENT`, `NODE_SYNC_SPACE`, `NODE_SYNC_REPORT`, `NODE_RELAY`, `NODE_POLICY_PUSH`, `NODE_PARTITION_CATCHUP`, `NODE_PARTITION_GAP` and `NODE_DRAIN_HINT` — `{instances:[<instance id>…]}` (≤ 256 ids), sent fire-and-forget after the sender queued an envelope or member item for those households in the shared `gfs_envelope_queue`; the receiver drains each listed household it holds a `/gfs/ws` socket for (malformed ids and ids not connected here are ignored). The same frame may carry `relays:[{aud:[kind, key], skip, q, frame}…]` (≤ 64 items, ≤ 768 KiB): broadcast relay items the sender accepted (`/gfs/publish` broadcasts, member publishes, channel items, public moments and their tombstones), forwarded ONCE per peer node; the receiver resolves the audience (`space` → the space's active subscribers, `channel` → the channel's open-epoch seats, `followers` → the author's follower households that can open its moments, `instance` → the one household named) from the shared DB, pushes `{type:"relay", …frame}` — for `followers` and `instance` items the frame's own `event_type` as `type`, only `incoming_public_moment` / `incoming_public_moment_delete` resp. `follow_changed` / `moment_key_request` / `highlight_signal` / `moment_reaction` — to the members it holds a socket for (never `skip`, never twice for the same frame bytes within 10 min — 25 h for `followers` items — except `instance` items) and never forwards again. The listed `instances` also have their undelivered sealed moment keys pushed (shared mailbox). It then answers `{status:"ok", held:{"<kind>:<key>":[<instance id>…]}}` — for `q` items the audience members it holds — so the sender queues offline copies only for the recently-seen subscribers no node holds. An older node ignores `relays` and answers without `held`. A node sends at most one `NODE_DRAIN_HINT` per 1.5 s, so the stream stays inside the 60 / min budget however busy it is. A frame from an older node omits `to`, `nonce` and `sig_suite`; a missing suite means Ed25519 and a missing `to` is accepted (as is a HELLO to a configured peer URL whose node id the sender does not know yet) — **deprecated** for non-HELLO frames: refused once every node runs the release after the first one that shipped `to`; `[cluster] peers` URLs are normalised at load like stored URLs, so a HELLO to a known peer always names it — the compatibility tripwire, like the suite. **Membership:** a node is a member only if its frames verify under a key this GFS already holds — its own identity key (the shared seed the operator gave the node) or the key an operator approved with `POST /admin/api/cluster/peers` (`cluster_nodes.approved_key`; the legacy `public_key` column is never trusted). There is no trust-on-first-use. Checked in this order (a source address that has spent its unverified budget is *shed*: its frames take the same checks, but every rejection below is answered 429 instead): not a JSON object → 400 `invalid_json`; `type` / `from` missing or not a non-empty string, `payload` not an object (absent reads as `{}`; `[]`, `0`, `null` are malformed), or `to` not a string → 400 `invalid_message` (the sender id is the signed `from` only — no header fallback); unknown `sig_suite` → 400 `unsupported_sig_suite`; `ts` not an integer → 400 `invalid_timestamp`; `ts` more than ±300 s from the receiver's clock, or before this process started → 401 `stale_timestamp`; `to` present and not this node's id → 409 `wrong_recipient`; byte-identical frame already accepted (remembered until its `ts` + 301 s on the wall clock; a frame whose `ts` is at or below the newest one the cache has forgotten counts as seen) → 409 `replay`; a non-HELLO frame from a node with no row → 403 `unknown_node`, or whose row is no member (neither an approved key nor this GFS's own key on it — e.g. a row an old-version node wrote by trust-on-first-use) → 403 `unapproved_node`; a `NODE_HELLO` under a key the GFS does not hold → 403 `unapproved_node` (nothing is written); a `NODE_HELLO` for a known node under a key other than its approved key → 403 `key_mismatch` (logged at WARNING; an approval never moves in-band — rotate by removing and re-adding the peer); bad signature → 401 `invalid_signature` (429 from a shed address, like every rejection there; from a shed address the verify runs only while the 30 / min failed-verify budget of the (named node, source address) pair and the node's 1200 / min ceiling across addresses last, else 429 without a verify); then the per-node rate limit → 429 (see Rate limits); then, if the node's share of the replay cache (660 live digests; there is no total cap) has no room, 503 `replay_cache_full` with `Retry-After: 60` — a live digest is never evicted to make room. A first `NODE_HELLO` under this GFS's own key for a node with no row while 32 shared-seed siblings (rows with no approval holding this GFS's own key; approved rows and non-member rows do not count) already have rows → 403 `cluster_full` (nothing is written); a sibling row unseen for 300 s (10 heartbeat intervals) is dropped by the heartbeat loop, so allocation churn never fills the roster for good — an approved row is never dropped. Every failure before the rate limit spends the source address's budget, never a node's verified budget, and a frame that verifies under a member's key is accepted even from a shed address — junk sent from a member's address cannot lock it out. A HELLO never changes a row's URL once it has one (the operator's URL wins); only a row with no URL yet takes the HELLO's `url`, and only if it is a valid base URL (else it stays empty and the HELLO is not answered). A HELLO from a node whose row was never seen (`last_seen` empty, e.g. just admin-added) is answered with our own HELLO, so two approvals converge in either order. **Residual risks:** a HELLO to a configured URL the sender has no row for yet carries no `to`, so within its 300 s window it can be replayed once to each other node (each replay cache is per node; the worst effect is a `last_seen` refresh); an attacker who can send from a member's own address and forge more than 30 frames a minute naming it — or forge 1200 a minute naming it from many shed addresses while the member's own address is shed — delays that member until the window slides (cluster nodes should set an explicit `trusted_proxies`); nodes whose clocks drift more than 300 s apart refuse each other's frames and partition (run NTP); two nodes configured with the same `node_id` are indistinguishable (an empty `node_id` falls back to `instance_id`; a node announcing our `node_id` from another address is flagged — see `GET /admin/api/cluster`). `NODE_HELLO` and `NODE_HEARTBEAT` payloads carry the sender's `instance_id` and `instance_id_aliases` (additive; older nodes neither send nor read them): a sibling whose frame verified under this node's own identity key and reports a different id is flagged in `GET /admin/api/cluster` — never a `/healthz` failure. |
| GET | `/cluster/health` | Node health: this node's `node_id` and `status`, and its member peers (`node_id`, `status`, `last_seen`). Never a peer URL — with `[cluster] advertise_url` it is an internal node address, so URLs are admin-only (`GET /admin/api/cluster`). Only cluster members are listed — a row with an approved key or our own identity key; a row an old-version node wrote by trust-on-first-use is never shown. |
| POST | `/cluster/signaling-session` | **Legacy.** Pick a least-loaded signaling node for a sync session (spec §24.10.7). Kept for older households; current households never call it (a sync tells the GFS nothing — see `protocol/sync.md`). |
| POST | `/cluster/signaling-session/release` | **Legacy.** Release a signaling session on `SPACE_SYNC_DIRECT_READY` / `DIRECT_FAILED`. Older households only. |

## GFS — Admin portal

**Portal**

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/admin` | SPA entrypoint. |
| GET | `/admin/static/{path}` | SPA assets. |
| POST | `/admin/login` | Bcrypt-verified login. |
| POST | `/admin/logout` | End session. |

**Admin API** (all require an active admin cookie session)

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/admin/api/overview` | Dashboard stats, plus `fanout`: frames and bytes this node pushed to household sockets since it started (`total`, `by_kind`, `by_size`). |
| GET | `/admin/api/clients` | Registered instances. |
| POST | `/admin/api/clients/{instance_id}/{accept\|reject\|ban}` | Moderate instances. |
| GET | `/admin/api/spaces` | Published spaces, including owner-withdrawn ones (each row carries `withdrawn`). |
| POST | `/admin/api/spaces/{space_id}/{accept\|reject\|ban}` | Moderate spaces. |
| GET / PATCH | `/admin/api/policy` | Operator policy. |
| GET / PATCH | `/admin/api/branding` | Branding text. |
| POST / DELETE | `/admin/api/branding/header-image` | Header image. |
| GET | `/admin/api/reports` | Report queue. |
| PATCH | `/admin/api/reports/{id}/review` | Decide. |
| GET | `/admin/api/appeals` | Appeal queue. |
| PATCH | `/admin/api/appeals/{id}/decide` | Decide. |
| GET | `/admin/api/audit` | Audit log. |
| GET | `/admin/api/cluster` | Cluster status — `{node_id, public_key, instance_id, instance_id_mismatches, instance_id_transitional, duplicate_node_id_urls, status, nodes}`. `public_key` is this node's own identity key (hex Ed25519): what an operator approves for this node on every other node. `instance_id` is this node's public id (`[server] instance_id`); `instance_id_mismatches` lists `[{node_id, instance_id}]` for each sibling under the same identity key that recently (300 s) reported another, unlinked one (logged at ERROR); `instance_id_transitional` the same shape for a sibling whose id is linked to ours through either side's `instance_id_aliases` — a rolling id change (WARNING); `duplicate_node_id_urls` the addresses that recently announced THIS node's `node_id` (ERROR — two nodes share one `node_id`, e.g. an empty one falling back to the shared `instance_id`). None of them fails `/healthz`. Operator action: the same `instance_id` on every node, old ids in `instance_id_aliases`, a unique `node_id` per node. Each `nodes[]` entry carries `node_id`, `url`, `status`, `last_seen`, `connected_clients`, `active_sync_sessions`, `is_self`, `public_key` and `key_source` — `approved` (`public_key` is the key an admin approved), `own` (this GFS's identity key: approved for it, or a shared-seed sibling's HELLO verified under it) or `none` (no approved key and no sign of the shared seed; `public_key` is empty, only this GFS's own key can verify its frames — a peer with its own key must be re-added after upgrading, since GFS migration 0017 starts every existing row unapproved). |
| GET | `/admin/api/cluster/peers[/{node_id}]` | Peer list / detail. |
| POST | `/admin/api/cluster/peers` | Approve a peer node. Body `{node_id, url, public_key}`: the node's unique id (1–128 chars of `[A-Za-z0-9._:/-]` — ids like `gfs-node-0`, UUIDs, `host:port` or URL-shaped ids; no whitespace, control, bidi or other non-ASCII characters — and not this node's), its base URL (`http`/`https` with a host; no userinfo, query or fragment; no whitespace, control, bidi or other format character anywhere, no non-ASCII outside the host, a host IDNA can encode and a port in 1–65535; not a link-local or cloud-metadata address — `169.254.0.0/16`, `100.100.100.200`, `fe80::/10`, `fd00:ec2::254`, also as an IPv4-mapped / -compatible / SIIT-translated / NAT64 IPv6 or legacy numeric IPv4 spelling, anything in the local-use NAT64 range `64:ff9b:1::/48`, or a metadata host name (`metadata.google.internal`, `metadata`, `instance-data`, `instance-data.ec2.internal`); an IP address must be written in ASCII (a fullwidth or circled-digit host that IDNA maps onto one is refused) — while RFC 1918, ULA and loopback addresses are fine; stored re-serialised, IDNA-encoded and lower-cased) and its 64-hex-char Ed25519 identity key (the `public_key` its own `GET /admin/api/cluster` shows; a small-order point — under which anyone could forge its frames — is `invalid_public_key`). 422 `{error}` with `invalid_node_id` / `node_id_is_self` / `invalid_url` / `invalid_public_key`; 409 `key_mismatch` when the node is already approved under a different key — also when a concurrent approval under another key won the race; the stored key and URL are then left exactly as the winner wrote them (rotate by deleting and re-adding); 201 `{node_id, url, public_key}` on success, after which this node HELLOs the peer. Re-adding the same key only refreshes the URL. |
| DELETE | `/admin/api/cluster/peers/{node_id}` | Remove a peer. Removing a shared-seed sibling (a node holding this GFS's own key) is temporary: it rejoins with its next `NODE_HELLO`. An approved peer stays out until it is added again. |
| POST | `/admin/api/cluster/peers/{node_id}/ping` | Healthcheck a peer. `409 not_a_member` for a row that is neither approved nor a shared-key sibling (its address was never validated, so nothing is sent). |

## GFS — Public SSR pages

| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/` | Operator landing page: welcome text, the pairing QR and — unless the operator sets `[server] landing_space_list = false` (`GFS_LANDING_SPACE_LIST`) — the list of active spaces with search (`?search=`) and category filter (`?category=`). With the list off those parameters are ignored and no space is named; `/spaces/{id}` and `GET /gfs/spaces` are unchanged. |
| GET | `/spaces/{slug}` | Public space detail. With `[server] public_viewer_base_url` set (default `https://viewer.social-home.io`) a `200` hand-off page — the space's `og:` / `twitter:` tags (name, description, `og:image` of `/gfs/pages/spaces/{id}/image`; generic for an unlisted id) and a `<meta http-equiv="refresh">` to the pinned viewer, `{viewer}/v/<hash>/#v=space&g=<base_url>&s=<id>` (`Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`) — which renders it from `GET /gfs/pages/spaces/{id}`; an id the viewer would refuse (outside `A-Za-z0-9._~-`, over 128 chars, only dots) is never forwarded and renders here. Empty / unusable setting: rendered here as before. |
| GET | `/join/{gfs_token}` | **Invite-link landing.** Renders the space's already-public directory metadata (name, icon, accent) next to the `socialhome://invite#<blob>` code — as copyable text **and** as a QR of the same string — which the visitor pastes into their OWN Social Home (Spaces → Join with invite code). There is deliberately no clickable deep link: the redeem has to happen on the visitor's household, and a link can only ever open the issuer's. **The page writes nothing to the database** — no use counter, no fetch row, and the handler adds no log line of its own; the connection server must never become a durable record of who redeemed what (the `uses` / `max_uses` columns on `gfs_invite_tokens` are dead by design). The residual is the HTTP access log, which records `GET /join/<token>` with the visitor's IP — signed off in [`principles.md`](principles.md); operators who care should redact the `/join/` path at the front end and keep retention short. 404 — with the same styled "expired or revoked" page — when the token is unknown, expired, revoked, or its space is no longer publicly listed; one answer for all of them, so the page is not an existence oracle. With a viewer configured it is a `200` hand-off page with GENERIC preview tags and a refresh to `{viewer}/v/<hash>/#v=join&g=<base_url>&t=<token>` **before** the token is looked up (every token gets the same page), the token only in the fragment; the viewer reads `POST /gfs/pages/invite`. |
| GET | `/highlight/{instance_id}/{highlight_id}/{token}` | Public highlight viewer shell. Always `200` with the same page and `Cache-Control: no-store` (the URL carries the share token): no token lookup, no author-online check (not a presence oracle). The viewer's `POST /gfs/highlight_rtc/offer` consults state. |
| GET | `/moments` | Public-Momentum directory (SPA shell). Loads `/static/users_directory.js`, which fetches `GET /gfs/moments/users` and renders cards + search. |
| GET | `/moments/{user_id}` | Per-user landing — avatar + display_name + bio + follower count + "Follow on your Social Home" deeplink. With a viewer configured a `200` hand-off page — display name, bio and picture (`/gfs/moments/users/{id}/picture`) as preview tags for an active registration, generic otherwise — refreshing to `{viewer}/v/<hash>/#v=person&g=<base_url>&u=<id>`; the viewer reads `GET /gfs/pages/people/{user_id}`. |
| GET | `/gfs/pages/spaces/{space_id}` | Anon, CORS (below). The space page's content for the pinned viewer: `{server_name, space: {space_id, name, description, about_markdown (≤ 4000 chars), cover, icon, primary_color, accent_color, member_count, category}}`. `cover` / `icon` are the stored raster `data:` URIs or `null` (anything else — an `https://` URL, an SVG — is never passed on); colours plain hex. `404 {server_name}` for anything not listed (unknown, pending, banned, withdrawn). |
| GET | `/gfs/pages/spaces/{space_id}/image` | Anon. A listed space's stored raster cover (else icon), decoded, for link previews (`Cache-Control: public, max-age=3600`, media CSP, inline); `404` for anything unlisted or without a raster `data:` image. In the pages' rate-limit window. |
| POST | `/gfs/pages/invite` | Anon, CORS. Body `{token}` (≤ 256 chars, body ≤ 1 KiB; `400` / `413` otherwise) — a POST so the token never sits in a request line. `{server_name, space: {name, accent_color, icon}, invite_code, qr}` (`qr` a PNG `data:` URI rendered here, `Cache-Control: no-store`), or `404 {server_name}` for every dead link, as `GET /join/{token}`. A pure read: nothing written, counted or logged. |
| GET | `/gfs/pages/people/{user_id}` | Anon, CORS. A person's profile for the viewer: `{server_name, gfs_id, person: {user_id, username, display_name, instance_id, bio, picture, follower_count}}`, `picture` the mirrored avatar as a `data:` URI or `null`. `404 {server_name}` without an active registration. |

These pages are server-rendered HTML and require no auth.

## Rate limits

| Endpoint | Limit |
|---|---|
| `POST /api/presence/location` | 10 / min per person posted (300 / min per token as a ceiling) |
| `POST /api/calls` | 10 / min / user |
| `POST /api/calls/{id}/decline` | 10 / min / user |
| `POST /api/calls/{id}/hangup` | 30 / min / user |
| `POST /api/calls/{id}/ice` | 300 / min / user — every leg of a group-call mesh trickles several candidates within seconds |
| `POST /api/calls/{id}/answer` | 60 / min / user — a group callee answers the ring plus one leg per other callee |
| `POST /api/calls/{id}/join` | 30 / min / user |
| `POST /api/calls/{id}/quality` | 30 / min / user — the call page samples every 10 s; on the broad `/api/calls` bucket it starved `join` |
| `GET /api/calls/ice-servers` | 30 / min / user |
| `/api/pairing/*` (handshakes: `initiate`, `accept`, `confirm`, `introduce`, auto-pair, relay requests) | 5 / min / user |
| `/api/pairing/pending/*` (cancelling a pending pairing) | 30 / min / user — its own bucket, like connection management: an admin clearing a few stale cards in a row must not hit the handshake bucket. |
| `/api/pairing/connections/*` (list, per-peer reads, `PATCH` toggles, unpair) | 30 / min / user — its own bucket: opening one connection's Manage panel already spends two requests, so the 5 / min handshake bucket 429'd after two opens and a switch. The longest matching prefix decides the bucket. |
| `/api/spaces/*` (reads and every write without its own rule) | 600 / min / user — opening one space spends ~10 requests (detail, members, feed, theme, moderation, proposals, notification prefs, chat summary, …), so the 60 / min default 429'd the ~6th space opened in a minute |
| `POST /api/spaces` (create), `POST /api/spaces/join`, `POST /api/spaces/{id}/join-requests`, `POST` / `DELETE /api/spaces/{id}/subscribe` | 20 / min / user each — method rules: create shares its path with the list, so the limiter matches method and whole path |
| `POST /api/spaces/{id}/invite-tokens`, `POST /api/spaces/{id}/remote-invites` | 30 / min / user each |
| `POST /api/spaces/{id}/ban` | 5 / min / user |
| `PATCH /api/highlights/{id}/audience` | 6 / hour / user — each change can send the day's frames (≤ 30) to every household it adds, so one author can cause at most 6 × 30 envelopes per added household an hour |
| `POST /api/apps/{app_id}/messages` | 300 / min / user — its own bucket (a quick game's moves no longer share the 60 / min `/api/apps` default with the store writes around them); each send is one envelope to one household. |
| `GET /api/apps/{app_id}/store`, `GET / PUT / DELETE …/store/{key}` | 300 / min / user — its own bucket, sized like the moves its writes go with (a fast game saves around each shot); each call touches one key of the caller's own data, ≤ 64 KiB, local only. |
| `POST /api/link-preview` | 30 / min / user — on top, the service caps **fresh page fetches** (cache misses, including the ones a post create causes) at 20 / 5 min per member and 60 / 5 min per household; over budget the answer is simply "no card". |
| `POST /unlock` (household dashboard port) | 5 wrong PINs / 15 min per lock — the bucket is the locked session's id (minted by `/lock`, kept by cookie renewal), never the client address: tablets behind one reverse proxy don't share it, and rotating addresses or renewing the cookie doesn't reset it. Separate from the password bucket; never feeds the global brake. The wrong PIN that reaches the limit (or any try past it) signs the tablet out (303 `/login?signed_out=1`) and revokes the session; a hold-back for any other reason only asks it to wait (303 `/?pin=wait`). |
| `POST /ack` (household dashboard port) | 30 / 5 min per session (the session id, like the PIN bucket) — far above any household; caps writes from a stuck or scripted tablet. 429 + `Retry-After`. |
| `POST /api/display/v1/unlock` (household dashboard port, bearer) | 5 wrong PINs / 15 min per paired panel (its id — a fresh bucket needs an admin to pair it again). Separate from the tablets' buckets; never feeds the global brake. The wrong PIN that reaches the limit (or any try past it) unpairs the panel (401 `DISPLAY_SIGNED_OUT`); a hold-back for any other reason is 429 + `Retry-After`. In memory: a restart forgets it, as for tablets. |
| `POST /api/display/v1/unlock/ha` (household dashboard port, bearer) | 6 / 5 min per paired panel (its id), counted before the admin setting is checked — refused calls (403 `DISPLAY_HA_UNLOCK_OFF`) count too — caps a looping automation or a scripted lock / unlock toggle. Separate from the PIN bucket. 429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After`. In memory: a restart forgets it. |
| `POST /api/display/v1/ack` (household dashboard port, bearer) | 30 / 5 min per paired panel, the tablet's ack budget. 429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After`. |
| `/api/display/v1/*` (household dashboard port, bearer) | Per paired panel: 120 JSON requests / min (every bearer request but the images, including `lock` / `unlock` / `ack` and a locked panel's 423s) and, separately, 600 images (`avatar` / `emoji` / `map.png`) / min — a panel polls a board every 30 s and loads images when they change. Of those, at most 6 map pictures / min may be freshly painted (a new size, scheme or map); a kept picture or a 304 doesn't count. 429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After`. |
| `POST /api/display/v1/pair` (household dashboard port) | 10 / 10 min per client address (socket peer) — a panel needs one per pairing attempt; stops a flood pushing real panels' codes out of the 32 waiting. 429 + `Retry-After`. |
| `POST /api/display/v1/pair/claim` (household dashboard port) | 10 / 10 min per client address (socket peer), a bucket of its own — code-pairing tries can't use up what a panel needs to spend its claim in time. Claims are 256-bit, so this only caps the work of a flood. 429 + `Retry-After`. |
| `POST /api/admin/dashboard/displays` (wrong pairing codes) | 5 wrong codes / 15 min — one bucket for every admin (the code is what's guessed, not the account); counted before the check, cleared by a right code. Never feeds the dashboard's global login brake. 429 `DASHBOARD_PAIRING_THROTTLED`. |
| `POST /login`, `POST /enter` (household dashboard port) | 5 failed attempts / 15 min per client address (socket peer, never `X-Forwarded-For`); `/login` also 30 failures / 15 min across all clients — the global brake (a valid setup token neither feeds nor obeys it). Counted in memory by `DashboardAuth` (survives a port change, not a restart); one password check at a time. Behind a reverse proxy every tablet shares one client bucket. |
| `GET /api/media/{filename}`, `GET /api/users/{id}/picture`, `GET /api/spaces/{id}/members/{user_id}/picture`, `GET /api/spaces/{id}/cover`, `GET /api/spaces/{id}/icon` | 600 / min each — per **user** for a bearer caller, per **client address** (IPv6 by /64) for a signed-URL `<img>` fetch. A signed URL authenticates as one constant principal, so these used to share a single household-wide 60 / min bucket per path family and one Friends page of ~22 avatars 429'd on its third load. Behind the HA ingress proxy every browser has the proxy's address, so the bucket is household-wide there. |
| `GET /api/gfs/{gfs_id}/moments/users/{user_id}/picture` | 300 / min — one shared bucket for signed-URL fetches (like map tiles): every hit is an upstream fetch from the GFS, so a leaked URL must not multiply it per client. |
| `GET /api/map/tiles` | 1200 / min — one shared bucket: every Leaflet `<img>` authenticates as the signed-URL principal, and a desktop viewport is ~20 tiles. Still a ceiling, so a leaked signed URL can't drive unbounded upstream traffic from the household IP. |
| `POST /cluster/sync` | 60 / min per **verified** peer node — counted only after the frame verified under the key this GFS holds for that node (its pin, or the GFS's own key for a shared-seed node), never on the `from` a request claims, so forged traffic naming a real peer cannot lock it out. Every request that proves no member — malformed body, unknown suite, stale timestamp, unknown or unapproved sender, key mismatch, bad signature, or a replay — spends a separate 30 / min per **source address** budget instead (wrong recipient included). Once that is spent the address is shed: its frames are still parsed and matched against the roster (no crypto), anything not naming an approved node gets 429, and a frame naming one is verified — accepted if it verifies, else it spends that node's 30 / min **failed-verify** budget, after which further such frames get 429 without a verify. Verify CPU on forgeries is therefore bounded per address, then per approved node, and the node's own budget is never spent by a forgery. Genuine peers never touch the address budget. Behind a shared reverse proxy configure `trusted_proxies` (below) so the budget keys on the real client. |
| `POST /cluster/signaling-session{,/release}` (legacy) | 60 / min / paired instance |
| `GET /` + `GET /spaces/{id}` + `GET /join/{token}` + `/gfs/pages/*` (GFS public pages and their viewer JSON, one window — a page opened through the viewer costs two) | 30 / min / IP, cluster-wide (see the note below the table) — `/join/` rides the same window: it is the page an attacker would hammer to walk the token space, and since it writes nothing there is no household identity to key a limiter on. |
| `GET /api/invite-links/{token}/code` | 30 / min / IP — the endpoint is unauthenticated (the token IS the credential), so the client address is the only handle. Not brute-force protection: a token is a uuid4 hex and is not guessable. It is ordinary anonymous-endpoint shedding, and one visitor legitimately hits it once per link they open. Live and dead tokens answer identically apart from the status code. |
| `POST /gfs/spaces/{id}/invite` | 20 / min / **instance** — the mint is signature-authenticated, so the accountable identity is known before anything is written and the limiter keys on the household rather than on an address it could rotate. Far above a human minting invites, while bounding how many rows one household can park on a server per minute. |
| `POST /gfs/{highlight,moment}_rtc/offer` + the anon `/relay/*` GETs | 20 / min / IP, cluster-wide — bounds the WS-push amplification from the anonymous RTC entry points (live slots per address and per author are capped too, see [highlights_public.md](protocol/highlights_public.md)) |
| `GET /gfs/highlights/ice-servers` | 30 / min / IP, cluster-wide — each answer mints a short-lived TURN credential, so this bounds how many one address can collect |
| `POST /gfs/register` | 10 / min / IP — a household registers once per pairing; sheds token-guessing floods before the token lookup. |
| `POST /gfs/proof` | 20 / min / IP — a household asks once at pairing and at most once per 5 minutes per connection on GFS-WebSocket (re)connects. Per-IP only: one Ed25519 signature is cheap, and a server-wide cap would let one flooder stop every household from proving the server. |
| `POST /gfs/signup-token` | 5 / min / IP **and** 30 / min across all addresses (the global window is the sybil brake that does not depend on believing a client address), shed before any database work; on top, the token service hands one token per address per 30 s. |
| `POST /gfs/publish` | 120 / min / IP — the relay is authorized by the space-authority signature alone and carries no household identity, so the per-IP window is the only shedding handle, set far above a real household's publish rate (one per public post / subscriber key handoff, per GFS). Bodies over 256 KiB are refused with 413 before being read; a byte-identical payload replayed inside 5 min is a 200 no-op that fans out nothing, and one relay's fan-out is capped at 8 s (below the household's 10 s client timeout). |
| `POST /gfs/channels/*` (v_51) | 120 / min / IP before any signature work (shared window); registration 10 / min per client address; trusted publish 30 / min per (household, channel) and 120 / min per channel; anonymous publish 30 / min per (channel, client address), 120 / min per channel and per writer key; an epoch notice ahead of its time allowance is a 429 with `Retry-After`. Control bodies over 16 KiB, publishes over 256 KiB → 413. |
| `POST /gfs/moments/publish`, `POST /gfs/moments/delete` | 30 / min per author household (one shared budget, counted once the household signature, shape, author and key step are checked), cluster-wide: each node allows its share over the live nodes. 429 with `Retry-After: 60`. |
| `POST /gfs/moments/delete_cert` | 30 / min per author household (its own budget, so a delete's two posts don't halve the one above), counted once the certificate, author and delete time are checked, cluster-wide (each node its share). 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After: 60`; and ≤ 1 000 live certificates per author (429 `author_delete_quota`). |
| `POST /gfs/moments/users/{user_id}/react` | v_68. 30 / min per (follower household, author) and 120 / min per follower household, cluster-wide (each node its share), counted once the follow and the shape are checked and only for a seal not relayed in the last 10 min (a retry is free). 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After: 60`. Reactions queued for an offline author household: ≤ 60 at a time, the oldest making room; dropped (still `202`) rather than evict its queued moments or space items. The author's household opens ≤ 600 `moment_reaction` frames a minute per GFS connection and applies ≤ 60 a minute per reactor household — charged only for a reaction that changed something (a replay is free). |
| `POST /gfs/moments/counts` | v_69. 10 / min per author household (each node its share) and 3 / day per moment (every node counts the updates it is forwarded too), cluster-wide; a retried post spends nothing. `429` + `Retry-After: 60`. |
| `POST /gfs/member-publish`, `POST /gfs/member-publish-anon`, `POST /gfs/spaces/{id}/epoch` | 120 / min / IP before any signature work (shared window); on `/gfs/member-publish-anon` 30 / min per (space, client address), 120 / min per space and 120 / min per writer key once `writer_sig` verified, and an exact replay inside 10 min is a 403; plus 30 / min per (household, space) and 120 / min per space on `/gfs/member-publish` once the household signature has verified (429 with `Retry-After: 60`). Bodies over 256 KiB are refused with 413 before being read; a byte-identical member publish replayed inside 5 min is a 200 no-op. |
| `POST /gfs/envelope` | 600 / min / IP — the relay is anonymous by design, so as with `/gfs/publish` the client address is the only shedding handle. It was 30/min, which was wrong by an order of magnitude: this relay is not a sideband for the redeem handshake, it is the **only** transport a link-joined household has, so *every* federation envelope to that peer rides it — each space post, reaction, calendar event and sync chunk — and a single space catch-up backfill is hundreds of chunks. 600/min still caps what one household can push through a relay it does not own, and the durable cost stays bounded per household by the 2000-row / 64 MiB queue cap (tail-drop at either ceiling). Bodies over 320 KiB are refused with 413 before being read. A 429 is a **cooldown**, not a verdict: the sending household backs off and retries rather than failing the envelope. |
| Federation inbound (per signing instance) | Rolling window; see §24.11. |
| `POST /federation/inbox/{inbox_id}` (per remote IP) | 1000 / min — defends against unauthenticated floods that the per-user limiter would otherwise miss. The 429 carries `Retry-After` (whole seconds until the sliding window frees a slot); a sending household's outbox waits at least that long and retries — a 429 is never a drop. |

**GFS clusters.** The GFS's per-IP windows are counted in each node's
memory. The three anonymous public ones marked *cluster-wide* above are
split over the live nodes: each node allows `ceil(limit / live nodes)`
(`public.node_share`; live = this node + the member peers that answered
the last heartbeat round), so behind a round-robin balancer one address
gets about the listed budget in all instead of one per node (4 nodes: 8,
5 and 8 per node). A balancer that pins a client to one node makes it
stricter, never looser. They are not shared through the database: that
would put a write into the one SQLite file the nodes share on every
anonymous request. The other GFS limits above stay per node — they gate
signed household traffic, where the budget is far above real use.

Rate-limit responses return HTTP 429 with a `Retry-After` header.

**Signed media URLs.** Browser-loaded media (`<img>`, `<video>`) carries no
bearer header, so every `picture_url` / `media_url` / `cover_url` /
`icon_url` / `thumbnail_url` / `image_urls` a response returns is signed as
`?exp=<unix>&sig=<hmac>` (HMAC-SHA256 over path and `exp`, key derived from
the instance identity). The default expiry is not `now + 1 h` but the first
UTC six-hour boundary (00/06/12/18) at least one hour away, so every response
inside that window hands out the **same** URL for a resource and the
browser's HTTP cache (`Cache-Control: private, max-age=…`) serves a revisited
avatar instead of refetching it. A leaked URL therefore opens one resource path
for between 1 and 7 hours (it was exactly 1 h) — for a picture, cover or icon
that includes a newer image uploaded at the same path, since `?v=` sits outside
the signature. Bytes a browser already fetched were cacheable for a day or
more anyway, so the signature lifetime was never a revocation guarantee. Signatures minted with an explicit
lifetime — app bundle entry (5 min), map tile template (7 days) — expire at
exactly that lifetime.

**Which IP a GFS limiter counts** — and the same answer for the admin-login
throttle, the `admin_ip` written to the admin audit log, and every route view's
`client_ip()`, which all resolve through the one shared resolver.
`X-Forwarded-For` is client-supplied, so the
GFS believes it only when the TCP peer is itself a trusted proxy, and then uses
the **last** entry (the hop that proxy appended); otherwise the peer address is
used. The trusted set is `[server] trusted_proxies` in `global_server.toml`
(env: `GFS_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, comma-separated IPs/CIDRs, empty string to clear).
It defaults to loopback + the RFC1918 ranges + `fc00::/7`, so the usual
"reverse proxy on the same host or private network" deployment keeps per-client
limiting with no configuration, while a peer connecting straight from the
internet can never spoof its own client IP. IPv4-mapped IPv6 peers and header
entries (`::ffff:1.2.3.4`) are unmapped to their IPv4 form before both the
trusted-peer test and the bucket key, so a dual-stack listener still recognises
a loopback/RFC1918 proxy and one host cannot claim two buckets by switching
address family. An IPv6 zone (`2001:db8::1%eth0`) is stripped from both
the peer and the header entry before keying, so one host cannot mint a fresh
bucket per zone spelling — nor inflate the bucket key with an arbitrarily long
zone string. **An IPv6 client is counted by its /64**, not its full address.
One subscriber is routinely given a whole /64 and could otherwise rotate
through its addresses for a fresh bucket each time. IPv4 is counted per
address. Every per-client limiter and throttle uses this key: the windows
above, the admin-login lockout, the pairing-token interval, the
channel / member-publish per-address windows and the `/cluster/sync`
source-address budget. The household's unauthenticated per-IP limits use the
same key: `/api/auth/token`, password reset, `/federation/inbox/*` and
`/api/invite-links/{token}/code` (`socialhome/rate_limiter.py:client_bucket`).
The admin audit log keeps the exact address. Each limiter tracks at most
10 000 buckets, evicting the least recently seen.

Three limits of this scheme, stated so operators can design around them:

- **The front end must OVERWRITE `X-Forwarded-For`.** Trust here means "this
  peer's last entry is authoritative". An L4/TCP proxy — or an L7 proxy set to
  *append* rather than *set* — passes the client's own header through, so the
  last entry is attacker-chosen again and one source can mint unlimited
  buckets. Behind such a front end the only safe setting is
  `trusted_proxies = []` (every request then keys on the proxy's address, i.e.
  one shared bucket).
- **Single hop.** Only the last entry is read, so a chain of two or more
  trusted proxies resolves to the address the *innermost* proxy appended — the
  outer proxy, not the client — and every client behind the chain shares one
  bucket. Collapse the chain at the outermost proxy before the GFS sees it.
- **Not a defence against a distributed flood.** The counters are per-source
  and LRU-bounded at 10 000 addresses; an attacker with at least that many
  source addresses evicts every bucket each window and is never limited. The
  per-IP windows shed a single noisy source, they do not shed a botnet —
  volumetric defence belongs at the network edge.

## Version & compatibility

API responses include `X-Social-Home-Version` when running in
standalone mode (derived from `pyproject.toml`). Breaking changes
bump the major version. Endpoints added in minor versions are
announced in `CHANGELOG.md`.
